# Upgrading from v0.9.0 to v0.10.0

> **Scope note:** this file is the root Markdown deliverable required by `.project/v0.10.0/00-program-overview.md`
> §9. It is a **living document**, created in the first epic of the v0.10.0 program (Phase 22,
> `ENG-08`) and appended to by every later epic that touched a section below. At the v0.10.0 close
> (Phase 29, `SHIP-01`), every section is substantive: each section that once carried a marker
> naming the requirement or phase responsible for closing it now carries the content that
> requirement produced — a citation of the test that proves the claim, a confirmed-empty
> statement, or a completed checklist. No open marker remains, and this note is written so that no
> future edit reintroduces one without also filling it. The companion mdBook "Upgrading" page
> overview §5.4's Definition of Done also requires is added separately, by this same closing phase
> (Phase 29, plan 29-06), and links back to this file.

> **2026-09-20 addendum:** this title and every section below it describe the v0.10.0
> **milestone's feature scope**, which a patch release does not change, so the title is not
> rewritten here. The version that actually shipped through the release pipeline is **v0.10.1** —
> the `v0.10.0` tag published only 3 of 12 crates before stopping at a publish-order defect;
> `v0.10.1` republishes the full set at one coherent version with that defect and one other fixed.
> See `CHANGELOG.md`'s `[0.10.1]` section for what changed. Nothing in this migration guide's
> technical content is affected — the patch ships no library source change and no API change.

## 9.1 Behavioral changes (user-visible without code changes)

| ID | Change | Who is affected | Required user action |
|---|---|---|---|
| M-B-01 | **BUG-01 fix.** `EdgeCondition::Custom(name)` no longer evaluates to `true` when no evaluator is registered. Campaign/graph validation now fails with `BattalionError::InvalidGraph` naming each unregistered condition, before any node executes. | Any v0.9 workflow using `EdgeCondition::Custom`. Such workflows were routing *incorrectly* (always following the custom edge); after upgrade they fail loudly at validation. | Register an evaluator for each name via the new registry API (CF-FR-01), or replace the condition with `Contains`/`Regex`/`Always`. |
| M-B-02 | **Graceful shutdown.** On SIGTERM/SIGINT the process now waits up to `shutdown_grace` (default 30 s, env `APP_ENGINE_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_SECS`) for in-flight engine runs to halt before exiting — `paladin-server`'s `shutdown_signal` and `ServiceRunner::wait_for_shutdown` both cancel a shared `ShutdownCoordinator` and wait for registered runs to drain (HITL-04, D-21/D-22). | Operators; container orchestration. | Set `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` ≥ 2 × `shutdown_grace` — `k8s/server/deployment.yaml` and `k8s/deployment.yaml` ship `60` (landed, HITL-FR-15). Set `APP_ENGINE_GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN=false` to restore the old no-wait behavior for legacy-only deployments (landed, HITL-04). |
| M-B-03 | **No behavioral change.** `tool_error_mode` (RT-FR-24) NAMES a policy the v0.9 loop already implemented, rather than introducing one: a failed Arsenal or handoff tool call was always appended to the accumulated output and the run continued (`paladin_execution_service.rs`'s Arsenal and handoff error-handling arms, unchanged since before this phase). This phase adds `FailRun` as a new opt-in (`PaladinError::ArmamentFailed { tool, reason }`) and sanitizes the fed-back text (redact-then-bound, D-34) before the model ever sees it — the sanitization is the ONLY observable difference for an existing user. | Users of the Arsenal/handoff tool loop. None from the policy itself; a leaked credential previously visible in a tool's raw error text is now redacted before the model sees it. | None required to keep today's behavior — `tool_error_mode` defaults to `FeedToModel`, byte-for-byte matching v0.9 apart from sanitization. Set `tool_error_mode = FailRun` (globally via `AgentRuntimeConfig.tool_errors.mode`, or per tool via `tool_errors.per_tool`) to opt into failing the run on a tool error instead. |
| M-B-04 | **Automatic per-superstep checkpointing.** Any graph executed through the new `WarEngine` (via `WarGraph`/`WarEngine::start`/`WarEngine::resume`) now writes one `Waypoint` — a **full `Battlefield` snapshot**, including whatever a workflow places in shared state (which may include raw LLM prompts and model outputs) — after every superstep, by default (`WaypointDurability::Strict`). The write goes to whichever `WaypointPort` backend the caller wires in: `InMemoryWaypointStore` (ungated, tests/dev) or, once landed, `SqliteWaypointStore`/`PostgresWaypointStore` (ENG-05) for durable deployments. Growth is bounded by `EngineLimits` (`max_supersteps` default 50, `max_node_visits` default 25) and, once configured, by `WaypointRetentionConfig` (`max_age_days`, `max_waypoints_per_thread` — see §9.5). | Any workflow author who adopts the new `WarEngine`/`WarGraph` APIs (ENG-01…ENG-08). | **Legacy `FormationExecutionService`, `PhalanxExecutionService`, `CampaignExecutionService`, and `Commander` execution paths are completely unaffected — they write no Waypoints and their behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged (ENG-FR-20).** A v0.9 workflow gains **no new persistence** unless it is explicitly rebuilt against the new engine. To adopt it: choose a `WaypointPort` backend, review `WaypointDurability` (default `Strict` fails the run on a write error; `BestEffort` downgrades to a logged warning), and configure `WaypointRetentionConfig` if snapshots — which may contain raw prompts/outputs — must not accumulate unbounded. |
| M-B-05 | **Rate pacing is on by default (PACE-02, Phase 43 plan 43-01).** Every agent the server builds from configuration or provisions at runtime now waits out a per-(provider, model) gate after a provider 429: later calls to that provider and model are held for an exponential back-off with full jitter, from 500 ms up to 30 s (a `Retry-After` header is honoured from plan 43-02), instead of being sent straight back into the rate limit. `treasurer.cadence.enabled: false` restores the previous behaviour exactly. The OpenAI adapter also now surfaces a 429 on its first attempt instead of retrying it inside its own loop (D-02), so the pacing layer sees every 429 and each one costs the provider exactly one hit; from plan 43-03 the Anthropic adapter does the same (its network and 5xx retries are unchanged), and from plan 43-04 so do the compat-engine presets (Kimi, Qwen, Grok, Ollama and the OpenAI-compatible adapter), DeepSeek and Gemini, each of which used to retry a 429 inside a private loop; those three now read `Retry-After`, `retry-after-ms` and the `x-ratelimit-*` names into the error too. A shared conformance case now proves, for every adapter fixture, that a 429 reaches the provider exactly once. The OpenAI and Anthropic adapters read the provider's own retry delay and rate-limit headers (`Retry-After`, `retry-after-ms`, OpenAI `x-ratelimit-*`, Anthropic `anthropic-ratelimit-*`) into the error (`LlmError::retry_after()`, `LlmError::rate_limit_hints()`). An account-level quota or spend-cap 429 (OpenAI `error.code`/`error.type` `insufficient_quota`, Anthropic `error.details.error_code` `enforced_spend_limit_reached`) is now reported as `LlmError::UsageLimitExceeded` (permanent: not retried, not paced) instead of `LlmError::RateLimitExceeded`. From plan 43-05 streaming calls (`generate_stream`) are paced exactly like buffered ones: the call waits on the gate before it starts, and a 429 (as the call's own error or as the stream's first item) opens the gate while the stream's items are still delivered unchanged; and callers released by one gate are spread by up to `min(wait / 10, 1 s)` so they do not all fire in the same instant (the spread is only ever added, never below a provider's explicit delay). From plan 43-06 a fallback chain is paced first and hops last: a chain built with `FallbackLlmAdapter::with_cadence`, `ModelFallbackMiddleware::paced` or `AgentRuntimeConfig::build_chain` (which is paced by default) now retries the same provider after its gate on a 429, for up to `treasurer.cadence.fallback_pace_budget_secs` (default 60), before it hops to the next provider; 5xx responses, timeouts and network errors still hop at once, and a chain built with `FallbackLlmAdapter::new` and no `with_cadence` still hops on a 429 immediately. From plan 43-08, when a shared pacing backend (Redis) is configured and becomes unreachable, pacing degrades instead of failing: calls keep being paced by per-process gates whose delays are multiplied by `degraded_multiplier` (default 2.0, so a delay-less first 429 gates for twice the base back-off and a provider's own minimum is only ever raised), exactly one `warn` record is emitted under the `paladin::cadence` log target per outage and one `info` record on recovery, and the shared backend is retried at most once every 5 seconds and used again automatically once it answers; a gate recorded locally during the outage is still honoured after recovery. No LLM call fails because the pacing backend is down. From plan 43-09 the run engine's own port (the one every `POST /v1/runs` run and every `WarGraph` node calls through) is paced as well, and `paladin-server` builds ONE wiring at boot that the resident agents, the runtime provisioner and the run engine all share, so a 429 seen through any of them delays the next call to the same provider and model through the others; `treasurer.cadence.backend: { redis: { url_env: NAME } }` shares that state across a fleet (needs a binary built with `redis-cadence`; one without it refuses to start naming the feature rather than pacing in-process). From plan 43-11 a `WarEngine` built with both `with_node_cache` and the new `with_cadence(port, lock_ttl)` executes a cache-policy node once across concurrent identical cache misses (two workers, or two threads of one worker): the dispatch that takes the Cadence stampede lock executes and stores its result, and the others wait up to `lock_ttl` (polling about every 100 ms) and serve the stored delta as a cache hit; a lock error, an elapsed `lock_ttl` or a cancelled run never fails a node. Engines built without `with_cadence`, and nodes without a cache policy, are unchanged, and `paladin-server` attaches no node cache and so no lock. Later Phase 43 plans extend this row. | Every deployment that calls an LLM provider. A workload that previously rode out brief 429 bursts by immediate retry may now see those calls wait or surface a rate-limit error after `max_wait_secs`. Code that matched `LlmError::RateLimitExceeded` to detect an exhausted OpenAI quota or Anthropic spend cap now receives `LlmError::UsageLimitExceeded` for it. | None required. To opt out, set `treasurer.cadence.enabled: false`; to tune, adjust the `treasurer.cadence` keys (section 9.5). |
| M-B-06 | **Battalion timeouts bound each attempt, not the run (LEGACY-02, Phase 44 plans 44-01/44-04/44-05/44-07, D-02/D-03).** Formation, Phalanx, Campaign and the Conclave bound every Paladin, expert or aggregator attempt by `BattalionConfig.aegis.timeout` (the tighter of `run_timeout` and `idle_timeout`; `idle_timeout` degrades to a wall clock because `PaladinPort::execute` reports no progress). There is no whole-Battalion clock any more, so a ten-step Formation may run ten times the bound. The Commander adds none either: ChainOfCommand and Grove are unbounded unless the caller wraps `Commander::execute()` in `tokio::time::timeout`, while Council keeps its own per-speaker 300 s. `BattalionConfig::new` arms no timeout where v0.10 defaulted to 300 s per run; `CommanderBuilder`'s default, when no `aegis` is set, is 300 s per attempt; the CLI's 600 s default and its `timeout_seconds` setting (YAML key unchanged) now apply per attempt, not per run; and the Commander's default Maneuver flow timeout is `aegis.timeout.run_timeout` (none when unset). A timed-out attempt surfaces per pattern: Formation and Campaign return `BattalionError::Node` with a `Timeout` source, Phalanx records it inside `AggregationError`, a Conclave expert sees a retried `PaladinError::Timeout`, and the Conclave aggregator (a single attempt) returns `ConclaveError::AggregatorFailed`. The v0.10 `BattalionError::Timeout`, `ConclaveError::Timeout`, `BattalionConfig.timeout_seconds` / `with_timeout` and `ConclaveConfig.timeout_seconds` / `with_timeout` are removed (section 9.2). | Anyone who relied on a Battalion run ending after 300 s (or the CLI's 600 s) in total; operators of ChainOfCommand or Grove Battalions, which now have no clock at all. | Set `BattalionConfig::with_aegis(Aegis { timeout: Some(TimeoutPolicy { run_timeout: Some(..), idle_timeout: None }), .. })` to the per-attempt figure you want, and size it as the longest single Paladin call rather than the whole run. For a whole-run bound wrap `Commander::execute()` (or the service call) in `tokio::time::timeout`. |
| M-B-07 | **Battalion retry follows the Aegis policy (LEGACY-01, Phase 44 plans 44-01/44-04/44-05, D-01/D-05).** `aegis.retry.max_attempts` counts total attempts, so a v0.10 policy of N retries is `max_attempts: N + 1`. The default `RetryPredicate::TransientOnly` does not retry `Unknown` failures such as `ExecutionError` (v0.10 retried every error); set `RetryPredicate::TransientAndUnknown` to keep the retry-everything behaviour. Permanent failures are never retried. Phalanx and Campaign now retry under `aegis.retry` (v0.10's Phalanx retry arm only logged and Campaign had none). The wait between attempts is the engine's `backoff_delay` (default 500 ms doubling to a 60 s cap, jitter adding up to one base delay). Campaign ignores `aegis.on_error` (it has no continue mode) and logs a warning. The v0.10 legacy `RetryPolicy`, `ErrorStrategy` and the `retry` module are removed (section 9.2). | Any Formation, Phalanx or Campaign that set `with_retry_policy` or `with_error_strategy`, or relied on the old retry-everything default. | Replace `with_retry_policy` with `with_aegis(Aegis { retry: Some(RetryPolicy { max_attempts: N + 1, retry_on: RetryPredicate::TransientAndUnknown, .. }), .. })` (omit `retry_on` to take the safer `TransientOnly`), and `with_error_strategy` with `on_error: Some(ErrorHandlerSpec::Absorb { .. })` (continue past a failed Paladin) or `None` (fail fast). |
| M-B-08 | **`node_errors` carries the structured `NodeError` (LEGACY-01, Phase 44 plan 44-06, D-06/D-08).** The `BattalionResult.node_errors` element changes from the v0.10 `{node_name, error}` summary to `{node_id, attempt, transience, source}`: `node_id` is the Paladin's name, `attempt` the 1-indexed attempt that produced the final failure, `transience` one of `Transient`, `Permanent`, `Unknown`, and `source` a tagged object (`{"Llm": {..}}`, `{"Paladin": {..}}` or `{"Timeout": "Run"}` / `{"Timeout": "Idle"}`). The JSON herald emits that shape. The Markdown herald line becomes `**<node id>:** attempt <n>, <Transience>: <source>` and the Table herald line `  <node id> (attempt <n>, <Transience>): <source>`; neither adds the provider or HTTP status. A Battalion that continued past failures still reports `Completed`. | Consumers of `BattalionResult.node_errors` (Rust code reading `.node_name` / `.error`, JSON consumers of the herald output, anything parsing the Markdown or Table lines). Stored documents: see section 9.4. | Read `node_id` where you read `node_name`, and render `source` (it implements `Display`) where you read `error`; update any parser of the herald lines to the new format. |
| M-B-09 | **The Conclave retries by `transience()` with the engine backoff (LEGACY-03, Phase 44 plans 44-03/44-07, D-10/D-11/D-12).** The retry decision reads `PaladinError::transience()` alone and never message text: only `Transient` failures are retried, so `Unknown` (for example `ExecutionError`, an `ArmamentFailed`) is not. Newly retried, because they classify as `Transient`: `CircuitBreakerOpen`, `GarrisonError` Storage and Tokenization, and `ArsenalError` Timeout and TransportError. A `Permanent` failure whose message happens to read like a rate limit is no longer retried, and a `Transient` one with an empty message now is. `Timeout`, a `Transient` `LlmFailure` and the Permanent group (for example `ConfigurationError`, `GuardrailTripped`) keep their v0.10 answers. The backoff changes from 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 s with plus-or-minus 20 percent jitter to `aegis.retry`'s shape (default 500 ms doubling to a 60 s cap, jitter adding up to one base delay); `ConclaveConfig.retry_attempts` stays the retry count. The Commander bridges `aegis.retry` into `ConclaveConfig.retry_attempts = max_attempts - 1` (clamped to 5), because `max_attempts` counts total attempts while `retry_attempts` counts retries after the first, so `max_attempts: 3` gives each expert three attempts; with `aegis.retry` unset the default of 2 retries stands (D-04 as refined by ADR-0059 (e), plan 44-07). | Conclave users whose experts fail with the newly retried variants or rely on the exact v0.10 delay sequence; Commander users who set a retry policy on a Conclave. | None for the predicate. To keep a v0.10 retry count through the Commander set `max_attempts` to the old `retry_attempts + 1`; to keep the delays, there is no setting, the engine shape applies (tune `initial_interval`, `backoff_factor` and `max_interval` on `aegis.retry`). |
| M-B-10 | **The circuit breaker counts Transient failures only (LEGACY-03, Phase 44 plan 44-03, D-14).** `CircuitBreaker::call` and `call_async` count a failure only when `PaladinError::transience()` is `Transient`. A `Permanent` failure (for example a 401 from a bad API key) and an `Unknown` one (for example `ProcessingError`, `ExecutionError`) no longer trip it; `Timeout`, `CircuitBreakerOpen`, `GarrisonError` Storage and Tokenization and `ArsenalError` Timeout and TransportError now do. In production the breaker wraps only `generate()` and so sees only `LlmFailure` (research Finding 6): the practical change is that a Permanent or Unknown LLM failure no longer opens it, so a revoked key now fails fast at the provider on every call rather than being cut off by the breaker. | Operators relying on the breaker to stop calling a provider after repeated authentication or malformed-request errors; embedders calling `CircuitBreaker` directly with non-LLM errors. | Alert on repeated `Permanent` provider failures instead of relying on the breaker to cut them off; none otherwise. |
| M-B-11 | **The Commander's default Maneuver strategy is fail-fast (LEGACY-01, Phase 44 plan 44-07, D-04).** v0.10 mapped the continue-on-error and retry-then-continue strategies to `ContinueParallel`; a Commander now runs a Maneuver with `maneuver::ErrorStrategy::FailFast` unless `CommanderBuilder::error_strategy` sets one. The Maneuver's own `ErrorStrategy` is unchanged (only the v0.10 `battalion::ErrorStrategy` is removed). | Commander users who ran a Maneuver under the v0.10 continue-on-error or retry-then-continue strategies and relied on the siblings finishing after one branch failed. | Call `CommanderBuilder::error_strategy(maneuver::ErrorStrategy::ContinueParallel)` (or the strategy you want) explicitly. |
| M-B-12 | **Persisted entity JSON uses the new keys (ENTITY-01, Phase 48 plans 48-03/48-04/48-05, D-03/D-08/D-09/D-10/D-11).** v0.11 reads both the v0.10 keys and the new ones, and writes only the new keys: the payload of every entity is `data` (v0.10 wrote `node`), a `ContentItem` or `PromptItem` holds its entity under `entity` (v0.10 wrote `node`), and a version record carries `entity_id` and `entity_data` (v0.10 wrote `node_id` and `node_data`). A document that carries both spellings of one key is rejected, never merged. The result is one-way: a v0.10 binary cannot read JSON written by v0.11, and it cannot read a Citadel snapshot whose Paladin status is `Reasoning`, because the Citadel now stores the real `PaladinStatus`, which has that variant the v0.10 placeholder lacked. The v0.10 key spellings stay readable for the whole 0.x line; removing them needs its own ADR. They are data compatibility for stored documents, not an API compatibility path: the Rust names changed outright (section 9.2, Phase 48 rows). | Anyone who stores or exchanges Paladin, Council, Grove, User, Log, `ContentItem`, `PromptItem`, version-record or Citadel `PaladinState` JSON: Citadel snapshot files, caller-owned stores and queue payloads. The built-in SQL content and user tables are not affected (section 9.4). | Back up that state before upgrading. Upgrade every reader (and every queue consumer) before any writer, so no v0.10 process meets a document with the new keys. To downgrade, restore the pre-upgrade backup; v0.10 cannot read what v0.11 wrote. |
| M-B-13 | **Every adapter follows the per-model parameter policy (PARITY-02, Phase 50 plans 50-03 and 50-05, ADR-0061).** An explicit temperature or stop words that a model's profile does not admit now fail the build, naming the provider, the model and the parameter. `auto_temperature` is no longer refused on a narrow provider: the value is omitted with a `warn!` and recorded in `paladin.params_omitted`, so a Kimi swap with the default `auto_temperature` builds. Kimi sends no `temperature`, `top_p` or penalty on any model, seeded or not: an explicit value is refused at build, naming `kimi`, the model and the parameter, and a framework default is omitted and recorded in `paladin.params_omitted`, never swapped for the vendor's legal value (which depends on the thinking mode). An `openai-compatible` endpoint that declares no temperature range is now held to ADR-0004's default `(0.0, 1.0)` at the wire as well as at build, so a direct call with a temperature above `1.0` is omitted and recorded where it used to be sent. The compat presets now send stop sequences in the order declared, except Grok, which does not take them (an agent with stop words is refused at build; a direct call's `stop` is omitted and recorded). Unprofiled models are reported through `paladin.unprofiled_model`: until the per-model tables land, every model on all nine providers is unprofiled and runs on its adapter-wide fallback, so every buffered response carries the key. A `response_format` a model does not support is downgraded or omitted and recorded in `paladin.response_format_adjusted` (a JSON-schema request on a compat preset or DeepSeek is still sent as a plain JSON object, now recorded as `json_object`; Anthropic has no JSON mode and a requested format is recorded as `omitted`). A `max_tokens` above a model's profiled maximum output is refused by name before anything is sent, never clamped. Streamed calls warn only, with no response metadata, pending owner reading R-1 (plan 50-18). **OpenAI** no longer puts `temperature` 0.7 or `top_p` 1.0 on the wire when the caller set none: an unset parameter is absent from the body and the vendor default applies (an agent run through `PaladinExecutionService` still sends the agent's `temperature` and its stop words, and leaves `top_p` unset, so the `1.0` the adapter used to add there is gone, which is the vendor default anyway; a prompt built with `PromptParameters::default()` carries its own `0.7` and `1.0` and still sends them; only a direct `LlmRequest` that left them unset changes); OpenAI's `max_tokens` default stays 4096. **Anthropic**: an explicit temperature on an Anthropic agent is now refused at build, naming `anthropic`, the model and `temperature` (the adapter has never sent it and used to validate the value and then silently drop it; a direct call's `temperature` or `top_p` is now omitted and recorded instead). Stop sequences are now sent by OpenAI (`stop`, at most 4: a longer list is omitted whole and recorded, never truncated), DeepSeek (`stop`) and Anthropic (`stop_sequences`); Gemini already sent `generationConfig.stopSequences`. OpenAI, Anthropic and Gemini have never carried penalties, so a prompt built with `PromptParameters::default()` (which sets both penalties to `0.0`) now lists them in `paladin.params_omitted` on buffered responses, with one `warn!` per model; nothing on their wires changed, and a request that sets no penalty (every agent run through `PaladinExecutionService`) records nothing. | Agents on these providers that set an explicit temperature or stop words; direct `LlmRequest` callers on OpenAI that relied on the adapter's `0.7` / `1.0`; anything that reads `LlmResponse.metadata` or treats a `warn!` as an alert. | Drop `temperature` from Anthropic agents (set it only on a model whose profile admits it). Expect stop words to take effect on OpenAI, DeepSeek, Anthropic and the compat presets other than Grok, and keep an OpenAI agent's stop words to four or fewer. Set `temperature` explicitly on a direct OpenAI `LlmRequest` if you relied on `0.7`. Declare a model's real limits with a `models.<id>` override once the per-model configuration lands (plans 50-08 and 50-09). |
| M-B-14 | **Default models, model lists and windows now come from a dated registry (PARITY-04, Phase 50 plan 50-06, D-05/D-08).** Every default below was identified by the operator's live probe of 2026-10-15 (verified-on 2026-10-15) and is seeded in a dated per-vendor table in `paladin-llm`; a model with no table entry keeps working, logs one warning per model and runs on the adapter's own wide values. Default models, old -> new, when no model is configured: Anthropic `claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022` -> `claude-haiku-5-5` (the old id now answers 404), DeepSeek `deepseek-chat` -> `deepseek-flash`, Kimi `kimi-k3` -> `kimi-k2.6`, Qwen `qwen-plus` -> `qwen3.8-flash`, Grok `grok-4.6` -> `grok-4.3`, Gemini `gemini-3.6-flash` -> `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`; the previous model stays available and, except for Anthropic's retired ids and DeepSeek's two aliases, seeded. Windows reported by `get_capabilities().max_context_tokens`: Anthropic 200,000 -> 1,000,000, DeepSeek 64,000 -> 1,048,576, Kimi 131,072 -> 262,144, Qwen 131,072 -> 1,000,000, Grok 131,072 -> 1,000,000 (Gemini stays 1,048,576). Model lists: `AnthropicAdapter::get_available_models` and `validate_model` now serve the one probed Anthropic id (`claude-haiku-5-5`) instead of eight retired claude-3/claude-2 ids, and `DeepSeekAdapter::get_available_models` serves `deepseek-flash` instead of `deepseek-chat` and `deepseek-coder` (undocumented aliases that still answer 200). Kimi: the two seeded models (`kimi-k2.6`, `kimi-k3`) admit no explicit `temperature`, `top_p` or penalty (the vendor rejected every probed value), so an agent that sets one is refused at build; unseeded Kimi models follow the same sampling declarations as the seeded ones (against v0.10.1 the visible change for an unseeded id is that the penalties are no longer sent). Not probed, so unchanged: Ollama (default `llama3`, no table, no window) and OpenAI-compatible (no table); OpenAI has no adapter default model and keeps its 128,000 window, with `gpt-5.4-nano` and `gpt-4o-mini` seeded. Who: deployments that rely on an unconfigured default model or on the reported window. Action: pin the model explicitly (`ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, `DEEPSEEK_MODEL`, `MOONSHOT_MODEL`, `DASHSCOPE_MODEL`, `XAI_MODEL`, `GEMINI_MODEL`) to keep the old one. The `config.yml` and bridge defaults follow in plans 50-09 and 50-10. |
| M-B-15 | **`llm.<provider>` blocks now take effect (PARITY-03, Phase 50 plans 50-09/50-10).** Before this phase the `llm:` section of a configuration file was parsed and ignored by the server: every agent was built from environment variables alone. Now `paladin-server` builds every config-defined agent, the run engine and every runtime-provisioned agent through `LlmProviderFactory::create_from_config`, so a populated block decides how that provider is constructed. Per field, the most specific statement wins: the agent's own `model` (and `provider`), then the block, then the provider's legacy environment variable, then the adapter's built-in default; an environment variable never overrides a value written in the block. What changes for a deployment that already has a block: `base_url` and the key now decide where requests (and the credential) go; `default_model` is used when no model is named; `timeout_seconds` and `max_retries` now configure the adapter; **`default_temperature` is now an explicit value passed to every agent of that provider that sets no `temperature` of its own, and a model that does not accept it refuses the agent at startup** (the refusal names the provider, the model, `temperature` and the key, `llm.<provider>.default_temperature` or `agents[<id>].temperature`), for example the old example's `0.7` on `claude-haiku-5-5`, which takes only `1.0`; an agent's `provider` and `model` are honoured through the same path. `api_key` is optional (an empty or absent key falls back to the provider's own variable), and an exact `"${VAR}"` is read from the variable `VAR`: an unset variable is a startup error naming it, never sent as the key. A provider configured only in YAML now passes the startup availability check, `LlmConfig::validate` no longer requires the default provider's block to exist, and the check that fails for a provider with no credential names the agent, the provider, the model and the variable. Reasoning models default to a 300 s HTTP timeout when neither block nor environment sets one. A runtime `POST /agents` caller still supplies a model and nothing else: provider blocks are operator-only. **Who is affected:** anyone with a populated `llm:` block. **Action:** review every block (especially `base_url`, `default_temperature` and `${VAR}` keys) before upgrading; delete a `default_temperature` the model does not take. The agent's per-agent `timeout_seconds` is unchanged: it is the execution timeout, never the provider's HTTP timeout. |
| M-B-16 | **Empty output and refusals are typed (PARITY-05, Phase 50 plan 50-12, D-09/D-11a).** Empty text on any finish reason is now `LlmError::EmptyCompletion` on the five OpenAI-compatible presets (Kimi, Qwen, Grok, Ollama, the generic preset), OpenAI and DeepSeek, buffered and streamed. It used to be `Ok("")` unless the finish reason was `length`; OpenAI never checked; streams never checked, so a stream with no text ended in an `Ok` terminal chunk and now ends in `Err(EmptyCompletion)` as its last item (deltas already yielded, reasoning included, stay yielded). A `content_filter` finish and a function-call finish with no text stay `Ok`; whitespace-only text counts as empty. A vendor refusal is `LlmError::Refusal { provider, model, detail }` (permanent, never retried; `detail` is the vendor text with credentials removed first and then bounded to 512 characters) and wins over partial text: OpenAI refusals (`content: null` plus a `refusal` string) used to be parse errors, and the compat presets used to return the empty text. `EmptyCompletion`'s `Display` is now `Empty completion: {payload}`; it no longer appends max-tokens advice to every message, and each payload names its finish reason and carries the advice that fits it (a larger `max_tokens` for `length`, "the model returned no text" for `stop`). OpenAI `finish_reason: "tool_calls"` now maps to `FinishReason::FunctionCall` (it was `Error("Unknown: tool_calls")`). Reasoning text is reported on `LlmResponse.reasoning` and each streamed `StreamingResponse.reasoning` for the compat presets and DeepSeek, and the compat presets map `prompt_tokens_details.cache_write_tokens` to `TokenUsage.cache_write_tokens`. **Who is affected:** callers that treat `Ok` with empty `content` as an answer, that match on the old `EmptyCompletion` message text, that match `LlmError` exhaustively without a wildcard arm (the enum is `#[non_exhaustive]`, so a wildcard is already required), or that read an OpenAI `FinishReason::Error("Unknown: tool_calls")`. **Action:** handle `LlmError::EmptyCompletion` and `LlmError::Refusal` where an empty or refused answer matters; treat `Refusal` as a policy outcome to surface or to route to another provider deliberately, not as a retryable fault; read the finish reason and advice from the payload rather than from a fixed suffix. |
| M-B-17 | **Anthropic refusals, stop reasons and thinking text; Gemini empty output (PARITY-05, Phase 50 plan 50-13, D-09/D-11/D-11a).** An Anthropic refusal (`stop_reason: "refusal"`, HTTP 200 with `content: []`) is now `LlmError::Refusal { provider: "anthropic", model, detail }`, buffered and streamed; it used to be `LlmError::EmptyCompletion` with max-tokens advice. `detail` is built from `stop_details.category` and `stop_details.explanation` (either may be null; both null reads `stop_reason=refusal`), with credentials removed first and then bounded to 512 characters, and a refusal wins over partial text. On a stream the refusal ends the stream as its last item; text already yielded stays yielded. Every Anthropic stop reason has a name: `end_turn` and `stop_sequence` are `Stop`, `max_tokens` and `model_context_window_exceeded` are `Length`, `tool_use` is `FinishReason::FunctionCall`, `pause_turn` is `Error("pause_turn")`, and any other value is `Error("unknown: <value>")` (every one of these but the first three used to be `Error("unknown")`). A streamed Anthropic response reports its real stop reason on the terminal chunk; it used to report `Stop` unconditionally, so a truncated stream now ends with `Length`. The text of `thinking` blocks and `thinking_delta` events is reported on `LlmResponse.reasoning` and each streamed `StreamingResponse.reasoning`. An Anthropic or Gemini stream that reaches its end with no text ends in `Err(EmptyCompletion)` as its last item (thinking or reasoning chunks already yielded stay yielded). Gemini `STOP` with empty text is now `EmptyCompletion` on both paths (it was `Ok("")`; `MAX_TOKENS` already was); Gemini `RECITATION`, `BLOCKLIST`, `PROHIBITED_CONTENT` and `SPII` now map to `FinishReason::ContentFilter` like `SAFETY` (`RECITATION` used to be `Error("RECITATION")`; the others used to be `Error(<value>)`) and stay `Ok` with no text. An Anthropic `EmptyCompletion` message now reads like every other adapter's (`finish_reason=... with empty content ...`) followed by the content-block census. **Usage caps:** no compat preset gains a usage-cap mapping in this plan, because no quota-exhaustion body was captured for any of them. Kimi, Qwen, Grok, Ollama and the generic OpenAI-compatible preset keep today's status-based error mapping, and the owner accepted each as unverified at plan 50-17 Task 1 (2026-10-16, owner reading R-2); Anthropic and DeepSeek keep their existing quota mappings. A quota error from one of those five presets is therefore not `UsageLimitExceeded`. **Who is affected:** callers that matched an Anthropic empty-content error to detect a refusal, that read `FinishReason::Error("unknown")` for Anthropic `tool_use`, `pause_turn` or context-window stops, that read `FinishReason::Stop` from an Anthropic stream's terminal chunk, that treat Gemini `Ok` with empty `content` on `STOP` as an answer, or that match Gemini `FinishReason::Error("RECITATION")`. **Action:** handle `LlmError::Refusal` as a policy outcome to surface or to route to another provider deliberately (it is permanent, never retried); match `FinishReason::FunctionCall` and `Length` for the newly named stop reasons; read `ContentFilter` for every Gemini blocked-content finish; handle `EmptyCompletion` on Anthropic and Gemini streams the same way as on the other providers. |
| M-B-18 | **The OpenAI adapter sends its output cap as `max_completion_tokens` (G-50-5b, Phase 50 plan 50-19).** The wire key of the output-token limit changed from `max_tokens` to `max_completion_tokens` for every model the OpenAI adapter serves, on the chat path (buffered, streamed, every `response_format`) and the vision path; `gpt-5.4-nano` rejected the old key with HTTP 400. `OpenAIConfig.max_tokens`, `OpenAIConfig::with_max_tokens` and `PromptParameters.max_tokens` keep their names and meaning, a limit above the profiled maximum output is still refused before any HTTP call, and DeepSeek and the compat presets still send `max_tokens`. Acceptance of the new key was measured for `gpt-5.4-nano` and `gpt-4o-mini` only (2026-10-15); older OpenAI ids and other servers are unmeasured. **Who is affected:** an operator whose `OPENAI_BASE_URL` points at a server that accepts only the old key. **Action:** use the `openai-compatible` preset (which keeps `max_tokens`) for that server. |
| M-B-19 | **Grok usage now counts reasoning tokens (G-50-5a, Phase 50 plan 50-21).** On Grok reasoning calls `TokenUsage.completion_tokens` and `total_tokens` now include `completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens`, which xAI reports beside `completion_tokens` rather than inside it, so `TokenBudget`, allowance checks and Treasurer charges rise to what xAI bills (at $1/M input and $10/M output, prompt 663 / completion 50 / reasoning 124 was priced 1_163_000 nanos and is now 2_403_000). `total_tokens` is still `prompt_tokens + completion_tokens`, and `reasoning_tokens` is unchanged. A response from any of the five OpenAI-compatible presets whose vendor total disagrees with the mapped total, or whose reasoning or cache counts exceed their parent, now carries `paladin.usage_unreconciled` in its metadata (buffered response or the stream's terminal chunk) and logs one warning per model (target `paladin::usage`). xAI's reasoning-exclusive reporting was live-measured on 2026-10-18 (plans 50-22 and 50-24) on `grok-4.3`, `grok-4.5` and `grok-4.6`, and xAI's billed cost matched reasoning billed at the completion rate. **Who is affected:** anyone with Grok token budgets, allowances or spend limits tuned to the old totals. **Action:** re-size them. |
| M-B-20 | **Vendor usage totals are reconciled on every adapter (G-50-5a, Phase 50 plans 50-23 and 50-24).** The OpenAI, DeepSeek and Gemini adapters now read the vendor's own total (`total_tokens`, `totalTokenCount`) on buffered and streamed calls and compare it, along with the reasoning and cache containment, with the mapped `prompt_tokens + completion_tokens`. A disagreement or containment breach now adds `paladin.usage_unreconciled` to the response metadata (buffered response or the stream's terminal chunk) and logs one warning per model (target `paladin::usage`), as the five compat presets have done since M-B-19. Anthropic, which reports no total, runs the containment checks only. The vendor total is never copied: `total_tokens` stays `prompt_tokens + completion_tokens`. The total is now optional on the wire, so a usage object without it parses instead of failing deserialisation (A2-50-23). Two counts rise. Gemini `toolUsePromptTokenCount` is folded into `prompt_tokens`: prompt 100 / tool use 300 used to map to prompt 100 and now maps to 400. This path is latent today because the adapter sends no tools. On the Anthropic vision path, `cache_read_input_tokens` and `cache_creation_input_tokens` are folded into `prompt_tokens`, as on the chat path. The operator's live usage probe of 2026-10-18 flipped no provider's reasoning semantics. Grok (beside completion), DeepSeek, Kimi and Qwen (inside) and Gemini (thoughts beside candidates) are now measured; OpenAI, Anthropic, Ollama and OpenAI-compatible stay on their documented readings, not live-measured. | Anyone whose token budgets, allowances or spend limits were sized on the undercounted Gemini tool-use or Anthropic vision totals; anyone matching on `paladin.usage_unreconciled`, which can now appear on OpenAI, DeepSeek, Gemini and Anthropic responses. | Re-size those budgets, allowances and spend limits. Treat a `paladin.usage_unreconciled` entry as a signal that the vendor's reporting changed, and report it. |
| M-B-21 | **Billed failures, hopped-past fallback bills and failed engine attempts are charged (G-50-5a, Phase 50 plans 50-26 to 50-30).** A call the vendor answered and billed but that ended as `LlmError::EmptyCompletion` or `LlmError::Refusal` used to lose its usage with the error, so the Treasurer charged nothing for it. Every adapter now carries that usage on the error (`billed_usage()`), buffered and streamed; `PricingLlmAdapter` prices it; and `PaladinExecutionService` folds it into the run's usage and cost and settles it to the treasury ledger under the call's ordinal. A fallback hop moved past after a billed refusal (`hop_on_refusal(true)`) is priced against its own model and settled in its call's one ledger row, with a per-model `model_breakdown`, whether a later hop serves or the chain ends in `AllProvidersFailed`, and on every exit of a stream; the stream's terminal chunk folds the hop bills into its cost (and into its usage when the provider reported one). A Battalion engine attempt that fails with any `PaladinError`, or that a deadline or idle bound cuts off, now reports the spend it recorded on `NodeFinished`, and the superstep settle charges it once per attempt; a structured attempt counts every repair call. So settled spend, allowance consumption and the spend reports rise to what the vendors bill. `TokenUsage.total_tokens` stays `prompt_tokens + completion_tokens`, a successful attempt is still counted once from `PaladinResult.cost`, and a bill on a model with no `treasurer.pricing` row still settles nothing. Since plan 50-32 (review WR-03), a hopped-past bill counts toward the attempt's spend at its hop, so an attempt cut off while a later hop of the same call is in flight is charged for it too. | Operators whose allowances, spend limits or budgets were sized on the undercounted spend, in particular deployments with refusal-prone or reasoning-heavy models (empty `length` completions), `hop_on_refusal(true)` chains, or Battalion retries and timeouts. | Re-size those allowances, spend limits and budgets against the ledger's new figures. No configuration or code change is needed to get the new charging. |
| M-B-22 | **Two billed-failure error changes (G-50-5a, Phase 50 plans 50-26 and 50-27).** (1) On the five OpenAI-compatible presets (Kimi, Qwen, Grok, Ollama, OpenAI-compatible), a buffered body that is empty or refused and carries no `usage` object is now classified as `LlmError::EmptyCompletion` or `LlmError::Refusal` (with no bill), where it used to fail as a schema-mismatch `LlmError::ProcessingError`; a no-`choices` body without `usage` is now `EmptyCompletion("Response contained no choices")`. OpenAI and DeepSeek follow the same rule for a no-`choices` body: it was `ProcessingError("No choices in response")` / `ProcessingError("DeepSeek response contained no choices")` and is now `EmptyCompletion("Response contained no choices")` carrying the body's `usage` as `billed_usage()` (their `usage` is required, so the bill is always present). A successful body without `usage` is still that `ProcessingError`. Both new outcomes classify as `Transience::Permanent`, where that `ProcessingError` was `Transience::Unknown`. (2) DeepSeek's `EmptyCompletion` detail no longer ends in `(provider-reported usage: prompt_tokens=.., completion_tokens=.., total_tokens=..)`; the counts are on `billed_usage()`. | Anyone matching on `ProcessingError` for those bodies, alerting on its message, or parsing token counts out of the DeepSeek message. | Match `LlmError::EmptyCompletion { .. }` / `LlmError::Refusal { .. }` (or `transience()`), and read counts from `error.billed_usage()`. |

**Worked examples** (owed alongside the rows above, per D-08's rule that a pending item is acceptable only in later-epic-owned content):

- M-B-01: **landed (CF-01, Phase 23).** A v0.9 campaign edge like

  ```rust
  // v0.9 — this edge silently fired on EVERY run, regardless of what
  // `analyzer` actually output, because `EdgeCondition::Custom` had no
  // registration mechanism and evaluated as always-true (BUG-01).
  campaign.add_edge(CampaignEdge::new(
      analyzer_id,
      escalate_id,
      EdgeCondition::Custom("is_urgent".to_string()),
  ))?;
  let service = CampaignExecutionService::new(paladin_port);
  ```

  now fails loudly at validation unless an evaluator is registered for
  `"is_urgent"`. The v0.10 fix registers one on the service (the same
  `CampaignEdge`/`EdgeCondition::Custom` construction is unchanged):

  ```rust
  use paladin_battalion::edge_evaluator::{EdgeConditionEvaluator, EdgeContext, EdgeEvaluatorError};

  struct IsUrgent;

  #[async_trait::async_trait]
  impl EdgeConditionEvaluator for IsUrgent {
      async fn evaluate(&self, output: &str, _ctx: &EdgeContext<'_>) -> Result<bool, EdgeEvaluatorError> {
          Ok(output.to_lowercase().contains("urgent"))
      }
  }

  let service = CampaignExecutionService::new(paladin_port)
      .with_evaluator("is_urgent", Arc::new(IsUrgent));
  ```

  The `WarEngine` path registers the same evaluator via
  `WarEngine::with_edge_evaluator("is_urgent", Arc::new(IsUrgent))`.
  A user who does not want an evaluator at all can instead replace the
  condition with `EdgeCondition::Contains("urgent".to_string())`,
  `EdgeCondition::Regex(...)`, or `EdgeCondition::Always` — no registry
  entry needed for any of the three. Skipping both routes produces this
  exact validation error, transcribed verbatim from the shipped code
  (`BattalionError::InvalidGraph`'s `Display`, legacy path; the
  `WarEngine` path's `EngineError::UnregisteredEdgeCondition` names the
  same offending set):

  ```text
  Invalid graph: unregistered custom edge condition(s): is_urgent -- register with CampaignExecutionService::with_evaluator before calling execute
  ```
- M-B-02: **landed (HITL-04, Phase 24).** Before this phase, SIGTERM/SIGINT exited the process
  immediately — any in-flight superstep run was dropped mid-flight with no chance to reach a
  Halted boundary, and neither shipped manifest declared `terminationGracePeriodSeconds`:

  ```yaml
  # v0.9 — k8s/server/deployment.yaml's pod spec (no termination grace declared;
  # the kubelet's own default of 30s applied, with no relationship to any
  # engine-level grace concept because none existed)
  spec:
    template:
      spec:
        containers:
          - name: paladin-server
            # ...
  ```

  After this phase, the process waits up to a configured grace window for
  in-flight runs to drain before exiting, and both shipped manifests declare a
  termination grace derived from — and at least twice — that window:

  ```yaml
  # v0.10 — k8s/server/deployment.yaml's pod spec
  spec:
    template:
      spec:
        # 2x the default APP_ENGINE_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_SECS (30s) so the kubelet's
        # SIGKILL deadline never lands mid-drain.
        terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 60
        containers:
          - name: paladin-server
            # ...
  ```

  Two new env vars (`EngineConfig`, `src/config/engine.rs`) control the process-level wait:

  | Env var | Default | Effect |
  |---|---|---|
  | `APP_ENGINE_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_SECS` | `30` | Seconds `paladin-server`/`ServiceRunner` wait, after SIGTERM/SIGINT, for in-flight superstep runs to finish before giving up on the stragglers. |
  | `APP_ENGINE_GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN` | `true` | Set `false` to restore the v0.9 no-wait behavior for legacy-only deployments — the process cancels in-flight runs and exits immediately without waiting. |

  An operator upgrading with no config changes gets `shutdown_grace_secs=30`,
  `graceful_shutdown=true` and `terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 60` from the shipped
  manifests — a strictly safer default than v0.9's immediate exit, not a behavior an operator
  must opt into. A run still executing when SIGTERM arrives either finishes inside the grace
  window (Waypoint records completion normally) or is aborted at the deadline (`NodeOutcomeKind::
  Skipped { reason: "shutdown" }`, re-listed in the Halted Waypoint's vanguard for exactly-once
  resume) — no in-flight work silently vanishes either way.
- M-B-03: **landed (RT-07, Phase 26).** No behavioral change — the v0.9 loop already fed a failed
  tool call back into the model's context and continued; this phase names that policy
  (`tool_error_mode`, default `FeedToModel`), adds `FailRun` as a new opt-in
  (`PaladinError::ArmamentFailed { tool, reason }`), and sanitizes the fed-back text. The
  sanitization is the only observable difference:

  ```text
  // v0.9 / v0.10 default (FeedToModel) -- BEFORE this phase, an upstream
  // gateway's raw error text (including a leaked credential) reached the
  // model verbatim:
  🔧 Tool Execution: fetch_report
  Result: FAILED
  Error: upstream gateway rejected the request: Authorization: Bearer sk-live-abcdef0123456789

  // v0.10 -- the identical failure, sanitized (redact-then-bound, D-34)
  // before the model ever sees it:
  🔧 Tool Execution: fetch_report
  Result: FAILED
  Error: upstream gateway rejected the request: Authorization: Bearer [REDACTED]
  You may retry with corrected arguments or proceed without it.
  ```

  Set `tool_error_mode = FailRun` (`AgentRuntimeConfig.tool_errors.mode`, or per tool via
  `tool_errors.per_tool`) to fail the run instead of feeding the error back —
  `PaladinExecutionService::with_tool_error_config`, service-level configuration only
  (`PaladinConfig` is untouched, D-10).

  **Post-plan review fix (26-REVIEW.md CR-01/WR-03/WR-01):** the sanitization above was, at
  first cut, wired into only the `ArsenalError`/handoff `Err` path (`format_error`). A
  code-review fix pass widened it to the `ArmamentResult { success: false, .. }` business-failure
  `Error:` field and the successful `Output:` field too (`format_result`, `ToolResultFormatter`) —
  both previously embedded tool-supplied text raw, and the FAILED-business-result path is
  arguably the more likely one for a tool to surface an upstream response body through, since it
  is the tool author's own designed error-reporting mechanism. The same pass also tightened the
  `key=`/`token=` marker to require a word boundary, so an ordinary word like `monkey=5` embedded
  in a tool's JSON output is no longer misredacted. No further action is required to keep today's
  behavior — this only narrows what gets sanitized (adds sanitization where none existed, and
  removes a false-positive class), it does not change the `tool_error_mode` policy itself.
- M-B-04: no worked example owed — this phase (ENG-08) both introduces the behavior and documents it in full above.

**Note on Phase 25 (Node-Level Fault Tolerance — epic `FT`), D-30: no behavioral change; no row is added above.** Every Aegis capability — per-node retry, per-attempt `run_timeout`/`idle_timeout`, `Route`/`Absorb`/`Custom` error handlers, node result caching — is **opt-in per node** through `WarGraph::set_aegis`/`with_default_aegis` (code, not configuration; §9.5), and a v0.9 graph declares none, so an upgraded deployment's workflows execute identically: one attempt per node, no per-attempt bound, a node failure fails the run with the same `node execution error: {message}` display line as before, and nothing is served from a cache (`node_without_aegis_behaves_exactly_as_before`, `pre_aegis_and_limit_failures_carry_none`, `the_display_line_on_a_failed_waypoint_is_unchanged`). The one run-level knob this phase makes real, `EngineConfig.run_timeout_secs` (§9.5), keeps its `None` default, so no run gains a budget it did not configure. `FallbackLlmAdapter` is a new adapter a caller constructs explicitly — no existing provider adapter's behavior changes, and the shared `map_http_status` mapping (25-05) changes only which *typed* `LlmError` variant a non-2xx status becomes (`ProviderError { status }` instead of `ProcessingError(String)`), never whether an adapter's own retry loop retries it. The legacy `FormationExecutionService`/`PhalanxExecutionService`/`CampaignExecutionService`/`Commander` paths and the legacy Battalion `RetryPolicy`/`ErrorStrategy`/timeout services are untouched (X-03). Two accepted, documented caveats that are *not* behavioral changes for a v0.9 graph but matter once a node opts in: an `idle_timeout` on a node whose `PaladinPort` keeps the default `execute_observed` degrades to a per-attempt wall clock (D-19), and a hanging `EdgeConditionEvaluator` remains outside every timeout (R-23-01, still accepted — per-attempt timeouts wrap node execution, not edge evaluation).

## 9.2 Rust API changes (compile-affecting; the X-10 register)

Columns: **Crate · Type/Trait · Change · Mitigation (`#[non_exhaustive]` / default method / new trait / `#[serde(default)]`) · Deliberate-breaking? (Y/N, justification) · Requirement ID**.

The **Crate** cell of every row marked `Y` is the crate's **published crates.io package name** (`paladin-ai-core` for the `crates/paladin-core` directory, whose library is still imported as `paladin_core`), never the directory name: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `semver` job compares that cell against `.cargo/semver-checks-allowlist.toml`'s `crate = "..."` keys by exact string, in both directions, and the allowlist's own schema requires the package name. (Corrected in Plan 25-14 — the four FT-owned rows below were first written with `paladin-core`, which the set-equality step reads as a different crate from `paladin-ai-core` and fails on.)

| Crate | Type / Trait | Change | Mitigation | Deliberate-breaking? | Req |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `paladin-ai-core` | `StopReason` (defined in `paladin_core::platform::container::execution_result`, re-exported by `paladin_ports::output::paladin_port`) | new variants `CallLimit`, `TokenBudget`; enum marked `#[non_exhaustive]` | `#[non_exhaustive]`, suppressed via `[package.metadata.cargo-semver-checks.lints] enum_marked_non_exhaustive = "allow"` in `crates/paladin-core/Cargo.toml`, mirrored in `.cargo/semver-checks-allowlist.toml` | Y — both new variants are `is_successful() == true` and `is_limit() == true` (a budget stopped the run but the model's last response stands, D-08); the X-10.2 exception overview section 3 names for this exact enum is deliberately declined (D-07); every in-tree exhaustive match (`src/application/cli/formatters/output.rs`, `crates/paladin-web/src/agent_controller.rs`) gained a wildcard arm in the same commit | RT-FR-04, RT-FR-06 |
| `paladin-ai-core` | `BattalionError` | new variant `Node(NodeError)`, path-qualified to the structured `paladin_core::platform::container::node_error::NodeError` (never the legacy `battalion::NodeError` summary); enum marked `#[non_exhaustive]` | `#[non_exhaustive]`, suppressed via `[package.metadata.cargo-semver-checks.lints] enum_marked_non_exhaustive = "allow"` in `crates/paladin-core/Cargo.toml`, mirrored in `.cargo/semver-checks-allowlist.toml` | Y — a future node-execution failure variant must be addable without a semver-major bump (D-04); every in-tree exhaustive match over `BattalionError` gained a wildcard arm in the same commit | FT-FR-02 |

The suppression was restored byte-for-byte (proved with `cmp`). Every in-tree full `PaladinResult` literal (21 across 7 files, the consumer muster's included) gained `run_metadata: BTreeMap::new()` in the same commit as the field. | Y — a public field on a constructible struct breaks full struct literals downstream. | `paladin-storage` | `RedisNodeCache` (overrides `put_fenced`), `NODE_CACHE_PUT_FENCED_LUA` (new in 0.11, behind `redis-cache`, `crates/paladin-storage/src/node_cache/redis.rs`); `RedisCadence` lock scripts `CADENCE_TRY_LOCK_LUA` and `CADENCE_UNLOCK_LUA`, `InMemoryCadence` and `ResilientCadence` lock support (`crates/paladin-storage/src/cadence/`) | `RedisCadence::try_lock` is one atomic script: set-if-absent with `PX`, with a fencing token from an `INCR` on a per-key counter whose TTL is at least ten times the lock's and at least an hour; `unlock` is a compare-and-delete on the exact token and a `Local` token never reaches Redis. Lock and counter keys sit at `{prefix}:%lock:{key}` and `{prefix}:%fence:{key}`, which no pacing key can equal. `RedisNodeCache::put_fenced` runs an atomic script that ignores a `Distributed` token strictly lower than the last one seen for the entry (an equal one is accepted; the ignored write is `Ok(())`) and treats a `Local` token as a plain `put`. `InMemoryCadence` issues `Local` tokens from a per-instance counter; `ResilientCadence` fails the lock open onto the in-process lock during an outage (D-13) and routes `unlock` by token source, never returning an error | new items and trait implementations only; nothing pre-existing is changed | N -- new in 0.11; measured at the Phase 43 closeout (plan 43-13) with cargo-semver-checks 0.50.0 against the published 0.10.1, `--release-type minor`, paladin-storage's crate-wide allows disabled and restored byte-for-byte (built with `--features redis-cadence`, which the baseline lacks): 196 checks, 196 pass, none fired | PACE-04 |

With a node cache attached (`with_node_cache`), a cache-policy node that misses takes the lock before executing, stores its result through `NodeCachePort::put_fenced` with its fencing token and releases the lock on every exit; concurrent identical misses wait (polling about every 100 ms plus jitter, honouring the run's cancellation) and serve the stored delta as a cache hit, or execute themselves once `lock_ttl` elapses. A lock error runs the node without the lock after one warning (D-13, D-29). Child `Battalion` runs inherit the lock with the node cache. An engine without `with_cadence`, or a node without a cache policy, makes no lock call | additive builder method on a non-`#[non_exhaustive]` struct whose fields are private; nothing pre-existing is changed | N -- new in 0.11; measured at the Phase 43 closeout (plan 43-13) with cargo-semver-checks 0.50.0 against the published 0.10.1, `--release-type minor`, paladin-battalion's crate-wide allows disabled and restored byte-for-byte: 196 checks, 1 lint fired (`enum_struct_variant_field_added` on `RunOutcome::Halted.cause`, the Phase 42 row above), and this item fired none | PACE-04 |

Covered by the root crate's existing crate-wide `constructible_struct_adds_field = "allow"`, mirrored in `.cargo/semver-checks-allowlist.toml` | Y -- measured with cargo-semver-checks 0.50.0 against the published `paladin-ai` 0.10.1, `--release-type minor`, with the root crate's lint table removed and restored byte-for-byte (`cmp`-proven), with `--default-features` and again with `--default-features --features llm-all` (the nine adapter features): 196 checks, 1 lint fired in each run (`constructible_struct_adds_field`, naming `AgentDefinition.reasoning_effort` and `AgentDefinition.reasoning_fallback`, already allowed crate-wide). A public field added to a struct that is constructible from outside the crate breaks every full struct literal downstream. Intentional: YAML per-agent effort (D-00e) needs a home on the agent's definition | REASON-01 |

**Note on this plan (Phase 33, COMM-04) — empirically, not by oversight:** the two `paladin-memory` rows immediately above (`RagRetrievalService`, `retrieve_context_with_timeout`) are marked `N/A` rather than `Y` and have no `.cargo/semver-checks-allowlist.toml` mirror, because both fired **zero** `cargo semver-checks 0.50.0` lints across all six discovery runs this plan recorded (33-05-SUMMARY.md, Task 1) — confirmed, not guessed, by inspecting the tool's own 254-lint catalog: no lint in the 0.50.0 catalog checks an inherent method's or a free function's return-type change, or a parameter's type (as opposed to count) change, so a signature break of exactly this shape is invisible to the tool regardless of feature flags. This is the identical tool-coverage gap Phase 32 already documented for `Commissary::new`/`from_port`'s parameter-count break (immediately above), now confirmed to extend to return-type and parameter-type changes as well. The allowlist mirrors suppressions, so a pair with zero fired lints gets zero entries; both rows are kept as migration guidance for the downstream refactor (PRD R1/R2/R6), and the behavioural half of this break is recorded in `CHANGELOG.md`'s `[0.10.0]` section.

**Note on this plan (Phase 32, PRIM-05) — empirically, not by oversight:** the `paladin-llm | Commissary` row above is marked `N/A` rather than `Y` and has no `.cargo/semver-checks-allowlist.toml` mirror, and `paladin-ports | TokenCounterPort` (D-06/D-07's additive `is_exact` defaulted trait method) has no new row at all, because both fired **zero** `cargo semver-checks 0.50.0` lints across every discovery run this plan recorded (32-05-SUMMARY.md, Task 1, Runs 1-2 and 6) — for the port's additive defaulted method this matches D-07's own hedge ("likely nothing fires"); for the Commissary constructor break, `cargo semver-checks --list`'s full 254-lint catalog was inspected and contains no lint that checks an inherent/associated function's parameter count (`function_parameter_count_changed` exists only for free/module-level `pub fn`, and none of the fifteen `inherent_method_*` lints covers arity). The allowlist mirrors suppressions, so a pair with zero fired lints gets zero entries; the Commissary row is kept as migration guidance for the downstream refactor (PRD R6, CONTEXT D-15), and both breaks are also recorded in `CHANGELOG.md`'s `[0.10.0]` section (D-16).

**Note on Phase 28 (Observability & Tooling — epic `OBS`), D-39:** beyond the two rows above this
phase resolved (`Settings` marked `Y`; `PaladinExecutionService` marked `N`), this phase touches a
large number of **new-in-0.10** types that therefore get no row — `TraceEvent` (moved from
`paladin-ports` to `paladin-core` and reshaped to twelve variants under a `TraceRecord` envelope,
D-01/D-02; it was itself new-in-0.10, shipped by Phase 22, never present at the `v0.9.0` tag),
`TraceSink` (retargeted from `TraceEvent` to `TraceRecord`; also new-in-0.10, Phase 22), the new
`TraceEmitter`/`CompositeSink`/`StandaloneEmitter` types (`paladin-ports`), `FallbackLlmAdapter`
(`with_trace_sink` renamed `with_trace_emitter`; the adapter itself is new-in-0.10, Phase 25),
`RunStreamMode` (gained the `Replay` variant, and is marked `#[non_exhaustive]` in the same change
per X-10.2's spirit; the enum is new-in-0.10, Phase 27), `RunStreamEvent` (gained the additive
`trace_seq` field on its payload; also new-in-0.10, Phase 27), `RunTracePort`/`RunTraceError`
(new trait, `paladin-ports`), `RunInspectorPort`/`InspectorView` and its whole value chain (new
trait and types, `paladin-ports`), `WarEngine` (gained `trace_emitter()`,
`with_bound_trace`/`with_bound_trace_dispatcher`/`with_trace_capacity`; the whole type is
new-in-0.10, Phase 22), `WaypointRetentionService` (gained `with_run_trace_port`/
`last_run_traces_removed()`; also new-in-0.10, Plan 22-14), `ModelCallContext`/`ToolCallContext`
(gained `trace_emitter`/`middleware_action_hint` fields; both new-in-0.10, Phase 26),
`ExecutionMiddleware` implementors (`chain.rs`/`resilience.rs`/`guardrail.rs`, all new-in-0.10,
Phase 26), and the entire `crates/paladin-eval` crate, `crates/paladin-battalion/src/engine/export/`
module and `paladin-web`'s `dev_ui_controller.rs` (all wholly new in this phase). **`NodeContext`
deliberately did NOT gain a trace accessor** — the Claude's-Discretion item 28-CONTEXT.md's own
text raised ("whether `NodeContext` gains a `trace: Arc<dyn TraceEmitter>` accessor") was resolved
by NOT adding one: every below-engine producer this phase wired (`FallbackLlmAdapter`, the
middleware chain, `PaladinExecutionService`) reaches its run's emitter through the `RUN_TRACE_EMITTER`
task-local instead (28-06), so no new field was needed on `NodeContext` itself.
**`WarGraphDoc` is confirmed untouched** by this phase (`crates/paladin-battalion/src/engine/graph_doc.rs`
carries no diff attributable to any Phase 28 plan). **`MockLlmAdapter`/`MockScriptEntry` are
confirmed untouched** (D-30, verified `git diff --quiet` across the whole phase in 28-05's own
gate evidence). Because none of the types in this paragraph is a *pre-existing* public type at
`v0.9.0`, X-10 does not apply to any of them, and none gets its own §9.2 row here. **This is a
deliberate zero**, not an omission: a reader who diffs this phase's changes against a type list
and finds no row for any of the types named in this paragraph should read that absence as this
note, not as an oversight. The inventory above was taken FIRST, against the published `v0.9.0`
crate contents, before either of this phase's two real rows (`Settings`, `PaladinExecutionService`)
was written — matching X-10.1's own instruction.

**Note on Plan 22-01 (this phase's tracer plan):** Plan 22-01 added the `battlefield`, `battlefield_error`, and `waypoint` modules to `paladin-core`, `waypoint_port` to `paladin-ports`, the `waypoint` adapter module to `paladin-storage`, and the `engine` module to `paladin-battalion`. All of these are **new** modules/types — none of Plan 22-01's changes touched a *pre-existing* public type's signature (its "modified" files were module-registration edits, e.g. adding a `mod battlefield;` line). **This is a deliberate zero**, not an omission: no register row is added for Plan 22-01.

**Note on Phase 23 (Control Flow — Dynamic Routing, Fan-Out & Subgraphs), D-27:** this phase reshapes several engine and Waypoint types this plan's own read-list names — `StateNode` (`run`'s return type changed from a bare `StateDelta` to `Directive`), `NodeSpec` (a `Battalion` variant and, on `Paladin`, a `directive_parser` field added), `NodeContext` (a `muster: Option<MusterContext>` field added), `EngineLimits` (a `max_muster_tasks` field added), `EngineError` (new variants added to an already `#[non_exhaustive]` enum — `GotoUnknownNode`, `ParleyNotSupported`, `DirectiveParseFailed`, `EmptyMuster`, `DuplicateMusterTaskKey`, `MusterTaskLimitExceeded`, `MusterUnknownWorker`, `MusterWorkerNotATemplate`, `WorkerTemplateIsEntry`, `WorkerTemplateHasStaticIncomingEdge`, `MusterPrefixSchemaField` among them), `WarGraph` (`validate`'s signature changed — it gained an `edge_evaluators: &EdgeEvaluatorRegistry` parameter for CF-01's fail-closed unregistered-condition check — and a new `worker_templates` marker set was added, seeded via the new `add_worker_template` method) and `Waypoint` (two additive `#[serde(default)]` fields carrying Muster progress and `checkpoint_ns`). Every one of these types is **absent at the `v0.9.0` tag** — `StateNode`, `NodeSpec`, `NodeContext`, `EngineLimits` and `EngineError` were all introduced by Plan 22-01 (see the note immediately above); `Waypoint` is new in 0.10 and already carries the "N/A — new type" row above. Because none of them is a *pre-existing* public type, X-10 — which governs only a signature change to a type that shipped in `v0.9.0` — does not apply to any of them, and none gets its own §9.2 row here. **This is a deliberate zero**, not an omission: a reader who diffs this phase's changes against a type list and finds no `StateNode`/`NodeSpec`/`NodeContext`/`EngineLimits`/`EngineError`/`WarGraph`/`Waypoint` row should read that absence as this note, not as an oversight.

Every one of these types is **absent at the `v0.9.0` tag** or is itself new in 0.10 (`ThreadApiState`) — `RunOutcome`, `EngineError`, `NodeSpec`, `NodeContext` and `NodeOutcomeKind` were all introduced by Plan 22-01 (see the note above); `WaypointSummary` is new in 0.10 (`paladin-ports`, Plan 22-01). Because none of them is a *pre-existing* public type at `v0.9.0`, X-10 does not apply to any of them, and none gets its own §9.2 row here. **This is a deliberate zero**, not an omission: a reader who diffs this phase's changes against a type list and finds no `RunOutcome`/`EngineError`/`NodeSpec`/`NodeContext`/`NodeOutcomeKind`/`WaypointSummary`/`ThreadApiState` row should read that absence as this note, not as an oversight. `Waypoint`'s own `AwaitingInput` reshape and the new `fork_of` field are covered by the `Waypoint` row above; `ParleyPort` is recorded above as a new trait.

hashed byte stream, canonical JSON sorted and length-prefixed; a `v5:`-tagged fingerprint is
recognized as stale on resume, never reinterpreted, the same discipline every prior fingerprint
bump in this document follows), `DirectiveParser`'s internal `StructuredDirective` extraction
refactored to call the new, shared `paladin_core::platform::container::structured::extract_json`
rather than its own private `extract_envelope` (RT-05, D-26 — CF-FR-06's "MUST reuse" rule; no
behavior change, the directive parser's own tests are unchanged), and `MockLlmAdapter` recording
the `response_format` of the most recent request it received via a new
`last_response_format()` accessor (RT-05, D-28 — test-only surface, strictly additive). Every one
of these is either a field/method addition on a type that is itself absent at the `v0.9.0` tag
(`NodeSpec`, `NodeContext`, `EngineError`, `WarGraph` were all introduced by Plan 22-01; the
fingerprint format and `DirectiveParser` are likewise new-in-0.10) or a test-only adapter method.
Because none of them is a change to a *pre-existing* public type at `v0.9.0`, X-10 does not apply
to any of them, and none gets its own §9.2 row here. **This is a deliberate zero**, not an
omission: a reader who diffs this phase's changes against a type list and finds no
`NodeSpec::Paladin.output_schema`/`NodeContext.vault`/`EngineError`/`WarGraph`/`DirectiveParser`/
`MockLlmAdapter` row should read that absence as this note, not as an oversight.

## 9.3 Toolchain & dependencies

- **Final MSRV: 1.88** (Rust, edition 2024) — an X-11.2 stop-and-flag resolution, landed in Plan 22.1-04. The prior "1.85" figure was derived from edition 2024's own minimum and was never measured against this workspace's actual dependency graph; 1.88 is the first MSRV figure in this project's history with a passing measurement behind it (`cargo +1.88 check --workspace --all-features --all-targets --locked`, zero errors). The chain that forces it: the STDIO MCP transport requires `rmcp` at its exact `=2.1.0` pin (D-06, unchanged — no `rmcp` version restores 1.85 with `transport-child-process`), which with the `transport-child-process` feature requires `process-wrap ^9.0`, whose releases require rustc 1.86 or 1.87; separately, `time` >= 0.3.47 (the fix for RUSTSEC-2026-0009) requires rustc 1.88. The higher of the two — 1.88 — is the floor, and it clears the advisory with no new security exception. Two alternatives were considered and rejected: **1.87 plus a new RUSTSEC-2026-0009 exception** (rejected — adds to the exception sprawl ADR-0024 governs, for a floor one release lower than the clean option), and **1.86 with `process-wrap` held at an exact `.0` pin** (rejected — a transitive `.0` pin plus the same exception). The declared floor now lives in exactly one place, `workspace.package.rust-version = "1.88"` in root `Cargo.toml`; all ten crate manifests inherit it via `rust-version.workspace = true`. The lockfile was restored to its pre-`44e13fbd` resolution (reverting the 22-17 hand-downgrade of `time`, `process-wrap`, `darling`, `serde_with`, `tonic`, `idna_adapter`, `home` and others) and re-resolved under the new `[workspace] resolver = "3"` (D-09), which also serves as the recurrence guard against a future `cargo update` silently re-selecting a version above the declared floor. **X-11.4 default-feature-set confirmation:** `cargo tree -e features -p paladin-ai` under default features was compared before (HEAD's pinned-back lockfile) and after (the restored lockfile) this change — the only package-name delta in the full workspace lockfile is `bs58` reappearing (a resolution-only difference restored alongside the rest of the pre-`44e13fbd` graph, per CONTEXT.md's discretion note), and `bs58` does not appear anywhere in `paladin-ai`'s default-feature tree. The default set did not grow.
- **New feature added to an existing dependency — `uuid`'s `v7` feature.** Added to the existing pinned `uuid = 1.8.0` workspace dependency (D-03) to generate time-ordered `WaypointId`s via `Uuid::now_v7()`. No new crate name. Landed in Plan 22-01 (`Cargo.toml`'s `[workspace.dependencies] uuid` feature array now reads `["v4", "v7", "serde"]`).
- **New cargo feature — `postgres` on `paladin-storage`. Landed this plan (22-06), Task 2.** `postgres = ["dep:sqlx", "sqlx/postgres"]` (mirroring the existing `sqlite`/`mysql` feature-gate pattern) enabling `PostgresWaypointStore` (ENG-05/D-01), plus the facade passthrough `storage-postgres = ["paladin-storage/postgres"]` on `paladin-ai` (mirroring the existing `storage-mysql` passthrough) — `storage = ["storage-mysql", "storage-postgres"]` — so a consumer of the facade crate reaches it via `cargo build -p paladin-ai --features storage-postgres` without any `paladin-storage`-level override. Neither `default` nor this addition to `storage`/`full` puts Postgres in the default set.
- **`sqlx`'s `macros` feature added — Task 1 blocking-issue fix.** `sqlx::migrate!` (used by both `sqlite.rs` and `postgres.rs` to embed their versioned migrations at compile time) lives behind sqlx's `macros` feature, not `migrate` alone; added to the workspace-level `sqlx` dependency (`Cargo.toml`'s `[workspace.dependencies] sqlx` feature array now reads `[..., "migrate", "macros"]`). No new crate name — same already-pinned `sqlx 0.8`.
- **Default feature set impact (X-11.4), confirmed at this plan's HEAD:** `cargo tree -e features -p paladin-ai` (default features) contains no `sqlx/postgres`/Postgres-driver entry anywhere in the graph — verified by `cargo tree -e features -p paladin-ai | grep -i postgres` returning no matches. `cargo build -p paladin-ai --features storage-postgres` compiles the adapter; `cargo build -p paladin-storage --features postgres` and `cargo clippy -p paladin-storage --features postgres --all-targets -- -D warnings` are both clean. The default `paladin-ai` build also gains no heavyweight dependency from the earlier `uuid`/`v7` addition — `v7` only pulls in `uuid`'s own `atomic`/`rng` sub-features, not a new crate.
- **Phase 25 (Node-Level Fault Tolerance, D-30): no new dependency.** Verified by direct manifest read at close-out (Plan 25-14): `rand = "0.8"` was **already** a direct dependency of `paladin-battalion` (`crates/paladin-battalion/Cargo.toml`) before this phase — the retry loop's jitter source needed nothing added, which corrects D-30's own "`rand` if not already a battalion dependency" framing; `redis = "0.32.2"` was **already** `paladin-storage`'s optional dependency behind `redis-queue`, and the new cache adapter shares that one dependency line. This means `cargo tree -e features -p paladin-ai` (which includes dev-edges by default) shows `opentelemetry*` even on a build with no `--features otel` passed — but `cargo tree -e no-dev,features -p paladin-ai` (the PRODUCTION-build view X-11.4 governs) shows zero, matching the row immediately above. This is the plan's own explicitly-instructed dev-only widening, not a gap. **MSRV proof:** covered by the same `cargo check --workspace --all-targets --all-features` gate run as every other dependency in this section.

Both a `paladin-eval` LIBRARY dependency (`ScenarioRunner::trials` globs its scenario-file pattern at runtime, not compile time) and a root-facade optional dependency behind the `cli` feature (`paladin-cli eval run <glob>`'s own glob expansion).** Root `Cargo.toml`'s `glob = { version = "0.3.4", optional = true }` and `cli`'s feature list gains `"dep:glob"` alongside the pre-existing `"dep:paladin-eval"` entry. **MSRV proof:** `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN=1.88 cargo check --workspace --all-targets --features cli` passes clean — verified 2026-09-09.

No new package enters `Cargo.lock`.** `httpdate` was already resolved at `1.0.3` through `hyper`; `crates/paladin-llm/src/rate_limit_headers.rs` is its first direct consumer, parsing the three RFC 7231 HTTP-date forms (IMF-fixdate, RFC 850, asctime) of a `Retry-After` header and the response `Date` header. `Cargo.lock` gains one dependency-list line (`httpdate` under `paladin-llm`) and no new package; the package-legitimacy audit in `43-RESEARCH.md` records verdict OK (about ten years old, `github.com/pyfisch/httpdate`). The dependency is unconditional (the parser module is not feature-gated), and `cargo build -p paladin-llm --no-default-features` still compiles. `make security` (cargo-audit and cargo-deny) passes with the new direct edge.

The `dep:rand` entries are removed from those feature lists and `rand = { workspace = true }` is a plain dependency. `rand 0.8` was already a workspace dependency and already resolved in `Cargo.lock` (the same version `paladin-storage` uses for the in-process Cadence's jitter); `cargo tree -p paladin-llm --no-default-features -e normal --depth 1` now lists it once. - **No MSRV bump proposed by this phase.** Every dependency named above was verified against the pinned `1.88` floor and none required raising it; had one, it would have been a stop-and-flag item recorded here rather than applied unilaterally (D-39), and none was.

## 9.4 Persistence & schema migrations

- **New table: `waypoints`. Landed this plan (22-06).** Two versioned migration files, one per backend (superseding this section's earlier single-path placeholder from Plan 22-04 — one crate now ships two dialects of the same logical table, hence the per-backend subdirectory split): `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/sqlite/001_create_waypoints_table.sql` (Task 1) and `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/postgres/001_create_waypoints_table.sql` (Task 2; `001_` continues the per-crate numbering convention established by `crates/paladin-memory/migrations/001_create_garrison_tables.sql`). SQLite stores `payload` as `TEXT` holding serialized JSON (D-02, for debuggability and because the `sqlx` `json` feature is already enabled workspace-wide); PostgreSQL stores `payload` as `JSONB`, bound via an explicit `::jsonb` cast on the parameter (sqlx sends bound `String` parameters as text over the wire; the cast is what tells Postgres to store it as JSONB rather than TEXT). Both share the same logical schema: `waypoint_id` (primary key), `thread_id`, `parent_id` (nullable), `superstep`, `status` (its own JSON-serialized column, so `history`/`list_threads` summaries can be built without deserializing the full payload), `payload`, `created_at`, with an index on `(thread_id, created_at DESC)`.
- **Runs automatically, not manually.** Both migrations are embedded at compile time via `sqlx::migrate!` (which required adding `sqlx`'s `macros` feature to the workspace-level `sqlx` dependency — a Task 1 blocking-issue fix, no new crate name, see §9.3) and applied automatically when `SqliteWaypointStore`/`PostgresWaypointStore` is constructed — no manual `sqlx migrate run` step is required of the operator. Idempotent: constructing a store twice against the same database is safe (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`/`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` plus `sqlx::migrate::Migrator`'s own applied-version tracking), proven by `constructing_store_twice_against_same_file_is_idempotent` (SQLite) and exercised against a real server by the Postgres Tier 2 suite (`docker/docker-compose.test.yml`'s `postgres-test` service, `make test-integration-docker`).
- **Reversibility:** not reversible without data loss (dropping `waypoints` discards all checkpoint history). No `down` migration is provided, consistent with every existing migration in this workspace (`garrison`, `workflow`, `content`, `user` tables), none of which ship a reverse migration.
- **Storage growth guidance:** unbounded by default — every completed superstep writes one `waypoints` row containing a full `Battlefield` snapshot. Configure `WaypointRetentionConfig` (`max_age_days`, `max_waypoints_per_thread`; see §9.5) to bound growth. Pruning MUST NOT (and, per ENG-FR-18, will not) delete a thread's single latest Waypoint or any Waypoint with status `AwaitingInput`.
