- **Memory and storage growth are measured, not asserted (ENG-NFR-01/02, Plan 22-10).** `benches/engine_benchmarks.rs` measures `SqliteWaypointStore::save` latency at three Battlefield payload sizes (1 KiB, 512 KiB, just under 1 MiB) against the ENG-NFR-01 target of under 10 ms p50, and per-superstep engine wall-clock cost at two Vanguard widths; `examples/war_engine_memory_baseline.rs` measures resident-memory growth per superstep for a fixed graph and counts Battlefield clones per superstep (ENG-NFR-02: at most one shared clone per superstep, via the Arc-shared read snapshot, plus one per concurrently executing node view), failing loudly if that bound regresses. Measured figures and the commit they were taken at are recorded in `.planning/phases/22-battlefield-state-superstep-engine/22-10-SUMMARY.md` — treat the per-checkpoint storage-growth guidance above alongside those figures, not in isolation, when sizing a deployment's Waypoint retention policy.
- **Existing state is unaffected.** Existing Citadel JSON/SQLite state files remain readable unchanged — the `waypoints` table is entirely new and additive. `WaypointPort` is deliberately separate from `CitadelPort` (see the rustdoc rationale on `crates/paladin-ports/src/output/waypoint_port.rs`: Waypoints are high-frequency and append-mostly, addressed by thread; Citadel remains for coarse, explicitly-invoked whole-entity snapshots). No Citadel schema or file format is touched by this phase.
- **Persisted `GraphFingerprint` format.** Every Waypoint's payload embeds a `GraphFingerprint` string in the form `{version}:{blake3_hex}` — a versioned tag over the blake3 hex digest of the canonical, deterministically sorted byte stream of node ids (plus Paladin output fields), edge specs, schema field dispatch rules, the declared entry set, `defer_flags`, and `dynamic_targets` (excludes prompts/models, per D-04). This format was decided at Plan 22-01's Task 1 checkpoint (option-b), specifically so a future algorithm change is a detectable new tag rather than a silent invalidation of every stored thread's `resume`. **Bumped `v1:` -> `v2:` in Phase 22.1 (CR-01, D-17):** `v1`'s canonical byte encoding concatenated fields with unescaped ASCII delimiters (`|`, `-`, `:`) that were themselves legal `NodeId`/`FieldName` characters, so two structurally different graphs could be crafted to hash identically (e.g. two nodes `"a"`+`"b"` vs. one node `"a|nf|b"`). `v2` replaces this with a length-prefixed encoding with no delimiter collision. Every `v1:`-tagged fingerprint stored before this bump is recognized as stale (a version-tag mismatch) rather than silently reinterpreted under the new encoding. **Bumped `v4:` → `v5:` in Phase 25 (D-11, 25-03):** each node's own `set_aegis` sidecar entry's `on_error` and `cache` policies (and, separately, the graph-wide `default_aegis`) now enter the hashed byte stream, sorted by node id and length-prefixed, because they change what a run *does*; `retry` and `timeout` are deliberately excluded, exactly like every `EngineLimits` field, so tightening either never makes `resume` fail `GraphMismatch`. A `v4:`-tagged fingerprint is recognized as stale on resume, never reinterpreted.
- **Phase 25 (Node-Level Fault Tolerance, D-30): none — no SQL migration.** The node cache stores Redis keys (`{key_prefix}:{key}`, each a JSON `CachedDelta` carrying its own `schema_version`, expired server-side by `PSETEX`) or process-local in-memory entries, never a table. The Waypoint additions this phase makes — `NodeExecutionRecord.attempts`/`cache_hit`, `WaypointStatus::Failed.node_error`, `FieldSpec.cache` — are additive `#[serde(default)]` fields **inside the existing JSON `payload` column**, not new columns: `BATTLEFIELD_SCHEMA_VERSION` is unchanged (`1.0.0`, pinned by test), a pre-Phase-25 payload deserializes with the defaults, and the three-backend Waypoint contract suite round-trips both shapes. No migration file was added under `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/`.
- **New column: `garrison_entries.is_summary`. Landed Phase 26 (26-07), RT-03/RT-FR-12, D-17.** One new migration file, `crates/paladin-memory/migrations/002_add_garrison_is_summary.sql`: `ALTER TABLE garrison_entries ADD COLUMN is_summary INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0;`. `001_create_garrison_tables.sql` is byte-untouched — its version number and checksum are unchanged, so a v0.9 database's `_sqlx_migrations` row for `001` still verifies and `002` applies cleanly on top of it. No down migration (additive, out of scope per D-17).
- **Runs automatically at adapter construction, not manually.** `crates/paladin-memory/src/migrations.rs` declares the crate's one shared, compile-time-embedded migrator (`static MIGRATOR`, built from `crates/paladin-memory/migrations/`) — the **embedded migrator replaces the prior CWD-relative runtime path** (`sqlite_garrison.rs`'s old `Migrator::new("./migrations")` call, which depended on the process's current working directory containing a `migrations/` directory — fragile in a container). `SqliteGarrison::initialize()` now calls this one shared static; plan 26-09's `SqliteVault` will call the same one for its own `003_create_vault_tables.sql`, keeping the crate to a single `_sqlx_migrations` numbering sequence. No manual `sqlx migrate run` step is required of the operator. Idempotent: constructing `SqliteGarrison` twice against the same database file is safe, proven by `sqlite_garrison_constructs_twice_idempotently`; a pre-existing v0.9 database (only `001` applied) migrates forward with its rows intact, proven by `existing_v0_9_database_migrates_forward` (the pre-existing row reads back `is_summary == false`, matching the column default and the struct's `#[serde(default)]`).
- **The root `migrations/` mirror is removed in the same change.** The byte-identical copy that lived at the repo root (`migrations/001_create_garrison_tables.sql`) existed only to satisfy the old runtime path's `./migrations`-relative lookup in a container; with the embedded migrator, no runtime reader of that directory remains. `Dockerfile:28` (`COPY migrations ./migrations`) and `Dockerfile:57` (`COPY --from=builder /app/migrations /app/migrations`) are deleted, and `docs/src/deployment/docker.md`'s matching lines are updated to state that migrations are embedded in the binary and require no copy step.
- **Storage growth: negligible.** One `INTEGER` (SQLite stores small integers in as little as 1 byte) per existing `garrison_entries` row.
- **Existing state is unaffected beyond the one additive column.** Existing Citadel JSON/SQLite state files remain readable unchanged — `is_summary` is scoped to the `garrison_entries` table only.
- **New table: `vault_records`. Landed Phase 26 (26-09), RT-04/RT-FR-13…16, D-18/D-23.** One new migration file, `crates/paladin-memory/migrations/003_create_vault_tables.sql`: `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS vault_records (ns TEXT NOT NULL, key TEXT NOT NULL, value TEXT NOT NULL, created_at TEXT NOT NULL, updated_at TEXT NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY (ns, key))` plus an index on `(ns, key)` — `SqliteVault`'s persistent backing store for the Vault (RT-04). `ns` is the `Namespace`'s `/`-joined path, unambiguous because a segment can never itself contain `/`. Both `001_create_garrison_tables.sql` and `002_add_garrison_is_summary.sql` are byte-untouched. No down migration (additive, out of scope). **Runs automatically, via the same shared embedded migrator §9.4's `002` entry describes** — `crates/paladin-memory/src/migrations.rs`'s single `static MIGRATOR` now embeds all three files, so `SqliteGarrison` and `SqliteVault` share one `_sqlx_migrations` numbering sequence: a database that only ever constructed a `SqliteGarrison` gains an empty `vault_records` table too, and vice versa (`IF NOT EXISTS` on both statements — documented, harmless, D-23). `SqliteVault::new(path)` constructs twice idempotently (the `SqliteWaypointStore`/`SqliteGarrison` precedent test). **Storage growth:** bounded by the Vault's own `max_value_bytes` cap (default 64 KiB) per value; no automatic pruning — a value persists until explicitly deleted, by design (RT-04's "until explicitly deleted" lifetime). **Existing state is unaffected beyond the one additive table** — `garrison_entries` and every Citadel JSON/SQLite state file remain readable unchanged.
- **New table: `runs`. Landed Phase 27 (27-02), PLAT-01/PLAT-02, D-04/D-05/D-17/D-18.** Two versioned migration files, one per backend, following the `waypoints` precedent's per-backend subdirectory split: `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/sqlite/002_create_runs_table.sql` and `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/postgres/002_create_runs_table.sql`. Columns: `run_id` (primary key), `thread_id`, `assistant_id`/`assistant_version`, `status`, `input`, `submitted_at`/`started_at`/`finished_at`, `attempt`, `cancel_requested`, `error`, `webhook`, `pending_responses`, `fork_from`, `output`, `final_waypoint_id`, `schema_version`. SQLite stores the JSON-shaped columns (`input`, `webhook`, `pending_responses`, `fork_from`, `output`) as `TEXT`; PostgreSQL stores them as `JSONB`, bound via an explicit `::jsonb` cast on write (the `payload` precedent from `001`). Two indexes: `idx_runs_thread_active`, a **partial unique index** on `runs(thread_id) WHERE status IN ('queued','running','awaiting_input')` — this IS the one-active-run-per-thread invariant (D-17), enforced by the database rather than an application check, with `AwaitingInput` deliberately included in the busy set (D-18); and `idx_runs_submitted` on `(submitted_at DESC, run_id DESC)`, serving `RunRepositoryPort::list`'s documented ordering. No `run_status_history` table (D-05): every status transition is a single compare-and-set `UPDATE runs SET status = ?to WHERE run_id = ?id AND status = ?from` (D-04) on this one row; the Waypoint chain (`GET /threads/{id}/history`, Phase 24 D-27) is already the execution audit trail. **Runs automatically, not manually,** applied on construction of `SqliteRunRepository`/`PostgresRunRepository` via `sqlx::migrate!`, exactly the `waypoints` precedent — no manual `sqlx migrate run` step, idempotent across repeated construction. **The InMemory adapter (`InMemoryRunRepository`) needs no migration** — its own write-lock check and CAS-by-equality logic are the in-process twin of the same two invariants (D-17/D-18/D-04), proven identical by the one shared contract suite (`crates/paladin-storage/src/run/contract_tests.rs`) all three adapters pass unchanged. **Reversibility:** not reversible without data loss (dropping `runs` discards all run history); no `down` migration, consistent with every existing migration in this workspace. **Storage growth:** one row per submitted run, bounded by however many runs a deployment retains — no automatic pruning in this phase. **Existing state is unaffected** — `runs` is entirely new and additive; no existing table or Citadel/Waypoint file format is touched.
- **New tables: `assistants` and `assistant_versions`. Landed Phase 27 (27-09), PLAT-04, D-28/D-29/D-30.** Two versioned migration files, one per backend, following the `waypoints`/`runs` precedent's per-backend subdirectory split: `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/sqlite/003_create_assistants_tables.sql` and `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/postgres/003_create_assistants_tables.sql`. `assistants` columns: `assistant_id` (primary key), `latest`, `source`, `created_at`, `deleted_at` (nullable — soft-delete flag), `schema_version`. `assistant_versions` columns: `assistant_id`, `version`, `kind`, `body`, `created_at`, `created_by` (nullable), `note` (nullable), `schema_version`, with **PRIMARY KEY `(assistant_id, version)`** — this IS the D-29 immutability invariant: no adapter in this tree ever executes an `UPDATE` against this table's `kind`/`body` columns (grep-verified: `grep -rc 'UPDATE assistant_versions' crates/paladin-storage/src/assistant/` is `0` in every file), there is no `update_version` method on `AssistantRepositoryPort`, and no `PUT` route is ever registered above it — immutability holds because the code to violate it does not exist. SQLite stores `body` as `TEXT` holding serialized JSON (the `runs.input` precedent from `002`); PostgreSQL stores it as `JSONB`, bound via an explicit `::jsonb` cast on write. An index, `idx_assistant_versions_assistant_version` on `(assistant_id, version)`, documents `AssistantRepositoryPort::list_versions`'s ascending-`version` access pattern alongside the primary key. **`RunRepositoryPort::insert_with_latest` (D-30)** resolves and freezes a run's `assistant_version` from `assistants.latest` inside the SAME `INSERT ... SELECT ... FROM assistants` statement that inserts the `runs` row (`crates/paladin-storage/src/run/{sqlite,postgres}.rs`) — proven under true concurrency by `assistant_version_freeze_at_submit` (20 alternating `append_version`/`insert_with_latest` calls: every resolved version exists and is `<=` the final `latest`). **Runs automatically, not manually,** applied on construction of `SqliteAssistantRepository`/`PostgresAssistantRepository` (and, transitively, `SqliteRunRepository`/`PostgresRunRepository`, since `sqlx::migrate!` embeds every file under its `migrations/` directory) via `sqlx::migrate!`, exactly the `waypoints`/`runs` precedent — no manual `sqlx migrate run` step, idempotent across repeated construction. **The InMemory adapter (`InMemoryAssistantRepository`) needs no migration** — its own write-lock-serialized `create`/`append_version` are the in-process twin of the same PK invariant, proven identical by the one shared contract suite (`crates/paladin-storage/src/assistant/contract_tests.rs`) all three adapters pass unchanged. **Reversibility:** not reversible without data loss (dropping either table discards all assistant version history); no `down` migration, consistent with every existing migration in this workspace. **Storage growth:** one `assistants` row per assistant plus one `assistant_versions` row per published version, unbounded by design (immutability means no version is ever pruned in this phase). **Existing state is unaffected** — both tables are entirely new and additive; no existing table or Citadel/Waypoint/Run file format is touched.
- **Token usage serde policy: no new table or column, landed Phase 31 (31-01…31-07), ACCT-01/ACCT-02, D-25.** Every new `usage: TokenUsage` field on a persisted type — `NodeExecutionRecord.usage` (the `waypoints.payload` JSON column), the `TraceEvent::NodeFinished`/`RunFinished` records a `TraceSink` may persist, and `PaladinResult.usage` wherever a Citadel snapshot embeds one — carries `#[serde(default)]`, so a document written before this phase deserialises cleanly with `usage == TokenUsage::default()` (all six fields zero/`None`). **No legacy-shape deserializer exists**: nothing in this tree maps the retired `token_count`/`total_tokens` key forward into `usage.total_tokens` — the old bare count is simply dropped, because serde silently ignores unknown keys on every touched type (none declares `deny_unknown_fields`). **Waypoint and persisted trace rows written before this phase report zero usage for their history.** This is acceptable because token history is not resume-critical: `resume` reconstructs execution state from the Battlefield snapshot and graph fingerprint, never from accumulated token counts, so a pre-phase Waypoint resumes identically whether its `usage` field reads zero or a real historical split. `BATTLEFIELD_SCHEMA_VERSION` is unchanged (still `1.0.0`) — this is an additive field inside the existing JSON payload, not a schema version bump, matching the precedent §9.4's Phase 25 entry above already established for `NodeExecutionRecord.attempts`/`cache_hit`. The pre-existing `execution_result.rs` legacy-JSON test was rewritten to assert exactly this contract (`usage == TokenUsage::default()` on a pre-phase document), not the old total-preserving assertion.
- **New table: `run_schedules`. Landed Phase 27 (27-11), PLAT-05, D-36/D-37/D-38/D-39.** Two versioned migration files, one per backend, following the `waypoints`/`runs`/`assistants` precedent's per-backend subdirectory split: `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/sqlite/004_create_run_schedules_table.sql` and `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/postgres/004_create_run_schedules_table.sql`. Columns: `schedule_id` (primary key), `assistant_id`, `assistant_version` (nullable), `cron`, `timezone`, `input`, `enabled`, `thread_strategy`, `on_missed`, `webhook` (nullable), `last_tick`/`next_tick` (nullable), `skipped_ticks`, `created_at`/`updated_at`, `schema_version`. SQLite stores the JSON-shaped columns (`input`, `thread_strategy`, `webhook`) as `TEXT` (the `runs.input`/`assistant_versions.body` precedent); PostgreSQL stores them as `JSONB`, bound via an explicit `::jsonb` cast on write. `on_missed` is a plain TEXT enum column (`'skip'` | `'run_once'`), not JSON — it carries no payload. One index, `idx_run_schedules_next_tick` on `(enabled, next_tick)`, serves `RunScheduleRepositoryPort::due`'s documented filter/ordering. **`RunScheduleRepositoryPort::claim_tick` (D-37) is the restart/replica-safety mechanism as a single conditional `UPDATE run_schedules SET last_tick = ?, next_tick = ? WHERE schedule_id = ? AND next_tick = ?`** — `rows_affected() == 1` means the caller won the race; `0` means it lost (a stale `expected_next`, or the row does not exist) — proven under true concurrency by `claim_tick_race_admits_exactly_one` (8 concurrent tasks racing one schedule's tick: exactly one succeeds) on all three adapters, and by the facade's own `schedule_restart_exactly_once`/`two_services_one_tick_exactly_one_fire` (`src/application/services/run/schedule/tests.rs`). **Runs automatically, not manually,** applied on construction of `SqliteRunScheduleRepository`/`PostgresRunScheduleRepository` via `sqlx::migrate!`, exactly the `waypoints`/`runs`/`assistants` precedent — no manual `sqlx migrate run` step, idempotent across repeated construction. **The InMemory adapter (`InMemoryRunScheduleRepository`) needs no migration** — its own write-lock-serialized `claim_tick` compare-and-swap is the in-process twin of the same CAS invariant, proven identical by the one shared contract suite (`crates/paladin-storage/src/run_schedule/contract_tests.rs`) all three adapters pass unchanged. **Not `tokio-cron-scheduler` (D-36):** the pre-existing `TokioCronSchedulerAdapter` (`crates/paladin-storage/src/scheduler.rs`) shares no table and is behaviourally untouched except for its own `validate_cron_field_count` now delegating its field-COUNTING primitive (not its six-field acceptance requirement) to the new, always-compiled `crates/paladin-storage/src/cron.rs` (X-03). **Reversibility:** not reversible without data loss (dropping `run_schedules` discards all schedule state, including `skipped_ticks` history); no `down` migration, consistent with every existing migration in this workspace. **Storage growth:** one row per schedule, bounded by however many schedules a deployment retains — no automatic pruning in this phase. **Existing state is unaffected** — `run_schedules` is entirely new and additive; no existing table or Citadel/Waypoint/Run/Assistant file format is touched.

- **New table: `webhook_deliveries`. Landed Phase 27 (27-13), PLAT-FR-14/15, D-40/D-41/D-42/D-43.** Two versioned migration files, one per backend, following the `waypoints`/`runs`/`assistants`/`run_schedules` precedent's per-backend subdirectory split: `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/sqlite/005_create_webhook_deliveries_table.sql` and `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/postgres/005_create_webhook_deliveries_table.sql`. Columns: `delivery_id` (primary key), `run_id`, `thread_id`, `event`, `url`, `payload` (the EXACT JSON string that is signed and sent — plain `TEXT` on BOTH backends, never `JSONB`, since it is a byte buffer to sign, not a value to query by shape, D-41), `attempt`, `max_attempts`, `status`, `next_attempt_at`, `last_response_status` (nullable), `last_error` (nullable), `created_at`/`updated_at`, `schema_version`. **No signing-key column exists on this table** (prohibition P1) — the HMAC signing value lives on the run's own `runs.webhook` column (`002`) and is read from there at send time, never copied onto a delivery row. Two indexes: `idx_webhook_deliveries_due` on `(status, next_attempt_at)`, serving `WebhookDeliveryRepositoryPort::claim_due`'s documented `Pending|Retrying` + due filter; `idx_webhook_deliveries_run` on `(run_id, created_at DESC)`, serving `list_for_run`'s documented ordering. **`WebhookDeliveryRepositoryPort::claim_due` (D-40) is the restart/durability mechanism as a per-row conditional `UPDATE webhook_deliveries SET status = 'in_flight', updated_at = ? WHERE delivery_id = ? AND status IN ('pending', 'retrying') AND next_attempt_at <= ?`** — `rows_affected() == 1` means the caller won that row's claim; `0` means a concurrent caller already took it — proven under true concurrency by `claim_due_race_admits_each_row_once` (8 tasks racing 6 due rows: the union of every claimed batch admits each row exactly once) on all three adapters. **Runs automatically, not manually,** applied on construction of `SqliteWebhookDeliveryRepository`/`PostgresWebhookDeliveryRepository` via `sqlx::migrate!`, exactly the `waypoints`/`runs`/`assistants`/`run_schedules` precedent — no manual `sqlx migrate run` step, idempotent across repeated construction. **The InMemory adapter (`InMemoryWebhookDeliveryRepository`) needs no migration** — its own write-lock-serialized `claim_due` is the in-process twin of the same CAS invariant, proven identical by the one shared 10-clause contract suite (`crates/paladin-storage/src/webhook/contract_tests.rs`) all three adapters pass unchanged. **Reversibility:** not reversible without data loss (dropping `webhook_deliveries` discards all delivery-attempt history); no `down` migration, consistent with every existing migration in this workspace. **Storage growth:** one row per enqueued delivery (one per subscribed lifecycle event per run), bounded by however many runs/webhooks a deployment retains — no automatic pruning in this phase. **Existing state is unaffected** — `webhook_deliveries` is entirely new and additive; no existing table or Citadel/Waypoint/Run/Assistant/RunSchedule file format is touched. **Delivery outcome never affects run status** — every attempt is persisted and queryable on this table independently of the `runs` row's own status column (prohibition P2).

- **New table: `run_traces`. Landed Phase 28 (28-04), OBS-02, D-17.** Two versioned migration files, one per backend, following the `waypoints`/`runs`/`assistants`/`run_schedules`/`webhook_deliveries` precedent's per-backend subdirectory split: `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/sqlite/006_create_run_traces_table.sql` and `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/postgres/006_create_run_traces_table.sql`. Columns: `thread_id`, `seq`, `run_id` (nullable), `superstep`, `at`, `schema_version`, `record` (the FULL serialized `TraceRecord` JSON — `thread_id`/`run_id`/`seq`/`at`/the `TraceEvent` payload all flattened — so `read()` deserializes a row directly with zero reconstruction from the other columns, which exist purely to serve `read()`'s `(thread_id, seq > ?)` range scan and `prune_thread()`'s `(thread_id, superstep < ?)` deletion without parsing `record` for either). **`PRIMARY KEY (thread_id, seq)`**, append-only — no adapter in this tree ever executes an `UPDATE`/`DELETE` against this table outside `prune_thread`'s own bounded deletion. SQLite stores `record` as `TEXT` (the `runs.input`/`assistant_versions.body`/`run_schedules.input` precedent); PostgreSQL stores it as `JSONB`, bound via an explicit `::jsonb` cast on write. Two indexes: `idx_run_traces_thread_seq` on `(thread_id, seq)`, serving `read`'s pagination; `idx_run_traces_thread_superstep` on `(thread_id, superstep)`, serving `prune_thread`'s bounded deletion. `superstep_of()` derives each row's pruning bucket: 5 of `TraceEvent`'s 12 variants carry a `superstep` field directly, `RunFinished` uses its own `total_supersteps`, everything else (`RunStarted`, `NodeProgress`, `EdgeEvaluated`, `ParleyRaised`, `FallbackHop`, `MiddlewareEvent`) is stamped `0` (the oldest bucket, never protected past the run's own progress) — documented on the module since `TraceRecord` itself carries no envelope-level `superstep` field. **Runs automatically, not manually,** applied on construction of `SqliteRunTraceStore`/`PostgresRunTraceStore` via `sqlx::migrate!`, exactly the `waypoints`/`runs`/`assistants`/`run_schedules`/`webhook_deliveries` precedent — no manual `sqlx migrate run` step, idempotent across repeated construction. **The InMemory adapter (`InMemoryRunTraceStore`) needs no migration** — proven identical to both real backends by the one shared contract suite (`crates/paladin-storage/src/run_trace/contract_tests.rs`) all three adapters pass unchanged. **The persistence-file format for every EXISTING table is unchanged** — `run_traces` is entirely new and additive; `waypoints`, `runs`, `assistants`/`assistant_versions`, `run_schedules`, `webhook_deliveries`, and every Citadel JSON/SQLite state file remain readable unchanged, and no existing migration file was touched. **Reversibility:** not reversible without data loss (dropping `run_traces` discards all persisted trace history — the trace is best-effort, never the durability truth; the Waypoint remains that); no `down` migration, consistent with every existing migration in this workspace. **Storage growth:** bounded by the SAME `WaypointRetentionConfig` age/count bounds already applied to Waypoints — `WaypointRetentionService::with_run_trace_port` prunes `run_traces` per thread using that thread's surviving-Waypoints' minimum superstep as the boundary; one config, one routine, two ports, no `run_traces`-specific tunable (D-17). Docker-dependent: the Postgres side of this table's own contract suite is Tier 2 (24-CONTEXT D-28), proven only by CI's `postgres-integration` job, never marked passed locally — see `.planning/phases/28-observability-tooling/28-CI-EVIDENCE.md`.

- **New columns: `runs.tenant_id` and `runs.api_key_id`. Landed Phase 40 (40-01), TENANT-02, D-09.** Two files, `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/{sqlite,postgres}/008_add_run_attribution_columns.sql`, both nullable `TEXT`, plus the non-unique `idx_runs_tenant_submitted (tenant_id, submitted_at DESC, run_id DESC)` serving the tenant-scoped keyset list. `NULL` means no principal recorded (every pre-v0.11 row, schedule-fired runs, same-process callers); role is never persisted; the ledger's own `unattributed` scope sentinel is a `treasury_ledger`-row concept and is never written to this table. Applied automatically by the embedded migrator at adapter construction, exactly the `waypoints`/`runs`/`assistants`/`run_schedules`/`webhook_deliveries`/`run_traces` precedent — no manual `sqlx migrate run` step. `002`-`007` are byte-untouched. **Reversibility:** not reversible without data loss (dropping the columns loses attribution history); no `down` migration, consistent with every existing migration in this workspace. **Storage growth:** two short nullable `TEXT` values per run. The SQLite adapter writes both columns inside the SAME `INSERT` as the rest of the row on both insert paths (`insert`/`insert_with_latest`), reads them back into `Run.submitted_by`, and rejects a half-attributed row (exactly one of the two columns `NULL`) as `RunRepositoryError::Serialization` — proven by `insert_then_get_round_trips_attribution_on_sqlite`, `insert_with_latest_persists_attribution`, `unattributed_run_is_stored_as_sql_null`, `half_attributed_row_is_a_serialization_error`. The Postgres adapter is deliberately untouched this plan (it compiles unchanged by ignoring the new nullable columns) — 40-02 owns its own attribution wiring and contract-suite coverage.

- **New constraint: `runs_attribution_all_or_none` (Postgres only). Landed Phase 40 review-fix, WR-03.** `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/postgres/009_add_run_attribution_check.sql` adds `CHECK ((tenant_id IS NULL) = (api_key_id IS NULL))` to `runs`, so a half-attributed row can never be written. It is a new file rather than an edit of `008` (migrations are append-only). SQLite has no counterpart -- it cannot add a cross-column `CHECK` via `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` -- and relies on the adapter's read-time guard (a half-attributed row reads back as `RunRepositoryError::Serialization`). Applied automatically by the embedded migrator; `ADD CONSTRAINT` validates existing rows, which are all either fully attributed or fully `NULL`.

- **New columns: `run_schedules.tenant_id` and `run_schedules.api_key_id`. Landed Phase 41 (41-05), ALLOW-02, D-08.** Two files, `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/{sqlite,postgres}/010_add_run_schedule_created_by.sql`, both nullable `TEXT`, recording the creating principal's tenant id and API key name on every schedule so fired runs are attributed to and admitted against it. Both-or-neither: PostgreSQL enforces it with the CHECK `run_schedules_created_by_all_or_none`; SQLite cannot add a cross-column CHECK through `ALTER TABLE`, so the adapter rejects a half-attributed row on read (`RunScheduleRepositoryError::Serialization`) instead. SQLite numbering skips `009` (the PostgreSQL-only `009_add_run_attribution_check.sql`), keeping the two backends aligned; `sqlx::migrate!` accepts the gap, proven by the SQLite adapter suite opening a fresh database. Applied automatically by the embedded migrator at adapter construction -- no manual step. `NULL` in both columns means no creator was recorded: every schedule created before Phase 41 keeps firing unattributed and ungated by any allowance (recorded as an open row in `.planning/WINDOWS.md`; an operator re-creates such a schedule through `POST /v1/schedules` to bring it under allowances). Role is never persisted; `PATCH /v1/schedules/{id}` never changes either column. **Reversibility:** not reversible without data loss (dropping the columns loses the creator attribution); no `down` migration, consistent with every existing migration in this workspace. **Storage growth:** two short nullable `TEXT` values per schedule.
- **New table: `treasury_notices`. Landed Phase 41 (41-06), ALLOW-04, D-16.** Two files, `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/{sqlite,postgres}/011_create_treasury_notices.sql`, recording one durable row per allowance warn crossing so that exactly one notice exists per scope kind, tenant, API key, limit kind, window start and ceiling across every replica sharing the database. Columns: `notice_id` (primary key), `scope_kind` (`tenant` or `api_key`), `tenant_id`, `api_key_id`, `limit_kind` (`window` or `lifetime`), `window_start`, `window_end` (NULL for a lifetime notice), `ceiling_nanos`, `currency`, `balance_nanos`, `warn_at` (0 to 100), `run_id` (NULL on the HTTP agent path), `recorded_at`, `schema_version`. The identity is the UNIQUE index `idx_treasury_notices_once` over `(scope_kind, tenant_id, api_key_id, limit_kind, window_start, ceiling_nanos)`; every adapter's `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (...) DO NOTHING` names exactly that column list (an arbiter test per adapter keeps the two in sync) and zero rows written is `AlreadyRecorded`, never an error. `api_key_id` is `NOT NULL` with the empty string for tenant scope and `window_start` is the Unix epoch for a lifetime notice, because a NULL in a UNIQUE key never conflicts (SQLite, and PostgreSQL before the PG15 opt-in `NULLS NOT DISTINCT`) and would let a tenant-scope notice repeat every admission. Raising a ceiling re-arms the notice for the same window; changing `warn_at` alone does not. Append-only apart from the `DELETE` of an abandoned admission's own rows (a run that was admitted but never persisted gives its notice back). A crash between the notice claim and the run insert can lose that window's notice but never duplicates it. SQLite stores instants as RFC 3339 `TEXT`, PostgreSQL as `TIMESTAMPTZ`. Applied automatically by the embedded migrator at adapter construction -- no manual step. **Reversibility:** none -- a one-way door confirmed at the 41-01 design checkpoint; reshaping the identity later needs a data migration, and there is no `down` migration, consistent with every existing migration in this workspace. **Storage growth:** one row per scope, limit, window and ceiling crossing -- at most one per allowance entry per window.
- **New index: `idx_treasury_ledger_tenant_window`. Landed Phase 41 (41-06).** Two files, `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/{sqlite,postgres}/012_add_treasury_ledger_tenant_index.sql`: `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_treasury_ledger_tenant_window ON treasury_ledger (tenant_id, attributed_at)`. Additive and idempotent; `007_create_treasury_ledger_table.sql` is byte-untouched. It serves the tenant-wide balance sum an allowance check runs (no `api_key_id` predicate), which `007`'s `(tenant_id, api_key_id, attributed_at)` index can only prefix-scan. Derived data, so dropping it loses nothing. **Reversibility:** `DROP INDEX idx_treasury_ledger_tenant_window` is lossless. **Storage growth:** one index entry per ledger row.
- **New column: `runs.halt_reason`. Landed Phase 42 (42-03), ALLOW-03, D-06.** Two files, `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/{sqlite,postgres}/013_add_run_halt_reason.sql`, adding one nullable column: `TEXT NULL` on SQLite and `JSONB NULL` on PostgreSQL (the types `runs.output` already uses), holding the serialized `HaltReason` (`{"reason":"allowance_exhausted", ...}` with the halted ceiling's figures as integer nano-units plus a currency, or `{"reason":"ledger_unavailable"}`). `NULL` for every legacy row and for every run that did not halt on spend, so a pre-013 row reads back with `halt_reason == None`. Additive only: no other table or column changes, `002`-`012` are byte-untouched, and the run schema version stays `v1`. Applied automatically by the embedded migrator at adapter construction. **Reversibility:** not reversible without data loss (dropping the column loses recorded halt reasons); no `down` migration, consistent with every existing migration in this workspace. A corrupt stored value is a typed `RunRepositoryError::Serialization` on read, never a panic.
- **New column: `treasury_notices.notice_kind`, and a rebuilt once-per-window index. Landed Phase 42 (42-10), ALLOW-03, D-18, G5.** Two files, `crates/paladin-storage/migrations/{sqlite,postgres}/014_add_treasury_notice_kind.sql`, each adding `notice_kind TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'warning' CHECK (notice_kind IN ('warning', 'halt'))` to `treasury_notices` and rebuilding `idx_treasury_notices_once` (`DROP INDEX IF EXISTS`, then `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`) with `notice_kind` appended to its column list, so a warning and a halt notice for the same scope, tenant, API key, limit kind, window and ceiling are two distinct once-per-window notices and raising the ceiling re-arms both. **Every pre-existing row reads back as a warning notice** (the column default), so no claim recorded before this migration is disturbed, and `TreasuryNoticePort::notices_for_run` returns warning rows only, so the worker's first-dispatch replay never emits a halt row as a warning. Each adapter's `NOTICE_INSERT` `ON CONFLICT (...)` arbiter list gained `notice_kind` and still textually matches the rebuilt index (the `notice_arbiter_matches_the_migration` tests). `011` and every other earlier migration are byte-untouched, and nothing claimed a halt notice until the boundary guard was wired in plan 42-11 (§9.6). Applied automatically by the embedded migrator at adapter construction. **Reversibility:** not reversible without data loss (a down-migration would drop the column and rebuild the old index, losing every halt notice); no `down` migration, consistent with every existing migration in this workspace. **Rollout caveat:** a replica still running a pre-014 binary against a migrated database cannot infer the rebuilt index from its own `ON CONFLICT (...)` arbiter list, so its notice claim returns a store error that it logs and skips (a notice observes the run, it never gates it, Phase 41 D-15); migrate and upgrade the replicas together so no warn notice is lost during the window.
- **`run_traces` gains an `allowance_warning` record kind. Landed Phase 41 (41-07), ALLOW-04, D-18 -- rollout note (RESEARCH Pitfall 12).** No schema change: a run that won a warn-threshold notice persists one extra `run_traces` row whose flattened `kind` is `allowance_warning` (the run's first `seq`, before `RunStarted`), through the existing `PersistingTraceSink`. A replica still running a build that predates this variant reads that row as an unknown `kind`, and only for runs that carry a notice -- every other run's trace is unchanged. **Upgrade every replica before setting `warn_at` on an allowance entry** (the operator webhook, 41-08, repeats this guidance). The persisted row carries the figures, the window and the threshold only -- never a tenant id or an API key name.
- **Stored `BattalionResult` JSON (Phase 44, LEGACY-01, D-06).** `BattalionResult.node_errors` changed element type (section 9.1, M-B-08), so a stored v0.10 `BattalionResult` document whose `node_errors` array is non-empty no longer deserializes into the v0.11 type: its elements carry `{node_name, error}` where the type now requires `{node_id, attempt, transience, source}`. A document whose `node_errors` is empty or absent still reads (`#[serde(default)]` is kept). No schema migration, migration job or compatibility reader exists, by design (D-00c, ADR-0059): read old documents as untyped `serde_json::Value` or regenerate them. No SQL table or migration file is touched; the field lives only inside JSON a caller serialized and stored themselves.
- **Stored entity JSON and Citadel snapshots (Phase 48, ENTITY-01, D-03/D-10).** Only key names changed (section 9.1, M-B-12): the entity payload `node` became `data`; `ContentItem` and `PromptItem` store their entity under `entity` instead of `node`; a version record stores `entity_id` and `entity_data` instead of `node_id` and `node_data`. Field order, field types and every other key are unchanged, and no `schema_version` was added. A document written by v0.10 still reads, through serde read-aliases that stay for the whole 0.x line. A document written by v0.11 cannot be read by v0.10. The Citadel now stores the real `PaladinData` (the same 13 fields as the placeholder it replaces, so a v0.10 snapshot loads) and the real `PaladinStatus`, which adds `Reasoning`: a v0.11 snapshot taken while a Paladin is `Reasoning` is unreadable by v0.10, and every v0.10 status string still loads. The places affected are the Citadel snapshot files and any store, cache or queue a caller fills with this JSON. No SQL table or migration file in this workspace stores entity JSON: the SQLite and MySQL content repositories store columns (the content body is the `entity.data.content` value) and rebuild the entity on read, and the user repository is likewise column-based. There is no migration job and no `schema_version` bump, by design (ADR-0060): old documents are read in place and rewritten with the new keys the next time they are saved. **Rolling upgrade:** upgrade readers, and any queue consumer that deserializes a `ContentItem` or other entity from a queue payload, before any writer that produces the new keys (a deployment that carries no such payload over a queue is unaffected; whether any does is not known in-tree). **Back up first, and downgrade only by restoring the backup:** a v0.10 binary started against files or payloads that v0.11 wrote fails to read them, and a Citadel snapshot in the `Reasoning` status is the case a partial rollback is most likely to hit.

## 9.5 Configuration & environment

Every new runtime behavior this phase introduces is tunable and **disabled or defaulted to today's behavior out of the box** (X-09), so a v0.9 configuration file boots v0.10 with identical behavior. This claim is proven, not merely stated: the root integration test target `v0_9_config_boot` (`tests/integration/v0_9_config_boot_test.rs`, `cargo test --features web-server --test v0_9_config_boot`, gated on every PR by CI's `e2e-platform-api` job — see §9.6) asserts it at two levels. **Config resolution:** the frozen v0.9.0 fixture loads with `Settings::load_from_file` with no edits, `Settings::agent_runtime`/`trace`/`web_server` all resolve to their own `Default`, and each of the nine platform config structs the v0.9 config file never named as a field (`EngineConfig`, `WaypointStoreConfig`, `RunStoreConfig`, `RunQueueConfig`, `RunWorkerConfig`, `RunStreamConfig`, `AssistantsConfig`, `SchedulesConfig`, `WebhooksConfig`) reports its own inert `::default()` state and validates `Ok`, with `apply_env_overrides()` proven a no-op against every relevant `APP_*` variable cleared under `#[serial]` (`EngineConfig::graceful_shutdown` is the one deliberate exception — it defaults `true`, M-B-02's own recorded decision above, and the test asserts that value, not `false`). **Behavioral:** the composed real routers answer `200` for the six v0.9 `/v1/agents…` paths plus `/health`, `/ready` and `/openapi.json`, and `501` — never `404` — for every route family new in v0.10 (`/v1/runs`, `/v1/threads/*/history`, `/v1/assistants`, `/v1/schedules`); `501`, not `404`, is the documented answer for a disabled platform subsystem everywhere in this file, cross-referenced against §9.6's own response-code tables below. **Fixture note:** the file the test actually loads is the frozen `v0.9.0` `tests/fixtures/config/v0.9.0-config.test.yml`, not `config.example.yml` — a byte-identical frozen `v0.9.0-config.example.yml` is committed alongside it as documentation evidence only, because it does not parse at either the `v0.9.0` tag or HEAD (`LlmProviderConfig::api_key` is a required `String` and its `ollama:` block has never carried one — a pre-existing defect unrelated to this program, recorded in `tests/fixtures/config/README.md`).

Config structs mirror the existing `CitadelConfig` shape at `src/config/citadel.rs`: `Default` + `validate()` + `EnvOverridable`.

- **`EngineConfig`** (`src/config/engine.rs`) — **landed this plan (23-07, Task 1)**:
  - `max_supersteps: u64` — default `50`. Env: `APP_ENGINE_MAX_SUPERSTEPS`.
  - `max_node_visits: u32` — default `25`. Env: `APP_ENGINE_MAX_NODE_VISITS`.
  - `run_timeout_secs: Option<u64>` — default `None` (no run-level bound). Env: `APP_ENGINE_RUN_TIMEOUT_SECS`. **Landed Phase 25 (FT-03, D-20, Plan 25-09)** — declared as plumbing in 23-07, the semantics now carry: `Some(n)` becomes `EngineLimits.run_timeout = Duration::from_secs(n)` (pinned by `config_seconds_convert_to_duration_exactly`), a wall-clock budget for the **whole** run measured from the start of each `WarEngine::start`/`resume` call (a resume restarts it; a `Battalion` child measures its own budget against its own limits). Exceeding it ends the run with the typed `EngineError::RunTimeoutExceeded { elapsed, limit }` through the same `Failed`-Waypoint path `RecursionLimitExceeded`/`NodeVisitLimitExceeded` take. It nests *outside* every per-node `TimeoutPolicy`: an attempt's effective deadline is `min(its run_timeout, the remaining run budget)`, the fired `TimeoutKind` names whichever was tightest, and an attempt the engine budget cuts records `Timeout(EngineRun)` — which is never retried. Never hashed into the graph fingerprint.
  - `waypoint_durability: WaypointDurability` (`Strict` | `BestEffort`) — default `Strict`. Env: `APP_ENGINE_WAYPOINT_DURABILITY`.
  - `max_muster_tasks: u32` — default `100` (CF-FR-13, D-16). Env: `APP_ENGINE_MAX_MUSTER_TASKS`. `validate()` rejects `0`.
  - `shutdown_grace_secs: u64` — default `30` (HITL-04, D-20). Env: `APP_ENGINE_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_SECS`. Seconds a mid-superstep cancellation races the in-flight batch of spawned node tasks against before aborting stragglers; also the upper bound `paladin-server`/`ServiceRunner` wait on SIGTERM/SIGINT before exiting. `validate()` rejects values above `3600` (1 hour). **Landed this plan (24-08).**
  - `graceful_shutdown: bool` — default `true` (HITL-04, D-20). Env: `APP_ENGINE_GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN`. Set `false` to skip the shutdown-grace wait entirely and exit immediately on SIGTERM/SIGINT — the M-B-02 disable switch for legacy-only deployments (§9.1). **Landed this plan (24-08).**
  - Converts into `EngineLimits`/`WaypointDurability` via `impl From<EngineConfig> for EngineLimits` (`src/config/engine.rs`); `waypoint_durability` is read directly off the source `EngineConfig` value and passed to `WarEngine::with_durability` separately. `shutdown_grace_secs`/`graceful_shutdown` are deliberately excluded from this conversion — both are runtime-only settings, never hashed into `WarGraph::fingerprint()` (proven by `shutdown_grace_does_not_change_the_graph_fingerprint`).
  - Both new fields default to today's absent behavior (an unconfigured v0.9 deployment gets `shutdown_grace_secs=30`, `graceful_shutdown=true` — the same values a v0.10 deployment that never mentions either env var gets), so upgrading changes nothing about a workflow's own behavior; it only changes what the *process* does on SIGTERM/SIGINT (see M-B-02, §9.1).
- **`WaypointRetentionConfig`** (`src/config/waypoint_retention.rs`) — **landed this plan (22-06, Task 3)**:
  - `enabled: bool` — default `false` (no pruning runs until an operator opts in — the new subsystem is off by default). Env: `APP_WAYPOINT_RETENTION_ENABLED`.
  - `max_age_days: Option<u32>` — default `None`. Env: `APP_WAYPOINT_RETENTION_MAX_AGE_DAYS`. `validate()` rejects `Some(0)`.
  - `max_waypoints_per_thread: Option<u32>` — default `None`. Env: `APP_WAYPOINT_RETENTION_MAX_WAYPOINTS_PER_THREAD`. `validate()` rejects `Some(0)`.
  - This config is driven through `WaypointRetentionService` (`src/application/services/waypoint_retention.rs`, Plan 22-14), which holds the one definition of "protected" — a thread's latest Waypoint plus every `AwaitingInput` Waypoint (ENG-FR-18, T-22-21) — and hands it to `paladin_storage::waypoint::retention::prune` as an argument. The routine itself no longer decides what protected means; it unions whatever it is handed with the configured bounds and removes the rest through `WaypointPort::prune_thread` (Plan 22-13), a keep-set primitive that never touches a protected id — not the delete-then-resave sequence this section originally described. Monotone, crash-safe, and idempotent by construction; runs unchanged over `InMemoryWaypointStore`, `SqliteWaypointStore`, and `PostgresWaypointStore`.

Since every new field either defaults to today's absent behavior (`enabled: false`, no retention pruning) or to a bounded-but-generous limit that only applies to the *new* `WarEngine` path (legacy Formation/Phalanx/Campaign call sites never construct an `EngineConfig`), a v0.9 configuration file — which has no `engine:`/`waypoint_retention:` section at all — resolves to identical runtime behavior: `EngineConfig::default()` converts to exactly `EngineLimits::default()` and `WaypointDurability::Strict`, asserted mechanically by `config::engine::tests::default_engine_config_matches_todays_engine_defaults` rather than left as an unchecked claim.

- **`LlmDecision` and Commander `StrategySelection::Semantic` add no config struct or environment variable (CF-05, D-26).** Both hold `Arc<dyn LlmPort>` trait objects assembled and passed in code — an `LlmDecisionEvaluator` registered by name through `EdgeEvaluatorRegistry`; a `StrategySelection::Semantic { llm, model }` passed to `CommanderBuilder::strategy_selection` — never resolved through an `APP_*` environment variable or a config-file section, so a v0.9 deployment gains no new outbound, paid, non-deterministic model call merely by upgrading (`grep -rn 'APP_LLM_DECISION\|APP_.*SEMANTIC' src/ crates/` returns no matches). `on_ambiguous` (per-`LlmDecisionEvaluator`) and `on_parse_error` (per-node `DirectiveParser`, CF-02) are likewise per-evaluator/per-node enums set in code, not runtime-global tunables — X-09 requires a config struct only for a *tunable*, and neither of these is one.

- **`NodeCacheConfig`** (`src/config/node_cache.rs`) — **landed Phase 25 (FT-06, D-29, Plan 25-04)**, mirroring `WaypointStoreConfig`'s shape (`Default` + `validate()` + `EnvOverridable`). **Off by default**: a v0.9 configuration file, which has no `node_cache:` section, resolves to `enabled: false` and no cache is wired, so no upgraded deployment gains new caching behavior.
  - `enabled: bool` — default `false`. Env: `APP_NODE_CACHE_ENABLED`.
  - `backend: NodeCacheBackend` (`in_memory` | `redis`) — default `in_memory`. Env: `APP_NODE_CACHE_BACKEND`. `redis` additionally requires the `redis-cache` cargo feature (§9.3).
  - `redis_host: String` — default `localhost`. Env: `APP_NODE_CACHE_REDIS_HOST`. `validate()` rejects an empty host when `backend` is `redis`.
  - `redis_port: u16` — default `6379`. Env: `APP_NODE_CACHE_REDIS_PORT`. `validate()` rejects `0`.
  - `redis_password: Option<String>` — default `None`. Env: `APP_NODE_CACHE_REDIS_PASSWORD`. **Never rendered by the struct's hand-written `Debug` impl** (a fixed placeholder is printed instead — T-25-18, `debug_rendering_never_prints_the_password`).
  - `redis_db: u8` — default `0`. Env: `APP_NODE_CACHE_REDIS_DB`.
  - `key_prefix: String` — default `paladin:node_cache`. Env: `APP_NODE_CACHE_KEY_PREFIX`. `validate()` rejects an empty prefix. Every entry the Redis backend writes is namespaced under it.
  - This config selects the cache **backend** only. It resolves into a `RedisNodeCacheConfig` (or an `InMemoryNodeCache`) handed to `WarEngine::with_node_cache`; which nodes are cached, for how long and under which key is a per-node `CachePolicy` in code (below).
- **Aegis policies add no config struct and no environment variable (FT-01…FT-06, D-29; the Phase 23 D-26 rule for per-node enums).** `Aegis { retry, timeout, on_error, cache }` — `RetryPolicy`, `RetryPredicate`, `TimeoutPolicy`, `ErrorHandlerSpec`, `CachePolicy`/`CacheKeySpec` — is attached per node in code through `WarGraph::set_aegis`/`with_default_aegis`, and `RetryPredicate::Custom`/`ErrorHandlerSpec::Custom` names resolve through registries populated by `WarEngine::with_retry_predicate`/`with_error_handler`, exactly like `with_edge_evaluator`. No `APP_AEGIS_*`, `APP_ENGINE_RETRY_*` or `APP_ENGINE_ON_ERROR_*` variable exists (`grep -rn 'APP_AEGIS\|APP_ENGINE_RETRY\|APP_ENGINE_ON_ERROR' src/ crates/` returns no matches). A v0.9 graph declares no `Aegis`, so every node in it keeps one attempt, no bound, no handler and no cache — byte-identical to before (see §9.1).
- **`AgentRuntimeConfig`** (`src/config/agent_runtime.rs`) — **landed Phase 26 (RT-01…RT-07, D-10), one grouped config carrying every built-in middleware's own sub-struct.** Mirrors `NodeCacheConfig`'s shape (`Default` + `validate()` + `EnvOverridable`); every scalar field is overridable through `APP_AGENT_RUNTIME_<SECTION>_<FIELD>`, while regex rules, per-tool maps and model tables are config-file only (no env var can populate a `Vec`/`HashMap`-typed field). **Every one of the twelve sub-structs is inert by default** — proven mechanically by `default_agent_runtime_config_is_inert` (plan 26-02) and, downstream, `build_chain_on_a_default_config_returns_an_empty_chain` (plan 26-20): a v0.9 `config.yml`, which has no `agent_runtime:` section at all, resolves to every section below at its default and `AgentRuntimeConfig::build_chain` returning an empty `Vec`, so an upgraded deployment gains no new middleware behavior merely by upgrading.

  | Sub-struct | Default | Key env vars |
  |---|---|---|
  | `model_call_limit: ModelCallLimitConfig` | `enabled: false`, `max_calls: 10` | `APP_AGENT_RUNTIME_MODEL_CALL_LIMIT_ENABLED`, `_MAX_CALLS` |
  | `token_budget: TokenBudgetConfig` | `enabled: false`, `max_tokens: 100_000` | `APP_AGENT_RUNTIME_TOKEN_BUDGET_ENABLED`, `_MAX_TOKENS` |
  | `tool_call_limit: ToolCallLimitConfig` | `enabled: false`; `per_tool` config-file only | `APP_AGENT_RUNTIME_TOOL_CALL_LIMIT_ENABLED`, `_MAX_CALLS` |
  | `guardrail: GuardrailConfig` | `enabled: false`, `rules: []`, `pattern_size_limit_bytes: 65536` (`1 << 16`); `rules` config-file only | `APP_AGENT_RUNTIME_GUARDRAIL_ENABLED`, `_PATTERN_SIZE_LIMIT_BYTES` |
  | `history_trimmer: HistoryTrimmerConfig` | `enabled: false`; `model_context_limits` config-file only | `APP_AGENT_RUNTIME_HISTORY_TRIMMER_ENABLED`, `_RESERVE_FOR_RESPONSE`, `_DEFAULT_CONTEXT_TOKENS`, `_RECALL_LIMIT` |
  | `summarization: SummarizationConfig` | `enabled: false` | `APP_AGENT_RUNTIME_SUMMARIZATION_ENABLED`, `_THRESHOLD_TOKENS`, `_THRESHOLD_MESSAGES`, `_KEEP_RECENT`, `_SUMMARIZER_MODEL` |
  | `vault_recall: VaultRecallConfig` | `enabled: false` | `APP_AGENT_RUNTIME_VAULT_RECALL_ENABLED`, `_TOP_K`, `_SCORE_FLOOR` |
  | `model_retry: ModelRetryConfig` | `enabled: false`, mirrors `RetryPolicy::default()` | `APP_AGENT_RUNTIME_MODEL_RETRY_ENABLED`, `_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, `_INITIAL_INTERVAL_MS`, `_BACKOFF_FACTOR`, `_MAX_INTERVAL_MS`, `_JITTER`, `_RETRY_ON` |
  | `model_fallback: ModelFallbackConfig` | `enabled: false`; `providers` config-file only, never a credential | `APP_AGENT_RUNTIME_MODEL_FALLBACK_ENABLED` |
  | `tool_errors: ToolErrorConfig` | `mode: FeedToModel` (D-33 — a today's-behavior *policy* default, not `enabled: false`; `per_tool` config-file only) | `APP_AGENT_RUNTIME_TOOL_ERRORS_MODE` |
  | `structured: StructuredOutputConfig` | `max_repair_attempts: 1` | `APP_AGENT_RUNTIME_STRUCTURED_MAX_REPAIR_ATTEMPTS` |
  | `vault_tools: VaultToolsConfig` | `enabled: false` | `APP_AGENT_RUNTIME_VAULT_TOOLS_ENABLED` |

  No field on any of the twelve sub-structs is secret-shaped: `ModelFallbackConfig.providers` names providers by string only, credentials still resolve through the existing `LlmProviderFactory` env/config path. `AgentRuntimeConfig::build_chain(&self, deps: AgentRuntimeDeps) -> Result<Vec<Arc<dyn ExecutionMiddleware>>, ..>` assembles every enabled section in a fixed order (limits → guardrail → trimmer/summarizer → recall → protocol → resilience) and is what an operator or `paladin-server`'s agent provisioning calls to turn this one config block into an installed chain.

- **Platform API config structs (PLAT-01..06, D-50, X-09) — landed Phase 27 (plans 27-06, 27-17).** Seven new structs in `src/config/`, each mirroring `WaypointStoreConfig`'s shape (`Default` + `validate()` + `EnvOverridable`, no `Settings` field — read directly by `src/bin/paladin-server.rs::run()` via `Default::default()` + `apply_env_overrides()`, exactly like `WaypointStoreConfig` itself). **Six of the seven default to OFF or today's-behaviour**; `AssistantsConfig.expose_code_registry` is the one deliberate exception, defaulting `true` (D-32) because it only renders read-only data already discoverable through the pre-existing `AgentRegistry`. A v0.9 `config.yml` (no section for any of these — they are never part of this file's own YAML schema) boots this v0.10 binary with the run server fully disabled: `src/bin/paladin-server.rs::build_run_api` returns an unwired `RunApiState` and spawns no background task, and every `/v1/runs*`, `/v1/assistants*`, `/v1/schedules*` route answers `501 not_implemented` naming the config key to set (D-44).

  | Struct | File | Default | Key env vars |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `RunStoreConfig`/`RunStoreBackend` | `src/config/run_store.rs` | `backend: disabled` | `APP_RUN_STORE_BACKEND` (`disabled`\|`sqlite`\|`postgres`), `APP_RUN_STORE_PATH` (sqlite), `APP_RUN_STORE_URL_ENV` (postgres — names the env var holding the connection url, never the url itself) |
  | `RunQueueConfig`/`RunQueueBackend` | `src/config/run_queue.rs` | `backend: in_memory` | `APP_RUN_QUEUE_BACKEND` (`in_memory`\|`redis`), `APP_RUN_QUEUE_URL_ENV`, `APP_RUN_QUEUE_KEY_PREFIX` (default `paladin:run_queue`) |
  | `RunWorkerConfig` | `src/config/run_worker.rs` | `concurrency: 4`, `lease_seconds: 60`, `min_probe_interval_ms: 1000` | `APP_RUN_WORKER_CONCURRENCY`, `APP_RUN_WORKER_LEASE_SECONDS`, `APP_RUN_WORKER_MIN_PROBE_INTERVAL_MS`. Deliberately no `heartbeat` field (D-10) — the worker derives `lease_seconds / 4` at runtime; `validate()` enforces `lease_seconds >= 4`. |
  | `RunStreamConfig` | `src/config/run_stream.rs` | `poll_interval_ms: 1000` | `APP_RUN_STREAM_POLL_INTERVAL_MS` — the degraded-mode `GET /runs/{id}/stream` polling interval (D-26). |
  | `AssistantsConfig` | `src/config/assistants.rs` | `expose_code_registry: true` (**the one ON-by-default exception**, D-32) | `APP_ASSISTANTS_EXPOSE_CODE_REGISTRY` |
  | `SchedulesConfig` | `src/config/schedules.rs` | `enabled: false`, `tick_interval_ms: 1000` | `APP_SCHEDULES_ENABLED`, `APP_SCHEDULES_TICK_INTERVAL_MS` |
  | `WebhooksConfig` | `src/config/webhooks.rs` | `allow_private: false`, `max_attempts: 5`, `timeout_secs: 10` | `APP_WEBHOOKS_ALLOW_PRIVATE` (the **only** override of the write-time/send-time SSRF guard, D-42), `APP_WEBHOOKS_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, `APP_WEBHOOKS_TIMEOUT_SECS` |

  `src/bin/paladin-server.rs::build_run_api` (plan 27-17) is the sole production reader: it loads all seven, converts `RunWorkerConfig`/`WebhooksConfig`/`SchedulesConfig` into `RunWorkerOptions`/`WebhookDeliveryOptions`/`ScheduleServiceOptions` (`impl From<&…Config>` kept in `src/infrastructure/web/run_api_wiring.rs`, never in `src/config/`, per this phase's own convention), and fails closed — naming the missing cargo feature — if `run_store` names `postgres`/`run_queue` names `redis` on a binary built without `storage-postgres`/`redis-queue` (§9.3), or if `run_store` is enabled with no `waypoint_store` backend wired (naming `waypoint_store.backend`).

- **`TraceConfig`** (`src/config/trace.rs`) — **landed Phase 28 (28-02), OBS-01/OBS-02, D-36.** Mirrors `RunStreamConfig`'s `Default` + `validate()` + `EnvOverridable` shape, nested under `Settings.trace` (`#[serde(default)]` — an absent `trace:` section resolves to every field below at its default, so a v0.9 configuration file boots this v0.10 binary with a fully inert trace pipeline apart from the default-on log sink, which itself is a facade composition decision, not a behavior change to any workflow).
  - `log_sink: bool` — default `true` (the trace stream's ONE on-by-default consumer; `RUST_LOG=paladin::trace=off` silences it without touching this config). Env: `PALADIN_TRACE_LOG_SINK`.
  - `channel_capacity: usize` — default `1024`. Env: `PALADIN_TRACE_CHANNEL_CAPACITY`. `validate()` rejects `0`.
  - `persist: bool` — default `false` (a new subsystem, `run_traces` persistence, stays off by default). Env: `PALADIN_TRACE_PERSIST`.
  - `state_values: bool` — default `false` (no `DeltaMerged` field value ever reaches a trace record, the SSE wire, or a persisted row unless explicitly opted in, D-05). Env: `PALADIN_TRACE_STATE_VALUES`.
  - `value_cap_bytes: usize` — default `256`. Env: `PALADIN_TRACE_VALUE_CAP_BYTES`. `validate()` rejects `0`.
  - `heartbeat_interval_secs: u64` — default `5`. Env: `PALADIN_TRACE_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_SECS`. **Not yet wired into the engine's own rate limiter** — the engine hardcodes its own 5-second default independently; threading this configured value through is a documented, deliberate scope reduction (28-06), not a behavior change (the two values happen to agree today).
  - `otel: OtelConfig { enabled, endpoint, headers, service_name }` — `enabled: bool` default `false`. Env: `PALADIN_TRACE_OTEL_ENABLED`, `PALADIN_TRACE_OTEL_ENDPOINT`, `PALADIN_TRACE_OTEL_SERVICE_NAME` (`headers`, a map, is config-file only — no env var can populate it). `validate()` rejects a non-`http(s)` `endpoint` when `enabled`, and rejects `enabled: true` on a build without the `otel` Cargo feature compiled in (`TraceConfigError::FeatureNotCompiled`, a typed error, never a silent no-op). `OtelConfig` has a **manual** `Debug` impl that redacts every `headers` value — never derived — so a header-shaped credential never reaches a log line or an error body.
  - **Deliberately diverges from the `APP_*` prefix every other config struct in this section uses** — `PALADIN_TRACE_*`, not `APP_TRACE_*` — a locked naming choice recorded in `trace.rs`'s own module docs.
  - A v0.9 configuration file (no `trace:` section at all) resolves to every field above at its default: the log sink is on (the only behavior-visible default), nothing is persisted, no state value is ever captured, and OTLP export is off.
- **`web_server.dev_ui.mermaid_url`** (`src/config/web_server.rs`, `WebServerConfig`/`DevUiConfig`, new types) — **landed Phase 28 (28-02), OBS-03, D-25/D-26.** Default: the jsDelivr `mermaid@11` ESM bundle CDN URL. Env: `APP_WEB_SERVER_DEV_UI_MERMAID_URL` (this one struct DOES use the `APP_*` prefix — it is not part of the `trace:` section's deliberate divergence). Purely a client-side asset URL the `dev-ui` inspector page's browser-side JS imports Mermaid from; an air-gapped operator repoints it at a local mirror (see the graph-visualization user guide). A v0.9 configuration file (no `web_server:` section) resolves to the jsDelivr default, identical to today's absence of any inspector page at all (the route itself is additionally feature-gated off by default, D-25).
- **`PALADIN_EVAL_LIVE`** — **landed Phase 28 (28-12), OBS-04, D-35.** Not a `Settings`-resolved config field — a bare environment variable `paladin-eval`'s `check_live_mode` (and, through it, `paladin-cli eval run --live`) reads directly. One of THREE conditions live mode requires simultaneously (the `--live` flag, this environment variable set to a non-blank value, and a configured provider credential per ADR-0012) — missing any one refuses with a typed `LiveModeError` naming which is missing, never silently falling back to scripted mocks. Absent (the default for every CI run), every eval scenario runs against `ScenarioLlm`'s scripted mocks — no behavior change to any deployment that never sets it.

- **`treasurer.allowance` -- landed Phase 41 (41-01, 41-03), ALLOW-01, D-02/D-11/D-15.** An optional subtree of `treasurer:`; omitting it is inert (X-09), so a v0.9/v0.10 config boots unchanged.
  - `treasurer.allowance.warn_at` (integer percent `0..=100`, default `80`, `0` disables; `101` is rejected, never clamped). Env: `APP_TREASURER_ALLOWANCE_WARN_AT`.
  - `treasurer.allowance.webhook.url` (non-empty `http(s)` URL, scheme and address checked by the SSRF guard at wiring and send time) and `treasurer.allowance.webhook.secret` (never rendered by `Debug`, never serialised, never echoed in an error). Env: `APP_TREASURER_ALLOWANCE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, applied only when a `webhook` entry exists -- a `${VAR}` placeholder in the YAML file is NOT expanded by the config loader and would load literally.
  - `treasurer.allowance.tenants.<id>` and `treasurer.allowance.api_keys.<name>`: entries with required `period` (`<integer><m|h|d>`, `1m` to `366d`) and `amount` (decimal string, whole currency units, positive, at most nine decimal places, no float arithmetic), plus optional `lifetime` (same grammar) and `warn_at`. A lifetime-only entry is not expressible. Both maps are config-file only (no env form).
  - **Stricter loading of an unreleased section:** `TreasurerConfig`, `AllowanceConfig`, `AllowanceEntryConfig` and `AllowanceWebhookConfig` reject unknown keys (`deny_unknown_fields`), so a typo such as `allowence:` or `perid:` fails the load instead of enforcing nothing. `treasurer:` shipped no published release with the allowance keys, so no published config breaks.
  - **Boot coherence (D-11):** `paladin-server` stops, naming the offending path, when `treasurer.allowance` has entries while `run_store.backend` is `disabled`, when an `api_keys.<name>` entry names no key in `http.auth.api_keys`, or when a `tenants.<id>` entry names a tenant no `http.auth.api_keys` entry or `http.auth.bearer_token.tenant` maps to. With `http.auth.enabled: false`, `api_keys.anonymous` and `tenants.open-access` are accepted.
- **`treasurer.cadence` -- landed Phase 43 (43-01), PACE-02, D-08/D-10. On by default, unlike the other opt-in subtrees of this section.** The subtree configures rate pacing (the Cadence): a gate per (provider, model) that opens a back-off window after a provider 429. `TreasurerConfig`'s `cadence` field is `#[serde(default)]`, so a file without it boots with pacing on at the defaults below; `CadenceConfig` rejects unknown keys (`deny_unknown_fields`), so a typo fails the load, and `validate()` names the offending `treasurer.cadence.*` key.
  - `treasurer.cadence.enabled` (bool, default `true`): `false` installs no pacing at all and restores the previous behaviour.
  - `treasurer.cadence.backend` (default `in_process`; plan 43-09 adds `{ redis: { url_env: NAME } }`): where the gate state lives. The `redis` form names the environment variable that holds the Redis URL -- never the URL, which may carry a password -- and `validate()` rejects an empty name or one naming a variable that is not set, naming `treasurer.cadence.backend.redis.url_env` and the variable (never its value). It needs a binary built with `redis-cadence`; without it `paladin-server` refuses to start naming the feature, and an unreachable Redis never blocks boot (the connection is lazy and the first failure degrades to the in-process fallback). All Redis keys sit under the fixed prefix `paladin:cadence`, so two independent fleets on one Redis server share pacing state: give each its own server or logical database. `backend` has no environment override.
  - `treasurer.cadence.base_backoff_ms` (default `500`) and `treasurer.cadence.max_backoff_ms` (default `30000`): the first and the largest delay-less gate; `base_backoff_ms` above `max_backoff_ms` is rejected.
  - `treasurer.cadence.max_wait_secs` (default `300`): the longest a single call waits on a gate before surfacing a rate limit.
  - `treasurer.cadence.degraded_multiplier` (default `2.0`, finite and at least `1.0`): the factor applied to delays while a shared backend is degraded (plan 43-08); it has no effect with `in_process`.
  - `treasurer.cadence.fallback_pace_budget_secs` (default `60`): how long a fallback hop is paced on a 429 before the chain hops to the next provider (plan 43-06); `0` is rejected, so a chain always has a budget. A 5xx, timeout or network error never waits on it.
  - `treasurer.cadence.lock_ttl_secs` (default `120`): the TTL a library caller passes to `WarEngine::with_cadence` for the cache stampede lock (plan 43-11); it should exceed the p99 node duration including retries. Validated at boot; `paladin-server` attaches no node cache, so it attaches no lock.
  - Environment overrides (plan 43-09, scalar keys only): `APP_TREASURER_CADENCE_ENABLED`, `APP_TREASURER_CADENCE_BASE_BACKOFF_MS`, `APP_TREASURER_CADENCE_MAX_BACKOFF_MS`, `APP_TREASURER_CADENCE_MAX_WAIT_SECS`, `APP_TREASURER_CADENCE_DEGRADED_MULTIPLIER`, `APP_TREASURER_CADENCE_FALLBACK_PACE_BUDGET_SECS` and `APP_TREASURER_CADENCE_LOCK_TTL_SECS` each override their field when set and parseable; an unparseable value leaves the field unchanged.
  - Every duration must be greater than zero.
- **`http.auth.api_keys[].tenant` and `http.auth.bearer_token.tenant` — landed Phase 40 (40-01), TENANT-01, D-03/D-05. The one deliberate X-09 exception of this phase: both are REQUIRED, not defaulted to today's behavior.**
  - `http.auth.api_keys[].tenant` (`ApiKeyConfig.tenant: String`, `#[serde(default)]` so the file still parses) is **required** — there is no implicit default tenant. A v0.9/v0.10 config whose `http.auth.api_keys` entries carry no `tenant` stops `paladin-server` at boot with `http.auth.api_keys[<name>]: 'tenant' is required — every API key must map to a tenant (Phase 40, TENANT-01)`, naming the key's `name`, never its secret `key` value. A malformed (non-empty but invalid, e.g. containing whitespace) value stops boot with `http.auth.api_keys[<name>]: 'tenant' is not a valid tenant id: <TenantIdError>`. To upgrade: add a `tenant:` line to every `api_keys` entry (a plain identifier — non-empty, no whitespace, printable ASCII, at most 128 bytes). A config with no `api_keys` at all is unaffected (the frozen `v0_9_config_boot` fixture has none, so that proof still passes unchanged).
  - **Extended, Phase 40 (40-03), TENANT-01:** the same boot check (`AuthConfig::validate`, called by `paladin-server`'s `build_auth_config`) now also rejects, in this order per entry: an empty key value — `http.auth.api_keys[#<index>]: 'key' must not be empty` (0-based list position, used because the name is not yet known to be printable); an invalid `name` — `http.auth.api_keys[#<index>]: 'name' is not a valid API key id: <error>` (the same identifier rule as a tenant id); a duplicate `name` — `http.auth.api_keys[<name>]: duplicate 'name' — every API key name must be unique`; and a duplicate `key` value — `http.auth.api_keys[<name>]: duplicate 'key' — the same secret is already configured for '<first name>'`, which names both entries by `name` and NEVER prints the shared secret. A v0.10 config that happened to carry two keys with the same name or the same value therefore stops at boot instead of silently letting the later entry shadow the earlier one; fix the duplicate, do not remove `tenant`. The `paladin-cli` onboarding template needed no change.
  - `http.auth.bearer_token.tenant` (`BearerTokenAuthConfig.tenant: Option<String>`) is **required when `bearer_token.enabled` is true** (D-03) — every verified bearer principal must carry a tenant. A verified bearer token while this is unset does NOT authenticate (401, fail closed, `verified_bearer_token_without_configured_tenant_fails_closed`); `paladin-server` itself refuses to boot with `http.auth.bearer_token: 'tenant' is required when bearer_token.enabled is true — bearer-token principals must map to a tenant (Phase 40, TENANT-01)` when `enabled: true` carries no `tenant`. `bearer_token.enabled: false` (today's default) is entirely unaffected.
  - **Why this is X-09's one deliberate exception:** every other config addition in this document defaults to today's inert behavior; this pair cannot, because TENANT-01/D-05 forbid an implicit default tenant — silently defaulting every key to one shared tenant would pool every caller's runs and spend into a single scope, precisely the cross-tenant leak Phase 40 exists to close. The cost is paid once, at upgrade time, by adding one `tenant:` line per configured key/bearer section.
- **`llm.<provider>` blocks -- new keys and new semantics, landed Phase 50 (plans 50-08, 50-09, 50-10), PARITY-03, D-12/D-13.** Every key is optional and a v0.10.1 file loads unchanged (`existing_yaml_loads_unchanged`); the behavior change for keys that already existed is M-B-15 (§9.1).
  - `llm.<provider>.max_tokens: u32` (default unset): the output budget sent when a request sets none; checked against the model's profiled maximum output like a request's own.
  - `llm.<provider>.context_window: u32` (default unset): an adapter-wide context window in tokens, replacing every compiled window of that provider.
  - `llm.<provider>.models.<model-id>.*` (default empty): a per-model profile override laid over the built-in entry field by field. Fields (`ModelOverride`): `context_window`, `max_output_tokens`, `temperature`, `top_p`, `frequency_penalty`, `presence_penalty` (each `{range: [lo, hi]}`, `{fixed: v}` or `not_sent`), `stop_sequences`, `reasoning`, `json_object`, `json_schema`. An unknown key under a model is a load error naming it.
  - `llm.<provider>.api_key` is now optional: an empty or absent key falls back to the provider's own environment variable (`OPENAI_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, ...); keyless Ollama needs none. A block without an `api_key` key now deserializes.
  - `${VAR}` placeholders: an exact `"${NAME}"` in `api_key` is read from the variable `NAME`; `abc${X}` and `$X` are literal. An unset or blank variable is a construction error naming provider, model and variable, never the literal placeholder sent as a key.
  - Precedence (D-13), field by field: the agent's `model`, then the block, then the provider's legacy environment variable, then the compiled default; an environment variable never overrides the block.
  - A reasoning model (a seeded entry or a `models.<id>.reasoning: true` override) defaults to a **300 s** HTTP timeout when neither the block nor the environment sets `timeout_seconds`; any other model keeps the adapter's own default.
  - `llm.<provider>.default_temperature` is now read: see M-B-15 (§9.1) and remove it where the default model's profile does not admit it.
- **`agent_runtime.model_fallback.steps` -- a fallback chain whose hops each send their own model, landed Phase 50 (plan 50-14), PARITY-06, D-15.** Additive and optional: a v0.10.1 file that sets `providers` loads and builds exactly as before.
  - Shape: `agent_runtime.model_fallback.steps: [{provider: <name>, model: <id>}, ...]`, tried strictly in the order written. Each step is built from that provider's `llm.<provider>` block through `LlmProviderFactory::create_from_config` (the credential comes from the block or its environment variable, never from the step), is sent its own model in place of the request's, and is paced under its own `(provider, model)`. Two steps may name the same provider with different models; they are two hops.
  - Precedence: there is none to resolve -- `providers` and `steps` are two spellings of one chain and are **mutually exclusive**. Setting both is a validation error that names both keys, and so is `enabled: true` with neither. A step with an empty `provider` or `model` is rejected naming its index (`agent_runtime.model_fallback.steps[1].model must not be empty`). A disabled section is not inspected.
  - There is no environment variable for `steps` (a list is configuration-file only, like `providers`).
  - Embedding code passes the operator's `llm:` section as `AgentRuntimeDeps.llm_config` (default `None`, which behaves as an empty `llm:` section); `ModelFallbackConfig::resolve_steps` and `ModelFallbackMiddleware::from_steps` are the building blocks.
  - A refusal stops the chain unless the code builds it with `FallbackLlmAdapter::hop_on_refusal(true)`; the configuration has no switch for that (D-10).
- **Reasoning effort keys -- landed Phase 51 (plans 51-03, 51-06, 51-08), REASON-01, REASON-02, REASON-04.** Every key is optional and unset by default: a v0.10.1 file loads unchanged, a request or agent that sets no effort sends exactly the wire body it sent before (pinned byte for byte by the golden files under `crates/paladin-llm/tests/golden/reasoning_unset/`), and no provider has a compiled-in default. Values are `ReasoningEffort` spellings: a level (`minimal`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`), `off`, `auto` or `{budget: N}`. Precedence, highest first: the request's own effort, the agent's, `llm.<provider>.reasoning_effort`, then the vendor's default. The Reasoning Effort page of the book documents each key with its per-model table.
  - `llm.<provider>.reasoning_effort` (default unset): the provider-level default effort, e.g. `reasoning_effort: low` on `llm.kimi`. Checked against the agent's model at agent build; a value the model cannot honour fails there naming the key.
  - `llm.<provider>.reasoning_fallback` (default unset, meaning `refuse`): `refuse`, `nearest_lower` or `nearest_higher`, what the adapter does when the model does not list the effort asked for. `off`, `auto` and a budget are never substituted for one another or for a level.
  - `llm.<provider>.reasoning_wire` (default unset, meaning `unsupported`): self-hosted providers only (`ollama`, `openai_compatible`); `unsupported`, `top_level_effort`, `thinking_object` or `enable_thinking`, the request field the endpoint parses an effort in. The seven hosted providers declare their wire themselves and reject this key.
  - `llm.<provider>.models.<model-id>.effort` (default unset, meaning the seeded row, or `unmeasured` for an unseeded model): `{supported: {levels: [low, medium, high], off: false, auto: true, budget: {min: 1024, max: 32000}}}`, or one of the words `unmeasured`, `wire_unsupported`, `no_reasoning`. An override states a fact about the model; it cannot unlock what the adapter's wire cannot carry.
  - `agents[].reasoning_effort` (default unset): one agent's effort, passed to `PaladinBuilder::reasoning_effort`; an agent whose model cannot honour it fails at load with an error ending in ``(set by `agents[<id>].reasoning_effort`)``.
  - `agents[].reasoning_fallback` (default unset): one agent's substitution policy, passed to `PaladinBuilder::reasoning_fallback`.

## 9.6 HTTP API

**New engine-backed endpoints — landed this phase (HITL-05, Plan 24-11):** three thread routes in
`paladin-web`, nested under `/v1` (ADR-0037) and authenticated by the same middleware `/v1/agents/*`
already uses. `ThreadApiState` is a new, separate router state (`AgentApiState` is untouched — a
deliberate zero, §9.2). Every route answers `501 not_implemented` (naming
`APP_WAYPOINT_STORE_BACKEND`) when no waypoint backend is configured — the spec still lists the
paths regardless of runtime wiring.

| Method | Path | Status codes |
|---|---|---|
| `GET` | `/v1/threads/{id}/state` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `404`, `501` |
| `POST` | `/v1/threads/{id}/resume` | `202`, `400`, `401`, `403`, `404`, `409` (two distinct envelope `code`s — `thread_not_awaiting_input`, `graph_not_registered` — sharing this one HTTP status), `501` |
| `GET` | `/v1/threads/{id}/history` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `501` |

`POST .../resume` additionally requires an admin-role credential via
`crate::agent_auth::require_admin`, returning `403` for an authenticated non-admin caller. This
narrows D-24's "authenticated callers, any role" default for the one mutating route only (CR-01,
`24-REVIEW.md`, commit `00b1e552`), because neither `ThreadId` nor `Waypoint` carries an
owner/principal to scope against yet. `GET .../state` and `GET .../history` remain
authenticated-any-role. Every credential configured for the resume route must be treated as
admin-equivalent until PLAT-06 (Phase 27) supplies real per-thread ownership scoping;
`crates/paladin-web/src/thread_controller.rs`'s module rustdoc carries the full rationale.

`POST .../resume` returns `202 Accepted { thread_id, state_url }` immediately — the actual engine
continuation runs as a background task registered with the process's `ShutdownCoordinator`
(D-25); a client polls `GET .../state` for the outcome, never holding the connection open.
`GET .../history` paginates with `limit` (≤ 100) and an opaque `cursor` (the last page's own
`waypoint_id`, D-27). `crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` was regenerated with these three paths —
purely additive (553 insertions, 0 deletions) — and every pre-existing `/v1/agents/*` path is
byte-identical, both by raw diff and by a dedicated drift-guard test
(`openapi_pre_existing_agent_paths_are_unchanged`).

**Golden diff, closed:** the program-wide proof that every pre-existing `/v1` path is unchanged
across the *whole* v0.10.0 cycle (not just this phase's own additions) is
`crates/paladin-web/tests/openapi_golden_v0_9.rs` (SHIP-02, Phase 29). It restricts both the
generated v0.10 document and the frozen `v0.9.0` baseline
(`crates/paladin-web/tests/fixtures/openapi-v0.9.0.json`) to the six pre-existing v0.9 paths,
follows their transitive `$ref` closure into `components.schemas`, keeps
`components.securitySchemes` in full, and asserts `serde_json::Value` equality with `info.version`
— regenerated by every release bump — as the ONLY sanctioned normalisation. Gated on every PR by
the existing `cargo test -p paladin-web` per-crate CI job; no new workflow job was needed. The
broader platform surface — background run submission, assistants, schedules, admin/writer scopes
on these same thread routes (PLAT-06) — is **PLAT-01…PLAT-06, Phase 27**.

**`POST /v1/threads/{id}/resume` response body gains `run_id` — landed Phase 27 (PLAT-03, Plan
