27-08).** The status-code table two paragraphs above is unchanged verbatim (`202`, `400`, `401`,
`403`, `404`, `409`, `501`, D-20) — only the mechanism behind `ParleyPort::resume_with` changed,
from Phase 24's in-process `tokio::spawn` continuation to a durable re-enqueue of the SAME `run_id`
under the run server's queue (PLAT-FR-06, D-19, D-23; see the `ParleyPortAdapter` row in §9.2
immediately below). The response body is now `202 Accepted { thread_id, state_url, run_id }`:
`run_id` is a nullable string, present whenever the run-server path handled the resume, and `null`
only for a thread suspended before a run-server (`RunRepositoryPort`/`RunQueuePort`) was wired at
all (X-03 — the exact Phase 24 in-process-spawn behaviour is preserved for that one case).
`crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` was regenerated; the only diff is the new nullable `run_id`
property on the `ResumeAcceptedResponse` schema (`git diff --stat` shows 8 insertions, 1 deletion),
verified by `openapi_matches_committed_baseline` and confirmed by direct JSON-path inspection that
every other schema and path is byte-identical.

**Remaining run routes, thread list/get/fork/delete, and pagination everywhere — landed Phase 27
(PLAT-06, Plan 27-15, D-45…D-47).** The last new `/v1/runs*` routes this phase's `RunApiState`
carries, plus four new routes on the existing `ThreadApiState` (D-45), completing PRD 06 §2.1's
run/thread surface and PLAT-FR-16's "pagination, auth and scopes are consistent everywhere" claim.

| Method | Path | Status codes |
|---|---|---|
| `GET` | `/v1/runs` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `501` |
| `POST` | `/v1/runs/{run_id}/cancel` | `202`, `400`, `401`, `403`, `404`, `409`, `501` |
| `GET` | `/v1/runs/{run_id}/webhook-deliveries` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `501` |
| `GET` | `/v1/threads` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `501` |
| `GET` | `/v1/threads/{id}` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `404`, `501` |
| `POST` | `/v1/threads/{id}/fork` | `202`, `400`, `401`, `403`, `404`, `409`, `501` |
| `DELETE` | `/v1/threads/{id}` | `204`, `400`, `401`, `403`, `404`, `409`, `501` |

**Pagination (D-47), applied verbatim on every list route in this table plus the pre-existing
`/v1/assistants*`/`/v1/schedules*`/`/v1/assistants/{id}/versions` routes:** `limit` (an omitted
value defaults to 20; `0` or a value above 100 is `400 bad_request`, message "limit must be
between 1 and 100") and an opaque `cursor` (`400 invalid_cursor` on anything malformed — never a
`500`, never a silent full-table scan, never the cursor's internal encoding in the error message).
`{ items, next_cursor }`, `next_cursor: null` on the last page, `{ items: [], next_cursor: null }`
on an empty result (never a bare array, never `404`). The cursor is a keyset walk — a STABLE
ordering for rows that already existed when the first page was fetched, but NOT a point-in-time
snapshot: a row inserted after the first page was read may be omitted from the walk. Every list
route's own `#[utoipa::path]` description states this explicitly. `crates/paladin-web/src/pagination.rs`
(`resolve_limit`, `encode_cursor`/`decode_cursor`, base64url no-padding over compact JSON) is the
one shared implementation `run_controller.rs`/`thread_controller.rs`/`assistant_controller.rs`/
`schedule_controller.rs` all call — the two latter files' limit handling switched from a locally
duplicated `parse_limit` to this shared helper in the same plan, with no behavior change beyond
the newly typed `limit == 0` rejection (previously silently defaulted to 20 in both files).

**Two-tier authorization scopes (D-46), applied on every mutating route across this whole phase:**
invocation-shaped (`POST /runs`, `POST /runs/{id}/cancel`, `POST /threads/{id}/resume`, `POST
/threads/{id}/fork`) — any authenticated principal, subject to the target assistant's own
`allowed_roles` (empty ⇒ any authenticated caller), authorized entirely inside
`RunSubmissionService` (`paladin-web` never resolves `allowed_roles` itself, ADR-0031); vs.
registry-shaped (`POST`/`DELETE /assistants*`, `POST`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /schedules*`, `DELETE
/threads/{id}`) — `require_admin`. Reads need authentication only. Proven at the router level (not
just per-file) by `run_controller.rs`'s `run_controller_auth` test group, which hits a
merged-in `/v1/assistants` admin route through the SAME `run_router` this table's own routes
mount on.

**Rate limiting (pre-existing `tower-governor` layer, D-44) proven on `/v1/runs*` for the first
time this plan** — `run_controller_auth::rate_limited_request_is_429` applies the SAME
`apply_rate_limit`/`RateLimitConfig` `/v1/agents/*` already used, confirming the layer generalizes
to the new router with no route-specific wiring.

`crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` was regenerated; the diff is exactly these seven new paths and
their schemas, verified by `openapi_matches_committed_baseline` — every pre-existing path and
schema is byte-identical.

**SsrfGuard collapsed onto one shared implementation (D-42), not a new endpoint but recorded here
since the wave_context called it out explicitly.** 27-14 (parallel wave, `src/application/
services/run/schedule/admin.rs`) built its own standalone `SsrfGuard` because 27-13's real guard
was not in that plan's worktree base at the time. This plan deletes that duplicate and routes
`ScheduleService`'s write-time webhook check through `src/application/services/run/webhook::SsrfGuard`
(the same guard 27-13's webhook client already uses at send time) — one guard, two call sites, no
behavior change to either.

**`POST/GET /v1/runs`, `GET /v1/runs/{run_id}`, `GET /v1/runs/{run_id}/stream` — landed Phase 27
(PLAT-01…PLAT-03, plans 27-01, 27-10, 27-15).** The submit/read/stream half of the run surface
this section's earlier table (`cancel`/`webhook-deliveries`, 27-15) did not itself repeat; recorded
here now so every `/v1/runs*` route this phase adds has one row (D-53).

| Method | Path | Status codes |
|---|---|---|
| `POST` | `/v1/runs` | `202`, `400`, `401`, `403`, `404`, `409` (`thread_busy`, `webhook_url_rejected` — D-42), `501` |
| `GET` | `/v1/runs` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `501` |
| `GET` | `/v1/runs/{run_id}` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `404`, `501` |
| `GET` | `/v1/runs/{run_id}/stream` | `200` (`text/event-stream`), `400`, `401`, `404`, `501` |

`POST /v1/runs` is invocation-shaped (D-46): any authenticated principal, subject to the resolved
assistant's own `allowed_roles`, authorized entirely inside `RunSubmissionService`. `GET
/v1/runs/{run_id}/stream` frames the seven frozen wire events (`superstep`, `node_started`,
`node_finished`, `state_delta`, `parley`, `done`, `error`, each carrying `seq`/`at`/`mode`/`dropped`)
with a 15s heartbeat on both the live and degraded paths (PLAT-FR-07, D-24…D-27) — see 27-10's own
route rustdoc for the full per-event payload shape.

**`/v1/assistants*` (seven routes) — landed Phase 27 (PLAT-04, plan 27-12).** Publish/resolve
surface for both `Agent` and `Workflow`-kind assistant definitions (D-28…D-32); no `PUT`/`PATCH`
route is ever registered anywhere (D-29 — a stored version is immutable) and every mutating route
rejects a code-registered `assistant_id` with `409 code_registered_immutable`, checked before the
admin port is ever called.

| Method | Path | Status codes |
|---|---|---|
| `POST` | `/v1/assistants` | `201`, `400`, `401`, `403`, `409` (`already_exists`, `code_registered_immutable`), `501` |
| `GET` | `/v1/assistants` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `501` |
| `GET` | `/v1/assistants/{assistant_id}` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `404`, `501` |
| `DELETE` | `/v1/assistants/{assistant_id}` | `204`, `400`, `401`, `403`, `404`, `409` (`code_registered_immutable`), `501` |
| `POST` | `/v1/assistants/{assistant_id}/versions` | `201`, `400`, `401`, `403`, `404`, `409` (`code_registered_immutable`, `version_conflict`), `501` |
| `GET` | `/v1/assistants/{assistant_id}/versions` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `501` |
| `GET` | `/v1/assistants/{assistant_id}/versions/{version}` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `404`, `501` |

`POST`/`DELETE /assistants*` and `POST .../versions` require an admin credential
(`require_admin`, registry-shaped per this section's own D-46 paragraph above); every `GET` needs
authentication only. `GET /assistants` merges in synthetic `{ source: "code" }` entries for
code-registered agents on the first page only when `assistants.expose_code_registry` is on
(default `true`, D-32) — a known, documented scope boundary for a multi-page heterogeneous merge,
tracked in `.planning/WINDOWS.md` (id 29), not this phase's own gap to close.

**`/v1/schedules*` (five routes) — landed Phase 27 (PLAT-05, plans 27-11, 27-14).** Cron-driven
recurring run submission against a `ScheduleAdminPort`, off by default (`schedules.enabled`, D-50)
so the tick loop only spawns when explicitly turned on.

| Method | Path | Status codes |
|---|---|---|
| `POST` | `/v1/schedules` | `201`, `400`, `401`, `403`, `501` |
| `GET` | `/v1/schedules` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `501` |
| `GET` | `/v1/schedules/{schedule_id}` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `404`, `501` |
| `PATCH` | `/v1/schedules/{schedule_id}` | `200`, `400`, `401`, `403`, `404`, `501` |
| `DELETE` | `/v1/schedules/{schedule_id}` | `204`, `400`, `401`, `403`, `404`, `501` |

`POST`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /schedules*` require an admin credential (registry-shaped); `GET` needs
authentication only. A `cron`/`timezone` change on `PATCH` recomputes `next_tick`; a `webhook`
change on either `POST` or `PATCH` re-runs the write-time SSRF guard (D-42, the same shared
`webhook::SsrfGuard` this section's collapse paragraph above names).

**Phase-wide `openapi.json` diff review (D-48, plan 27-18):** `git log --oneline --
crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` walked back to the commit immediately before 27-01 and confirmed
every change across the whole phase is either an added path/schema, or the one registered
field-level addition this section already documents (`ResumeAcceptedResponse.run_id`, above) — no
pre-existing path or schema was ever removed or altered. `crates/paladin-web/tests/openapi_golden_v0_9.rs`
(SHIP-02, Phase 29) is the program-wide ship-gate golden-diff proof that restricts itself to the
six pre-existing paths; this review is the phase-scoped superset (every path Phase 27 touched, not
just the pre-existing ones).

**Workspace line coverage (D-54, X-02):** the 82% floor (ADR-0006) is proven under CI's exact
invocation — `cargo llvm-cov --workspace --features integration-tests,llm-all --lcov
--output-path lcov.info --fail-under-lines 82 -- --test-threads=1` — see the `coverage` CI job;
`scripts/coverage.sh` (the same command, `make coverage`) hard-fails locally without a live
Redis/MinIO, so this figure is only ever produced in CI or a UAT run with those services present,
never claimed from a bare local run.

**Generated SDK clients (D-49, PLAT-FR-17, plan 27-18):** a `sdk-clients` CI job generates a Python
and a TypeScript (`typescript-fetch`) client from `crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` with one pinned
`openapi-generator-cli` image (`openapitools/openapi-generator-cli:v7.25.0`, the npm package the
documented fallback), asserts each output tree is non-empty (≥ 20 files), builds `paladin-server`,
and runs `scripts/sdk-smoke/{smoke.py,smoke.ts}` against it on the all-InMemory/SQLite profile:
list assistants → submit a run for a code-registered agent → poll to a terminal status. Runs on
every PR/push, no path filter.

**`GET /v1/runs/{run_id}/stream` payload gains `trace_seq`; `RunStreamMode` gains `Replay` — landed
Phase 28 (28-11), OBS-02, D-15/D-16.** The endpoint's status-code table (§9.6's earlier
`POST`/`GET /v1/runs*` row) is unchanged verbatim — this is purely additive to the streamed
payload shape, not a new route. Every live and replayed SSE event's `payload` now carries an
additive `trace_seq` field (the originating `TraceRecord`'s own `seq`), documented in the route's
own OpenAPI description as the correlation key that ties one SSE event to the same event in a log
line, an OTel span, and a persisted `run_traces` row (see the observability operations page).
Degraded-mode events synthesized from Waypoint polling (no persisted trace available) carry no
`trace_seq`. `RunStreamMode` gains a `Replay` variant, marked `#[non_exhaustive]` in the same
change (X-10.2 spirit): when `trace.persist` is on and a requested run's rows exist in
`run_traces`, the stream replays them through the SAME `map_trace_event` mapping — the seven
frozen wire names, `mode: replay`, the record's own original `at` and `trace_seq` — then
terminates with `done`/`error` exactly as a live run does, upgrading the existing degraded
Waypoint-polling path to full fidelity rather than replacing it (no rows still falls back to
today's degraded path, unchanged). **`crates/paladin-web/openapi.json`'s `UPDATE_OPENAPI=1`
re-bless produces an EMPTY diff for this change** — `RunStreamMode`/`RunStreamEvent` are not
reachable from the three router sources `openapi.rs`'s drift-guard assembles; the SSE endpoint's
wire-event shape is static prose in the route's description, not a schema derived via
`#[derive(ToSchema)]` from the Rust types this plan touched. This is recorded here as a measured
fact, not assumed: the re-bless was actually run and its diff actually inspected.

**`GET /v1/dev-ui/threads/{id}` — a non-API route, landed Phase 28 (28-15), OBS-03, D-25.** Renders
one thread's execution overlay as a static HTML page — see the graph-visualization user guide.
Deliberately carries **no** `#[utoipa::path]` attribute and is a plain `axum::Router`
(`create_dev_ui_router`) never merged into `create_app_router` or any `OpenApiRouter` — it is
structurally absent from `crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` regardless of the `dev-ui` Cargo
feature's compile state, verified by `git diff --exit-code crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` with
the feature enabled. Admin-gated (`require_auth` + `require_admin`, the same pair the crate's
other admin routes use) and feature-gated (`dev-ui`, off by default, absent from `full`) — a v0.9
deployment, and a v0.10 deployment that never compiles with `--features dev-ui`, has no such route
at all.

**`POST /v1/agents/{id}/execute` — response shape change, not a new route, landed Phase 31
(31-06/31-07), ACCT-02/ACCT-05, D-24.** `ExecuteResponse`'s bare `token_count: u32` field is
replaced by `usage: TokenUsageResponse` — a new `utoipa::ToSchema` DTO (`prompt_tokens`,
`completion_tokens`, `total_tokens`, plus optional `cache_read_tokens`/`cache_write_tokens`/
`reasoning_tokens`, mirroring `TokenUsage` field-for-field) converted through the one
`From<PaladinResult>` conversion point. `crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` was regenerated via
`make openapi` in the same commit that changed the struct; the committed-baseline drift-guard test
(`openapi_matches_committed_baseline`) passes against the regenerated document, and
`git diff --exit-code crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` is clean immediately after regeneration.
This is a genuine, deliberate break in the published response schema (the §9.2 `paladin-web |
ExecuteResponse` row records the Rust-API side; this entry is its HTTP-surface twin) — the route
itself, its path, its method and every other response field are unchanged. The pre-existing
SHIP-02 golden-diff gate (`crates/paladin-web/tests/openapi_golden_v0_9.rs`, Phase 29) that would
otherwise flag any `/v1/agents/*` schema drift against the frozen `v0.9.0` baseline carries one
narrowly-scoped, test-proven exception (`strip_known_v0_10_execute_response_divergence`) for
exactly this `token_count`/`usage`/`TokenUsageResponse` divergence — every other field of
`ExecuteResponse`, and every other schema in the six frozen paths, is still subject to the gate's
full power. The CI Python-client generation job (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) consumes the same
regenerated `openapi.json`; no other file it reads was changed by this phase.

**Run reads are tenant-scoped -- landed Phase 40 (40-01, 40-02, 40-05), PLAT-07, D-11/D-12/D-13.**
Every run now records the principal that submitted it (the tenant configured for the API key,
and the key's configured name -- §9.2 `Run` row, §9.4 migration `008`), and every run read route
serves only the runs the calling principal may see. The rule is one shared core value
(`RunReadScope`, `paladin-core`): a **user-role** principal sees only runs recorded under its own
tenant (two keys of the same tenant see each other's runs; a key of another tenant does not; a
user-role principal never sees a run with no recorded principal), and an **admin-role** principal
-- including the open-access principal when authentication is disabled -- sees **every** run,
including runs with no recorded principal (schedule-fired, same-process, pre-v0.11 rows). The
tenant comes only from the key's configured mapping (§9.5); no header, query parameter or body
field can name one, and a `tenant_id` query parameter on `GET /v1/runs` is unknown and ignored.
Behaviour by route: `GET /v1/runs` lists only the caller's tenant's runs (admin: all), with the
`thread_id`/`assistant_id`/`status` filters composing with the scope inside the store's own query
(another tenant's `thread_id` is an empty page; the keyset cursor walk stays gap-free);
`GET /v1/runs/{run_id}`, `GET /v1/runs/{run_id}/stream`, `GET /v1/runs/{run_id}/webhook-deliveries`
and `POST /v1/runs/{run_id}/cancel` answer the **missing-run `404`** -- the same status, code and
message as a run that does not exist, never a `403` -- for another tenant's run; cancel checks
visibility BEFORE the assistant `allowed_roles` check, so it neither leaks existence nor permits a
cross-tenant mutation. `/stream`, `/cancel` and `/webhook-deliveries` now also answer `501`
naming `run_store.backend` when the run store is not configured (previously they needed only
their own port), because the visibility check reads the run row. `GET /v1/runs/{run_id}/webhook-deliveries`
gains a documented `404` row in `crates/paladin-web/openapi.json`; the other three routes'
existing `404` descriptions are reworded to "Unknown run, or a run the caller may not see". A
client that relied on reading (or cancelling) other principals' runs under the v0.10
deployment-wide model must use an admin-role key. **`/v1/threads/*` is unchanged**: threads
carry no tenant, so `GET /threads`, `GET /threads/{id}/state` and `GET /threads/{id}/history`
remain deployment-wide for any authenticated principal (tracked as an open broken-windows row,
not implied closed by this entry). A route-enumerating test
(`every_run_id_route_hides_foreign_runs_behind_the_missing_run_404`) reads the `/v1/runs/{run_id}*`
operations from the router's own OpenAPI document and fails on any route missing from its table.

**`RunResponse` gains `submitted_by` -- landed Phase 40 (40-05), TENANT-02, D-19.** The
`GET /v1/runs/{run_id}` and `GET /v1/runs` item body gains an additive, always-serialised
`submitted_by` property: `{ "tenant_id": "<tenant>", "api_key_id": "<key name>" }` for a run
with a recorded principal, `null` for a run with none. It carries the API key's configured NAME,
never the key value, and never the role (roles are configuration, not data, and are not
persisted on the run row). `crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` was regenerated through
`openapi_matches_committed_baseline` (`make openapi`): a new `RunAttributionDto` schema, the
nullable `submitted_by` property on `RunResponse`, and the description/`404` changes recorded in
the entry above -- no path, method or pre-existing property is removed. The `v0.9.0` golden
gate (`crates/paladin-web/tests/openapi_golden_v0_9.rs`) is untouched: `RunResponse` and every
`/v1/runs*` path postdate the frozen baseline.

**Allowance refusals answer 429 allowance_exhausted -- landed Phase 41 (41-01), ALLOW-02, D-12/D-13.** `POST /v1/runs` by a principal whose API-key or tenant allowance is exhausted (a balance at or above a configured ceiling) answers `429` with the envelope `code` `allowance_exhausted` -- a different code from the per-IP rate limiter's, so a client can tell quota from pacing by the body code alone -- and `error.details` carrying exactly `scope` (`api_key` or `tenant`), `kind` (`window` or `lifetime`), `balance` and `ceiling` (display strings such as `25.0000 USD`) and `window_start`/`window_end` (RFC 3339, `null` for a lifetime ceiling). A `Retry-After` header carries the whole seconds from the store clock to the window's end, and is omitted for a lifetime ceiling (a client must not spin on a cap that never reopens). No run row, queue entry or ledger row is written, and the body never repeats the caller's tenant or key. Deployments without a `treasurer.allowance` section are unaffected. **Extended in Phase 41 plan 41-04 (D-06, D-07, C4, C9):** the same `429` now also answers `POST /v1/threads/{id}/fork` and the three HTTP agent routes that start spend -- `POST /v1/agents/{id}/execute`, `POST /v1/agents/{id}/execute/stream` (a plain JSON `429` returned before any event stream opens) and `POST /v1/agents/{id}/jobs` (refused synchronously, before a job id is issued or a task spawned, so a refused caller is never handed a job id for work that would later fail). An `Admin` principal with a configured allowance is bound exactly like a `User` (D-09). A failing allowance check answers `500` and runs nothing on every gated route (fail closed, D-10). `crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` is regenerated with a `429` response on all five operations, and the frozen v0.9 golden gate (`crates/paladin-web/tests/openapi_golden_v0_9.rs`) carries a third sanctioned, narrowly-scoped exception that removes exactly the `"429"` response key from the three agent operations in both documents before comparing -- nothing else about those paths is relaxed. **Extended in Phase 41 plan 41-05 (D-08, C12, C13):** a schedule tick whose creator's tenant or API-key allowance is exhausted fires no run -- the tick is recorded as skipped (`SkipReason::AllowanceExhausted`) and `skipped_ticks` on the schedule is incremented, so "why did nothing run" stays answerable from `GET /v1/schedules/{id}`. `POST /v1/schedules` stamps the creating principal's tenant id and API key name on the schedule, fired runs are attributed to that creator (their spend settles under it), and `ScheduleResponse` deliberately does not expose the creator while `GET /v1/schedules` is not tenant-scoped. A schedule created before Phase 41 has no recorded creator and keeps firing unattributed and ungated. The invocation role check is still not applied at fire time (the attribution is identity only, never a role), so a schedule created by an Admin on an assistant whose `allowed_roles` excludes Admin still fires. Later plans extend this entry (the warning and notice surfaces).

**Operator allowance notices -- landed Phase 41 (41-08), ALLOW-04, D-17.** When `treasurer.allowance.webhook { url, secret }` is configured, every warn-threshold notice an admission wins is delivered once to that operator URL through the existing durable webhook queue: a `webhook_deliveries` row with event `allowance_warning`, drained by `WebhookDeliveryService` over the existing no-redirect client with the same bounded retries, `3xx`/`4xx` dead-lettering and send-time SSRF check as a run webhook. The JSON payload has exactly twelve keys -- `event` (always `allowance_warning`), `scope` (`api_key` or `tenant`), `kind` (`window` or `lifetime`), `balance` and `ceiling` (display strings such as `0.8000 USD`), `window_start` and `window_end` (RFC 3339, `null` for a lifetime ceiling), `warn_at`, `run_id` (the admitting run, `null` on the HTTP agent routes), `timestamp` (the store instant the notice was recorded at), `tenant_id` and `api_key_id` (the API key NAME, `null` for a tenant-scope notice, never a key value) -- and never run input, a key value or a secret. It is signed `X-Paladin-Signature: sha256=<hex>` over the exact stored bytes with `treasurer.allowance.webhook.secret` (or `APP_TREASURER_ALLOWANCE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`), held on the delivery service and never written to the row; the request carries `X-Paladin-Event: allowance_warning`. The row names a correlation run id that no run owns and the fixed thread `treasurer-notices`, so `GET /v1/runs/{id}/webhook-deliveries` never lists it, and a caller cannot subscribe a run or schedule webhook to `allowance_warning` (the caller-facing event parser answers `400`). The URL is checked by the SSRF guard at boot -- `paladin-server` refuses to start, naming `treasurer.allowance.webhook.url` and pointing at `webhooks.allow_private`, for a private or loopback target; the cloud metadata address is always rejected -- and again at send time. Omitting `webhook` disables only this leg: the notice row, the trace event and the herald line still occur. An enqueue failure after a won notice is logged at `error` and not retried, and never affects the run. At most one notice per scope, limit kind, window and ceiling, so at most one delivery for each. **Rollout rule (RESEARCH Pitfall 12): enable `treasurer.allowance.webhook` only after every replica runs this build.** A replica from before this change decodes `webhook_deliveries.event` with a closed parser, so its `claim_due` rejects the unknown `allowance_warning` row and fails its whole batch, stalling run-webhook delivery on that replica until it is upgraded. Delivery is at-least-once: a retried attempt after an ambiguous failure can reach the operator twice, and `X-Paladin-Delivery` identifies the row.

**Halted runs report why and where to resume -- landed Phase 42 (42-03), ALLOW-03, D-06/D-19, G7.** `GET /v1/runs/{run_id}` and every row of `GET /v1/runs` gain two always-serialised properties: `halt_reason` (an object, or `null`) and `final_waypoint_id` (the run's final Waypoint id, or `null`). For a run halted by an exhausted allowance `halt_reason` is `{ "reason": "allowance_exhausted", "scope", "kind", "balance", "ceiling", "window_start", "window_end" }` -- the figures of the Phase 41 `429 allowance_exhausted` `details`, built by the same function, with `balance` and `ceiling` rendered from integer nano-units as display strings (`"1.0000 USD"`); it is `{ "reason": "ledger_unavailable" }` when the ledger could not be read and the run halted fail-closed; and `null` for every run that did not halt on spend. `final_waypoint_id` of a halted run is the Halted Waypoint -- the fork point to resume from. `error` stays `null` for a halt: a halt is a resume point, not a failure. The object carries only the halted ceiling's own figures -- never another scope's, a tenant id, or an API key name or value. The caller's `halted` run webhook payload gains the same optional `halt_reason` key (other events are unchanged and the `X-Paladin-Signature` is still computed over the stored payload bytes). `crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` was regenerated through `openapi_matches_committed_baseline` (`make openapi`): two nullable properties on `RunResponse`, nothing removed; the `v0.9.0` golden gate is untouched because `RunResponse` and every `/v1/runs*` path postdate the frozen baseline. **Resuming a halted run (Phase 42, 42-04, D-07):** a halted run is terminal, so resume is a new run forked from its `final_waypoint_id` -- `POST /v1/threads/{thread_id}/fork` with `from_waypoint_id` set to it -- which answers `429 allowance_exhausted` with `Retry-After` while the window is still exhausted and, once admitted, continues from the Halted Waypoint without re-running a completed superstep. A `ledger_unavailable` halt resumes the same way once the ledger reads again; an agent-kind run has no checkpoint and is resumed by a fresh `POST /v1/runs`. No new route, field or status code: the fork route and its `429` already shipped.

**SSE done reports why a run halted -- landed Phase 42 (42-05), PLAT-09, D-14/D-16, G10.** A spend-halted run's `done` event on `GET /v1/runs/{run_id}/stream` now carries `halt_reason` -- the same object `GET /v1/runs/{run_id}` returns, built by the same `HaltReason::wire_json` function -- beside `status`, `waypoint_id`, `usage` and `trace_seq`: `{ "status": "halted", ..., "halt_reason": { "reason": "allowance_exhausted", "scope", "kind", "balance", "ceiling", "window_start", "window_end" } }`. A ledger-unavailable halt is a `done` with `"halt_reason": { "reason": "ledger_unavailable" }` on both the live and the degraded path, never an `error` event (`error` stays reserved for `failed`). A halt with no Treasurer reason (a caller cancel or a token halt) keeps the old payload byte for byte, with no `halt_reason` key. The live, degraded and replay paths agree on `status` and `halt_reason` for the same run, and a replayed stream takes its terminal `status` and `halt_reason` from the run row when the row is terminal and skips a persisted `RunFinished` while the row is not (a worker draining on shutdown leaves its run `running`), so a stored trace for a caller-cancelled run, which recorded `halted`, now replays as `cancelled`. `waypoint_id` follows each path's existing rule. Additive: a client ignoring unknown keys is unaffected; the seven frozen event names are unchanged.

**SSE done reports cancelled; a drain is not terminal -- landed Phase 42 (42-06), PLAT-09, D-14/D-15.** A caller-cancelled run's `done` event on `GET /v1/runs/{run_id}/stream` now carries `status: "cancelled"` (it said `halted` before), equal to the `cancelled` status `GET /v1/runs/{run_id}` reports, and carries no `halt_reason` key. This holds on both routes a cancel can take: through the instance that dispatches the run (the in-process token, now reported to the engine by a per-run probe) and through another instance (the durable flag, observed at the next superstep boundary by the debounced probe). A worker shutdown that drains a run mid-flight emits no `done` and no `error`: the run row stays `running` and its queue message is redelivered, so a subscriber sees the stream end without a terminal event and reconnects to follow the redelivered run. A spend halt that lands during shutdown is still a `done` with `status: "halted"` and its `halt_reason`. A client that matched `done.status == "halted"` to detect a cancel must also match `cancelled`; a client that treated stream end without `done` as an error should treat it as "reconnect". The seven frozen event names are unchanged.

**Agent routes stop on the allowance -- landed Phase 42 (42-08), ALLOW-05, D-10/D-12/D-13.** The three HTTP agent routes that start spend -- `POST /v1/agents/{id}/execute`, `POST /v1/agents/{id}/execute/stream` and `POST /v1/agents/{id}/jobs` -- now bound an agent's loop by the caller's remaining allowance. Admission derives a per-run token budget from the tightest remaining ceiling at the agent model's dearest `treasurer.pricing` price (every token billed at the dearest axis, so a prompt-heavy agent stops earlier than its true spend would require), and the agent loop ends after the model response that crosses it. A call that ends this way answers `200` with `stop_reason: "allowance_halted"` and a `halt_reason` object -- the same object `GET /v1/runs/{run_id}` returns, built by the same `HaltReason::wire_json` -- with the partial output kept and the truncation notice appended. The figures report the binding ceiling with `balance` equal to the ceiling, a deliberately conservative bound because the loop does not know the true post-spend balance. `POST /v1/agents/{id}/jobs` records the same `stop_reason` and `halt_reason` on its job result, and the buffered fallback of `execute/stream` (an agent with no streaming backend, whose `done` event is the serialized `ExecuteResponse`) carries them in its `done` data. A **true streamed** `execute/stream` call is one provider call that the server never cuts: it is admission-only, and its overshoot is that whole call. Its terminal `done` stays `{ "done": true, "usage": ... }` byte for byte unless the final chunk's reported `total_tokens` is strictly greater than the derived figure, in which case it additionally carries an informational `halt_reason` object (the same shape) that reports the crossing -- not a halt the server performed (the operator's option-b decision at the Phase 42 design gate, ADR-0057 group e). Every other `stop_reason` response is byte-identical to before: the `halt_reason` key is absent unless the call was halted. **`422 model_unpriced`:** a principal with a configured allowance ceiling calling an agent whose model has no `treasurer.pricing` row is refused before the agent runs, on all three routes, with `code` `model_unpriced`, `details.model` naming the model and no `Retry-After` header (a configuration incoherence, not pacing; spend that cannot be metered cannot be bounded). A derived budget of zero is refused `429 allowance_exhausted` with the binding ceiling's real figures, and a principal with no ceiling is unaffected on every route. **The operator `agent_runtime.token_budget` now takes effect on the HTTP agent routes:** every per-agent service, config-defined or runtime-provisioned, installs the one `TokenBudget`, enforcing the tighter of the operator figure (when `enabled`) and the allowance-derived one (a tie goes to the allowance); before this change the operator figure was inert on these routes. A deployment with the operator budget disabled and no allowance is unchanged. `crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` is regenerated (`make openapi`): `ExecuteResponse` gains an optional `halt_reason` object and the three operations document a `422` response; the frozen v0.9 golden gate carries a fourth sanctioned, narrowly-scoped exception (`strip_known_v0_11_halt_reason`) that removes exactly those two additions from both documents before comparing, and a test fails if it widens.

**Agent-kind runs stop on the allowance -- landed Phase 42 (42-09), ALLOW-03, ALLOW-05, D-08/D-09/D-10/D-12/D-13.** A run of an agent-kind assistant (`Runnable::Agent`) is bounded exactly as the HTTP agent routes are. `POST /v1/runs` for an agent-kind assistant by a principal with a configured ceiling is admitted with the agent's model (taken from the resolved assistant, never from the request): a model with no `treasurer.pricing` row answers `422 model_unpriced` (`details.model`, no `Retry-After`) and an allowance too small to buy one token of that model answers `429 allowance_exhausted`, both before any run row is written; workflow assistants keep the model-less admission and are unchanged. The worker then re-derives the budget **at dispatch** from the ledger (never from a figure persisted at submit time, so an allowance spent while the run was queued is honoured) and the run ends after the model response that crosses it: the run is recorded `halted` (it was `completed` before, with no limit), `error` stays `null`, `halt_reason` is the `allowance_exhausted` object, the partial output is kept on the row with the truncation notice appended, the live and replayed SSE `done` carries `status: "halted"` and the `halt_reason`, and the subscribed `halted` webhook carries it too. A zero or exhausted figure at dispatch records the same halt, and an unreadable ledger records `halt_reason` `ledger_unavailable` (fail closed), in both cases without calling the model. A model that lost its price row between admission and dispatch records `failed` with an error naming the model, also without calling the model. A schedule-fired agent-kind run refused as unpriced is counted and logged like the schedule's other non-allowance submission failures, never as an allowance skip. **Agent-kind runs have no checkpoint (D-08):** the agent loop writes no Waypoint, so a halted agent-kind run has `final_waypoint_id: null`, cannot be forked from a checkpoint, and is resumed by submitting a fresh `POST /v1/runs`, which re-executes from the start; the engine path's continue-from-checkpoint is unchanged (recorded as a known window in `.planning/WINDOWS.md`). **The operator `agent_runtime.token_budget` does not apply to these runs:** the shared run-engine service, which also executes every engine node, installs the `TokenBudget` in Treasurer-only mode with the operator figure forced off, so only a call whose scope carries a derived figure is ever cut and an engine node is never capped (an operator budget there would hand a successful partial result to a Battlefield, ADR-0052). `crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` is regenerated (`make openapi`): `POST /runs` documents the `422`; `POST /runs` is not in the frozen v0.9 document, so the golden gate needs no new exception.

**Mid-run allowance notices -- landed Phase 42 (42-11), ALLOW-03, D-17/D-18.** A run that crosses `warn_at` while it is running now warns at its first superstep boundary past the threshold, instead of waiting for the next admission: the same once-per-window notice claim as admission, emitting exactly one `allowance_warning` trace event on that run's own stream and queueing one operator `allowance_warning` delivery; a lost claim (another run or an admission already owns the window's notice) emits nothing. A spend halt now sends the operator one `allowance_halted` delivery per scope, limit, window and ceiling: the payload carries the same twelve keys as `allowance_warning` with `event` set to `allowance_halted`, `run_id` the halted run and `warn_at` the ceiling's configured threshold, so a receiver that already handles the warning needs only a new `event` value; twenty runs halting in one window produce one delivery. A `ledger_unavailable` halt claims no notice and sends nothing (it is logged at `error`). The boundary guard remembers, per run, each notice claim it already tried, so a long run past its threshold writes at most one claim per ceiling and window rather than one per boundary; the store remains the source of truth and a claim never changes the guard's halt or continue decision. No configuration changes and no new route: the notices ride the Phase 41 `treasurer.allowance.webhook` target and signing secret, and a deployment with no operator webhook still records the durable rows and the trace warning. `crates/paladin-web/openapi.json` is unchanged. **Rollout caveat:** enable this on every replica before relying on it -- a replica running a build from before 42-10 cannot parse the `allowance_halted` event it would find in the shared delivery queue. No public Rust item is added (`Treasurer::spend_guard` keeps its signature; the emitter-carrying constructor is crate-private), so there is no §9.2 row.

## 9.7 Deprecations

Empty — no item is marked `#[deprecated]` by any phase so far, and **Phase 25 (FT) deliberately adds none (X-03)**: the legacy Battalion `RetryPolicy`, `ErrorStrategy` and `NodeError` summary types under `paladin_core::platform::container::battalion`, and the legacy Formation/Phalanx/Campaign timeout handling, remain live and undeprecated alongside the new `Aegis` family; the retained `PaladinError::LlmError(String)` variant, now constructed by no first-party production code (25-06), is likewise left undeprecated. Phase 44 (v0.11) removes them outright under ADR-0059, without a deprecation cycle (D-00c) -- see the Phase 44 rows in 9.2. **Phase 28 (OBS) also adds none:** no item touched by this phase — `TraceEvent`/`TraceSink` (moved and reshaped, not deprecated), `FallbackLlmAdapter::with_trace_sink` (renamed to `with_trace_emitter`, the old name simply removed rather than kept and marked `#[deprecated]`, since both are new-in-0.10 with the only caller being the facade itself, D-39), or any other type this phase's §9.2 note records — is marked `#[deprecated]`. Entries would have been added here as producing epics shipped theirs. At the v0.10.0 close
(SHIP-01, Phase 29) this section is confirmed empty: no phase in the program marked any item
`#[deprecated]`. Phase 48 (v0.11) renames the base wrapper, the version family and the two wrapper fields, and unifies the Citadel placeholder types, outright under ADR-0060 and without a deprecation cycle -- no item is marked `#[deprecated]`; see the Phase 48 rows in 9.2.

## 9.8 Upgrade checklist

One ordered, copy-pasteable checklist for an operator upgrading a v0.9.0 deployment to v0.10.0.
Every step below names a concrete command or file present in the shipped tree; none invents a
subcommand that does not exist.

1. **Back up state.** Snapshot every state directory and database this deployment uses: the
   waypoint store (`SqliteWaypointStore`/`PostgresWaypointStore`'s backing file or database,
   §9.4), the run store (`RunStoreConfig`'s SQLite file or PostgreSQL database, §9.5), the
   Garrison SQLite database if used, and any Citadel state files — Citadel's own file format is
   unaffected by this program (§9.4's repeated "existing state is unaffected" notes). There is no
   destructive migration to reverse a bad upgrade against; a restored backup plus the v0.9.0
   binary is the rollback path.
2. **Apply migrations — by starting the new binary, not a separate command.** Every migration
   this program added (`waypoints`, `garrison_entries.is_summary`, `vault_records`, `runs`,
   `assistants`/`assistant_versions`, `run_schedules`, `webhook_deliveries`, `run_traces` — the
   full list is §9.4) runs automatically at adapter construction via `sqlx::migrate!`; there is no
   `sqlx migrate run` step for an operator to invoke by hand. Start the new `paladin-server`
   binary once against the restored backup and confirm it comes up cleanly. **PostgreSQL note:**
   the same automatic-migration mechanism applies to `PostgresWaypointStore`/
   `PostgresRunRepository`/etc.; no separate manual migration step is needed there either.
3. **Update config — nothing is required.** Every new v0.10 config surface defaults to today's
   behavior, proven by the `v0_9_config_boot` integration test (§9.5): a v0.9 configuration file
   boots this binary with every new subsystem inert. No `config.yml` edit is required to preserve
   v0.9 behavior; add a section only when actually adopting a new capability.
   **Phase 40 note (TENANT-01, the one exception — see §9.5):** a deployment that configures
   `http.auth.api_keys` must add a `tenant:` line to EVERY entry, and a deployment with
   `http.auth.bearer_token.enabled: true` must also set `http.auth.bearer_token.tenant`;
   `paladin-server` refuses to boot otherwise, naming the key's `name`, never its value. Key names
   and key values must also be unique. After this upgrade a run is visible only to principals of
   the tenant that submitted it (another tenant's run answers `404` on every `/runs/{run_id}*`
   route, §9.6), so any operator tooling that reads other tenants' runs over HTTP needs an
   `admin`-role key. A deployment with no `api_keys` and bearer disabled is unaffected.
4. **Raise `terminationGracePeriodSeconds`.** Set it to at least `60` — twice the default
   `APP_ENGINE_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_SECS` of 30s, or at least twice whatever value you configure that env
   var to — in every Deployment manifest before rolling out this upgrade (M-B-02, §9.1). The
   shipped manifests (`k8s/deployment.yaml`, `k8s/server/deployment.yaml`,
   `k8s/server/worker-deployment.yaml`) already carry `terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 60`; an
   operator running a forked or hand-written manifest must make the same change there.
5. **Register a custom evaluator for every `EdgeCondition::Custom` name in use.** M-B-01's fix
   (§9.1) makes an unregistered custom edge condition a validation failure, not a silent
   always-true. Register one via `CampaignExecutionService::with_evaluator("name",
   Arc::new(evaluator))` on the legacy execution path, or `WarEngine::with_edge_evaluator("name",
   Arc::new(evaluator))` on the `WarEngine` path, before calling `execute`/`start` — M-B-01's
   worked example above shows the exact registration call and the validation error text this step
   avoids.
6. **Deploy.** Roll out the new binary and manifests with the grace period and evaluator
   registrations from steps 4-5 already in place.
7. **Verify.** Run `paladin-cli setup-check --verbose` for an environment/toolchain/provider/
   service connectivity check; if the deployment uses the Maneuver flow DSL, run `paladin-cli
   maneuver validate` against its flow configuration; and run `paladin-cli eval run <glob>`
   against a representative scenario glob for a behavioral post-deploy check. `GraphCommands`
   exposes exactly one subcommand, `export` (`paladin-cli graph export --format mermaid|dot`), for
   inspecting a graph's structure — there is no separate command for a runtime probe.

### Library consumers: v0.10.1 → v0.11.0

This subsection is for a library consumer that depends on the facade `paladin-ai` (plus
`paladin-ports` / `paladin-memory`) by path or submodule and moves from the `v0.10.1` tag to
`v0.11.0`. The operator checklist above is separate and does not apply to a consumer that only
links the libraries. Every item below already has its entry in section 9.2 (and, where relevant,
section 9.1); this subsection is a consumer-shaped index over those entries, not a new register
row.

**No total-only `TokenUsage` constructor (applies at `v0.10.1` already).** `TokenUsage::from_total`
was removed outright in Phase 31 (the `TokenUsage` row of section 9.2), and nothing replaced it. The
shape `TokenUsage { total_tokens: n, ..Default::default() }` compiles, but it has two defects. It
violates the documented invariants (`total_tokens == prompt_tokens + completion_tokens`; cache read
and cache write each at most `prompt_tokens`; reasoning at most `completion_tokens`). And it is not
additive: `impl Add for TokenUsage`, and `AddAssign` and `Sum`, which go through it, recompute
`total_tokens` from prompt plus completion with saturating addition, so such a value contributes 0
to any `+`, `+=` or `.sum()`. The lossless route is to carry the provider's full per-call
`TokenUsage` and accumulate it with `usage += response.usage.clone()` or `.sum::<TokenUsage>()`
(both saturating, both present at `v0.10.1`). Build values with `TokenUsage::new(prompt,
completion)` plus the chainable `with_cache_read` / `with_cache_write` / `with_reasoning`, or with
`..Default::default()` for the three optional fields, where `None` means the provider did not
report the figure.

**Starting before `v0.10.0`.** A consumer whose pin predates `v0.10.0` also applies these section 9.2
rows, each cited by type and phase:
section 9.2, the `TokenUsage` row (Phase 31: three new `Option` fields; `from_total` removed), the
`PaladinResult` row (Phase 31: `token_count` replaced by `usage`) and the `StreamingResponse` row
(Phase 31: `#[non_exhaustive]`, with `StreamingResponse::delta` / `terminal` / `with_usage`); section 9.2,
the `Commissary` row (Phase 32: `new` and `from_port` no longer take `is_exact_counter`), and the
`TokenCounter` and `TokenCounterFactory` rows (Phase 32: removed from `paladin-memory`); section 9.2,
the `RagRetrievalService` row (Phase 33: `retrieve_context` and `retrieve_context_with_timeout` change
their return type).

| # | Item | What changed | Replacement | Register |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DR-1 | Battalion error-strategy enum (`battalion::ErrorStrategy`) | removed outright, with no alias | `aegis.on_error` on `BattalionConfig`: `Some(ErrorHandlerSpec::Absorb { .. })` continues past a failed Paladin and records it in `node_errors`, `None` fails fast; honoured by Formation and Phalanx, ignored by Campaign. Import `paladin::core::platform::container::aegis::{Aegis, ErrorHandlerSpec, TimeoutPolicy}` and `paladin::core::platform::container::battlefield::StateDelta` (also in `paladin::prelude`) | 9.2 `ErrorStrategy` row (Phase 44); 9.1 M-B-07, M-B-11 |

A tuple pattern or constructor on `EmptyCompletion`, and any literal or field-complete pattern on either variant, no longer compiles | build with `LlmError::empty_completion(detail)` / `LlmError::refusal(provider, model, detail)` (plus `with_billed_usage(BilledUsage::new(model, usage))`); match with `LlmError::EmptyCompletion { .. }` or `EmptyCompletion { detail, .. }`, and `LlmError::Refusal { .. }`; read the bill with `error.billed_usage()` (`model`, `usage`, `cost`; `None` when the vendor reported no usage, and it looks through `AllProvidersFailed` to its last error). `Display` and `Permanent` are unchanged | 9.2 `LlmError` row (extended, Phase 50 plan 50-26) |
| DR-18 | Reading a failed execution's spend: `paladin_ports::output::spend_scope::{SpendScope, ATTEMPT_SPEND, current_attempt_spend}`, `HOPPED_BILLS`, `paladin_core::platform::container::cost::IncurredSpend` (Phase 50 plans 50-29 and 50-30, G-50-5a) | additive, no compile break. A failed or timed-out execution returns a `PaladinError` that carries no usage or cost, and a successful one's `PaladinResult.usage` / `cost` now include any hopped-past fallback bill. `PaladinExecutionService` records every call it folds (a success, a billed failure, each structured repair call, a hop bill) into the `ATTEMPT_SPEND` task-local scope when one is installed; the Battalion engine installs one per attempt. It installs its own `HOPPED_BILLS` collector around every model call, so a consumer never installs that one around the service; `FallbackLlmAdapter` fills it and `PricingLlmAdapter` prices it. A hop bill is priced and counted into `ATTEMPT_SPEND` the moment the fallback chain records it: `PricingLlmAdapter` installs its pricer on the call's collector with `HoppedBills::install_pricer` before it delegates, so an attempt the engine's deadline or idle bound drops while a later hop of that call is still in flight is charged for every earlier billed hop (plan 50-32, review WR-03). The service takes each collector with `HoppedBills::count_and_take` and settles every hop bill in the call's ledger row without counting it again | to read what a failed (or any) execution spent, wrap the call: `let scope = SpendScope::new(); let outcome = ATTEMPT_SPEND.scope(scope.clone(), service.execute(&paladin, input)).await; let spent = scope.snapshot();` then read `spent.usage()`, `spent.cost()` and `spent.calls()`. For a streamed call (`StreamingExecutorPort::execute_stream`), install the scope around the code that opens and drains the stream; the service carries it into its forwarding task. A direct `LlmPort` caller reads a failed call's bill from `error.billed_usage()`; one that drives a `FallbackLlmAdapter` with `hop_on_refusal(true)` itself and wants the hopped-past bills installs `HOPPED_BILLS.scope(HoppedBills::new(), ..)` and calls `take()` afterwards (with no collector, each hop bill is warned once per (provider, model) under `paladin::usage`). A direct caller that installs both `ATTEMPT_SPEND` and `HOPPED_BILLS` around its own chain already has the hop bills in its scope, so it must not record the taken bills into that scope again (`count_and_take` counts only entries that were not counted). An out-of-tree pricing decorator calls `install_pricer` before it delegates, because pricing an entry later with `price_unpriced` reaches the ledger row but not the attempt scope | none (additive); 9.1 M-B-21 |
| DR-19 | The `*Config::new(..)` constructors (Phase 50.1, SURFACE-02) | `new(api_key, base_url, model)` becomes `new(api_key, model)` on `DeepSeekConfig`, `GeminiConfig`, `QwenConfig`, `GrokConfig` and `KimiConfig`; `OpenAIConfig::new(api_key)` becomes `OpenAIConfig::new(api_key, model)`; and `AnthropicConfig::new(api_key, base_url, model, max_tokens)` becomes `AnthropicConfig::new(api_key, model)`, whose `max_tokens` is 4,096 for every model (the same as `from_env()` and the layered path; the model's larger seeded maximum is reachable only through `with_max_tokens`). Seven types change; `OllamaConfig::new(base_url, model)` and `OpenAiCompatibleConfig::new(api_key, base_url, model, capabilities)` are the two endpoint-only exceptions and do not | build with `new(api_key, model)`; the endpoint is set with `with_base_url`, the timeout with `with_timeout_seconds`, the other knobs with the existing builders (an `AnthropicConfig` that relied on a specific fourth argument adds `.with_max_tokens(n)`); `OllamaConfig::new(base_url, model)` keeps the endpoint as its first argument (it is not a key) and `OpenAiCompatibleConfig::new(api_key, base_url, model, capabilities)` is unchanged (D-08) | 9.2 `paladin-llm \| DeepSeekConfig` through `paladin-llm \| AnthropicConfig` SURFACE-02 rows |
| DR-20 | An explicit reasoning effort on a model nobody measured (Phase 51, REASON-02) | an explicit `LlmRequest.reasoning`, `PaladinBuilder::reasoning_effort` or `reasoning_effort` default is sent exactly or refused before HTTP: `LlmError::UnsupportedParameter` (`Permanent`, no retry) naming the provider, model, effort and reason, unless the model's seeded row (measured live on 2026-10-22) or an operator override lists the level. A model with no row, or an unmeasured one (`gpt-4o-mini`, `chat-latest`), refuses every explicit effort; so does a level the vendor coerces (`xhigh` on `grok-4.5`) | pick a level the model lists (the Reasoning Effort page's per-model table), set `reasoning_fallback` to `nearest_lower` / `nearest_higher` to accept a neighbour, or prove the model and add `llm.<provider>.models.<model-id>.effort`. A request that sets no effort is unchanged | 9.2 `LlmError`, `ModelProfile`, `ModelOverride` Phase 51 rows; 9.5 Reasoning effort keys |
| DR-21 | A provider-level default effort on a model that cannot honour it (Phase 51, REASON-04) | `llm.<provider>.reasoning_effort` (or `with_reasoning_effort`) is judged against each agent's own model by `PaladinBuilder::build` -- for any port, not only the agent host -- through `LlmPort::check_reasoning`: an agent whose model cannot honour the default (and sets no effort of its own) is refused at build with an error naming the key (`llm.<provider>.reasoning_effort`, or ``(set by `agents[<id>].reasoning_effort`)`` for an agent's own effort), so a Paladin built over a directly built adapter whose default its model cannot honour is refused at build rather than at the first call. A provider-level `reasoning_fallback` is honoured at build, at agent load and at pre-flight, always beneath the agent's own fallback. A fallback chain is pre-flighted on every hop before the first call, each hop against its own step model and its own provider-level defaults, even for a request carrying no effort, so a chain with a hop that cannot honour the effort fails whole with `fallback hop <index>` and no hop is called; `FallbackLlmAdapter::model_profile` reports effort `Unmeasured` when any hop has no profile. A refusal under an in-force `Refuse` fallback on an unlisted level ends with the reasoning-fallback hint at send time too. A decorator over an `LlmPort` should forward `check_reasoning` | remove the default, set one the model lists, give the model an `effort` override, set `reasoning_fallback`, or set the effort per agent. A deployment that sets no effort and no provider default is unaffected; a decorator author forwards `check_reasoning` as `model_profile` | 9.5 Reasoning effort keys |
| DR-22 | `CompatRequestParameters` (Phase 51, REASON-02) | gains the public field `reasoning: ReasoningWire` and `all()` becomes `all(reasoning)`; the type deliberately has no `Default`, so a custom Kimi, Qwen, Grok, Ollama or OpenAI-compatible preset must state where its vendor parses an effort | add `reasoning: ReasoningWire::Unsupported` to the literal, or call `CompatRequestParameters::all(ReasoningWire::Unsupported)`, which keeps today's behaviour | 9.2 `paladin-llm \| CompatRequestParameters` row; measured `constructible_struct_adds_field` and `method_parameter_count_changed` |
| DR-23 | `PaladinData` and `AgentDefinition` (Phase 51, REASON-01) | each gains the fields `reasoning_effort: Option<ReasoningEffort>` and `reasoning_fallback: Option<ReasoningFallback>`; both structs stay constructible and are not `#[non_exhaustive]`, so a full struct literal breaks. Stored snapshots and YAML load unchanged (`#[serde(default)]`) | name both fields in the literal (`reasoning_effort: None, reasoning_fallback: None`) or spread `..PaladinData::default()` / `..` from an existing definition | 9.2 `paladin-ai-core \| PaladinData` and `paladin-ai \| AgentDefinition` rows; measured `constructible_struct_adds_field` |
| DR-24 | `TraceEvent::NodeFinished` (Phase 51, REASON-05) | gains `reasoning_requested: Option<String>` and `reasoning_effective: Option<String>`, the effort one Paladin or structured-output attempt asked for and the effort it ran with (they differ when a `reasoning_fallback` substituted a neighbour); `None` for every other node kind, a cache hit, a failed attempt and a run with no effort in force, and not serialised when `None`. A constructing struct-variant literal and an exhaustive destructure break | name both as `None` in the literal; match with `..`. Stored `run_traces` rows and SSE frames load unchanged | 9.2 `paladin-ai-core \| TraceEvent` row; measured `enum_struct_variant_field_added` |
| DR-25 | Node-cache keys of Paladin nodes (Phase 51, REASON-01) | the Paladin configuration fingerprint appends the agent's `reasoning_effort` and `reasoning_fallback` only when they are set, so the same agent at two efforts never shares a cached answer. An agent that sets neither composes the same key as before, and `NODE_CACHE_KEY_VERSION` stays `k1`. A provider-level default (`llm.<provider>.reasoning_effort`) is not on the agent and is not part of the key | none for an agent that sets no effort. Changing a provider-level default needs a new cache namespace or a version bump if cached answers must not be reused | 9.5 Reasoning effort keys |
| DR-26 | A consumer that sets no reasoning effort (Phase 51) | nothing: no request field is added to any of the nine wire bodies, no response is stamped with the two metadata keys, and no refusal can occur, because every Phase 51 path begins with an explicit effort. The compile-time breaks are DR-22, DR-23 and DR-24 | none | 9.2 Phase 51 rows |

**Phase 50 behaviour changes (no compile break; each has its section 9.1 row).** A consumer that
swaps providers or models meets these at run time, not at the compiler:

- An **explicit temperature on an Anthropic agent is now refused at build**, naming the provider, the
  model and the parameter, because the current Anthropic model takes no temperature (M-B-13,
  ADR-0061); drop `temperature` from those agents. An auto-selected or default value is omitted and
  recorded instead (`paladin.params_omitted`).
- **Empty text on any finish reason is `LlmError::EmptyCompletion`**, buffered and streamed, on all
  nine providers (M-B-16, M-B-17); it used to be an empty `Ok` on several.
- **A refusal is `LlmError::Refusal { provider, model, detail, .. }`** (`Permanent`; a fallback chain
  stops on it unless built with `hop_on_refusal(true)`): match it (`LlmError::Refusal { .. }`; the
  variant is `#[non_exhaustive]` from plan 50-26, see DR-17).
- **Billed failures and hopped-past fallback bills are charged** (M-B-21): settled spend and
  allowance consumption rise to what the vendors bill, a failed or timed-out Battalion attempt
  counts what it spent, and `PaladinResult.usage` / `cost` include any hopped-past bill. Read a
  failed execution's spend through an `ATTEMPT_SPEND` scope (DR-18).
- On the five compat presets an empty or refused body **without a `usage` object is now
  `EmptyCompletion` / `Refusal`**, not `ProcessingError`, and DeepSeek's empty-completion message
  no longer carries token counts (M-B-22).
  Gemini's `RECITATION`, `BLOCKLIST`, `PROHIBITED_CONTENT` and `SPII` finishes are now
  `ContentFilter`, no longer `Error(<value>)` (M-B-16, M-B-17).
- A **direct `*Config` constructor defaults a seeded reasoning model to a 300 s timeout**, exactly as
  `create_from_config` does; a model marked reasoning only through `model_overrides` needs
  `timeout_seconds` set on the direct path (9.5, `llm.<provider>` block).
- An **adapter-wide `context_window` now beats seeded windows**, and a per-model override beats both
  (M-B-14; 9.5). Default models and windows come from a dated registry (M-B-14).
- **OpenAI no longer sends a fabricated `temperature` or `top_p`**; the vendor default applies
  unless the caller set one (M-B-13).
- **`llm.<provider>` blocks take effect** and `default_temperature` is judged against the model's
  profile (M-B-15).
- The run-metadata keys of DR-15 are visible per call, per run and on a stream's terminal chunk
  (owner reading R-1, ADR-0061 (h)).

**Direct-adapter consumers (no compile break).** A consumer that builds an adapter from a `*Config`
itself, instead of going through the server's composition root, meets four more run-time changes:

- **A directly built adapter has no 429 pacing unless Cadence is composed (M-B-05).** Since Phase 43 no
  adapter retries a 429 inside its own loop: each surfaces it on the first answer, because the Cadence
  decorator owns the waiting. A consumer that builds an adapter from a `*Config` therefore gets no pacing
  at all unless it composes Cadence; with no Cadence composed, a 429 is returned to the caller as
  `LlmError::RateLimitExceeded` (transient) and the next call goes straight back into the limit. Recipe:
  `let wiring = paladin::infrastructure::cadence::build_cadence(&CadenceConfig::default())?;` then
  `let llm = paladin::infrastructure::cadence::with_cadence(llm, wiring.as_ref());` (build the wiring
  once per process and wrap every adapter with it), or
  `compose_llm(llm, &prices, wiring.as_ref())` when the consumer also prices. A quota or spend-cap 429 is
  a different error: `LlmError::UsageLimitExceeded { .. }` is permanent and is never paced and never
  retried, where `RateLimitExceeded` is transient and is paced by Cadence. It is mapped for OpenAI
  (`insufficient_quota`) and Anthropic (`enforced_spend_limit_reached`). DeepSeek maps its documented
  insufficient-balance status, an HTTP 402, to `UsageLimitExceeded`, not a 429; Gemini maps a
  `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` answer to `RateLimitExceeded`, so a Gemini spend cap is paced as a rate limit.
  Kimi, qwen, grok, ollama and openai-compatible keep their existing error mapping and are unverified
  (owner reading R-2, ADR-0061: no quota-exhaustion body was captured for them); see
  `docs/src/user-guides/switching-providers.md`.
- **An `LlmPort` decorator must forward `model_profile`.** A consumer's own wrapper that does not forward
  `LlmPort::model_profile` returns the default `None`, so every model it wraps runs unprofiled for the
  layers above it. The in-tree decorators (`PricingLlmAdapter`, `CadenceLlmAdapter`,
  `FallbackLlmAdapter`) forward it.
- **The adapter-wide context window follows the seeded default model.** With `context_window` unset,
  `get_capabilities().max_context_tokens` is the seeded window of the provider's default-model constant,
  not of the configured model: Anthropic 200,000 at `v0.10.1`, now 1,000,000 (`claude-haiku-5-5`);
  DeepSeek 64,000, now 1,048,576 (`deepseek-flash`); OpenAI unchanged at 128,000. So
  `Commissary::from_port` rations 5x more prompt on Anthropic (1,000,000 / 200,000) and about 16x more on
  DeepSeek (1,048,576 / 64,000 = 16.384). Two remedies: set the window with `with_context_window(n)` on
  the config, or ration per model with `Commissary::for_model(&port, model, counter, plan)`.
- **A base URL must start with `http://` or `https://`.** On OpenAI, DeepSeek, Kimi, Qwen, Grok,
  Ollama and OpenAI-compatible, a `base_url` that only began with `http` (`httpx://host`,
  `http-gateway.internal/v1`) used to pass `validate()` and fail later at the HTTP layer; it is now
  refused by `Adapter::new` with `"Base URL must start with http:// or https://"`, as Anthropic and
  Gemini already did. Remedy: give the full scheme. Code that matched the old error text
  (`"... with http or https"`) must match the new one.
- **Models seeded in Phase 50.1.** The models listed on the Switching providers page with the
  `verified_on` date 2026-10-20 (`docs/src/user-guides/switching-providers.md`; the per-model list is
  there, not repeated here) now resolve to a seeded profile instead of the adapter-wide fallback. That
  covers `claude-opus-4-8` and `deepseek-v4-pro` (the two models web3sec runs), the other measured
  Anthropic, Gemini and Qwen ids, 17 OpenAI ids, and `grok-4.20-reasoning`, `grok-4.20-non-reasoning`
  and `grok-4.7`. A seeded row matches its id exactly, so an alias or dated id of the same model (for
  example `grok-4.20-0309-reasoning`) still runs on the fallback. Six things change for a model that
  gains a row:
  1. **An explicit parameter the profile does not admit is now refused at build**, naming provider,
     model and parameter (ADR-0061), where the fallback used to send it. Examples: a `temperature`
     other than 1.0, any `top_p` and any `stop` on `gpt-6.1-sol`, `gpt-5.5` or `o3` (measured "Only
     the default (1) value is supported"); any `stop` on any of the 17 new OpenAI ids; a
     `max_tokens` above the seeded maximum output (8,192 on `qwen-max`, 100,000 on `o3`, 32,768 on
     `qwen-flash`); a `temperature` of 2.0 on `grok-4.7` (its 2.00 probe failed at the vendor, so the
     seeded range stops at 1.99). Drop the parameter or change it to a value the page lists.
  2. **`paladin.unprofiled_model` is no longer stamped** for these models.
  3. **Anthropic and DeepSeek `validate_model` and `get_available_models` now include them**, because
     both return their seeded table. The other providers' lists are unchanged.
  4. **A direct `new` defaults a seeded reasoning model to 300 s** (the other models keep the
     provider's own default). The reasoning rows are the ones whose default call returned reasoning
     text or whose listing says so: `claude-fable-5`, `claude-opus-5`, `deepseek-v4-pro`, the nine
     new Gemini ids, eight of the new Qwen ids, `grok-4.20-reasoning` and `grok-4.7`. No new OpenAI
     row is marked reasoning, because Chat Completions returns no reasoning text.
  5. **`AnthropicConfig::new`'s `max_tokens` for these models is 4,096**, the compiled default, for
     every model including the new ones (owner decision `compiled-default` in plan 50.1-06, 2026-10-20).
     A model's larger seeded maximum output (128,000 for the Claude ids) is reached only by
     `with_max_tokens`.
  6. **`Commissary::for_model` rations against the seeded window**: for example 1,050,000 on
     `gpt-5.4` and `gpt-6-sol` against the OpenAI fallback's 128,000, but 32,768 on `qwen-max`
     against the Qwen fallback's 1,000,000, so a prompt that used to be admitted for a model with a
     smaller seeded window can now be rationed or refused.
- **A small output cap can come back empty on a model that reasons before it answers.** The probe
  measured it (`50.1-SEED-PROBE.md`, the `### Probes:` tables, finish reason and content columns):
  `gemini-3.5-flash`, `gemini-3.6-flash`, `gemini-3.7-flash` and `gemini-flash-latest` answered
  `max_tokens=64` with finish `MAX_TOKENS` and empty content; `gpt-5` and `gpt-5-nano` answered
  `max_completion_tokens=64` with finish `length` and empty content, and so did their `json_schema`
  probe at the baseline 256-token cap. Hidden reasoning spends the budget before any answer, so give
  such a model a generous output cap. Paladin changes no behaviour for it: an empty completion
  surfaces as `LlmError::EmptyCompletion`, as for any model.

**Insulating the next bump (consumer-side; no Paladin change).** Prefer
`PaladinResult { .., ..Default::default() }` or `PaladinResult::new(..)` over a full struct literal;
both exist at `v0.10.1`, and neither names a field a later phase may add. `LlmResponse` has no
`Default`, but since Phase 50 it has a constructor, `LlmResponse::new(request_id, model, content,
finish_reason, usage)`, that sets the five fields every mock knows and leaves the rest neutral
(`cost: None`, `reasoning: None`, empty `metadata`, `function_call: None`); chain
`with_reasoning(text)` for the one a test sets. DR-6 is the second break this paragraph warned
about: a consumer that routes mock responses through one consumer-owned test helper (for example
`fn mock_response(usage: TokenUsage) -> LlmResponse`, now built with `LlmResponse::new`) edits one
line for `reasoning`, and the next added field is then free for every caller of the constructor
instead of costing one edit per literal. The same holds for DR-15: a `PaladinResult` built as
`PaladinResult { .., ..Default::default() }` takes `run_metadata` for free, and `LlmResponse::new(..)`
plus `with_reasoning(..)` takes any field a later phase adds to `LlmResponse`.

`tests/consumer_surface_muster.rs` is the executable form of this list (`make
check-consumer-surface` runs it), including the Phase 50 surfaces the consumer adopts at v0.11.0:
`LlmProviderFactory::create_from_config`, `LlmConfig`, `LlmProviderConfig`, `ModelOverride`,
`ModelProfile`, a `LlmError::Refusal { .. }` match arm, `FallbackStep` and
`FallbackLlmAdapter::from_steps`, `LlmResponse::new` and the `AnthropicConfig` knob builders. Phase
50.1 (D-09, D-10, D-11) adds the surfaces a downstream consumer builds and matches, each pinned
through `paladin::` paths alone: a Battalion configured with `with_aegis` (the `Aegis` family) and
a result's `node_errors` read back; `DeepSeekConfig::new` and `AnthropicConfig::new` in the
SURFACE-02 shape (`fn(String, String) -> ..Config`) with `with_timeout_seconds` and
`with_base_url` in the builder chain and no field assignment; `LlmError::rate_limited`, the
`RateLimitExceeded { .. }`, `UsageLimitExceeded { provider, regain_hint }` and `ProcessingError(..)`
arms; `Commissary::for_model`, `dispense` and `verify_fits` with the `Stockpile` fields;
`MockLlmAdapter::with_response`, `with_responses`, `with_error` and `with_delay`;
`PaladinBuilder::new(..).name(..).system_prompt(..).build().await`; `MCPClient::connect_streamable_http`
and `invoke_tool`; `PromptItem::parameters` and `set_parameters`; the four-field `Armament` literal.
`FinishReason` and `ArsenalError` stay exhaustively matchable on purpose (neither is
`#[non_exhaustive]`): the muster matches both without a wildcard arm, so a new variant on either
is a registered break, with a section 9.2 row in the same commit, and not a silent addition (D-11).
The muster runs in CI as the `consumer-surface` job (`Consumer-Surface Muster`), which executes
`make check-consumer-surface` and fails if it ran no test (D-10). A red muster means the next
bump needs a row here and its section 9.2 row in the same commit.
